# Prediction Exchange - CONTRACTS

**Status: authoritative.** This document is the single source of truth for the
implementation. The PRD (`PRD_Prediction_Exchange v2.md`, French) defines *what*
the product does; this document defines *how the code is shaped* so that ~25
agents can build it in parallel without composing badly.

If this document and the PRD disagree on a functional point, the PRD wins and
this document must be amended in the same commit. If this document and the code
disagree, this document wins and the code is a bug.

Rules for every contributor:

1. **Never edit a file you do not own.** File ownership is table 1. If you need a
   change in someone else's file, it is a contract change: amend this document
   first.
2. **Never widen a public signature silently.** The signatures in section 7 are
   literal. Add keyword-only arguments with defaults if you must extend.
3. **English only** in code, comments, docstrings and documents.
4. **Never use the em-dash character** in any produced content. Use `-` or
   parentheses.
5. Async functions are prefixed with `a` (`acollect_actions`, `acall_agent`,
   `apropose_patch`), never suffixed with `_async`. Only `pxe.gateway` and
   `pxe.evolve.apropose_patch` are allowed to be async; there is deliberately
   no `arun_match`, because the runner is synchronous (FR-5.1.2).

---

## 0. Table of contents

1. Package tree and file ownership
2. Cross-cutting conventions
3. Determinism contract
4. Event sourcing contract
5. Tick phase contract (P1..P4)
6. Accounting invariants
7. Module map and public APIs
8. Agent interface contracts (observation and action)
9. Metric definitions
10. Test layout, markers and golden fixtures
11. Traceability (every FR, every AC, every O, every WBS task: no blank cell)
12. Decisions a reviewer should challenge, the register of PRD overrides
    (section 12.0) and the wave-1 integration rulings (section 12.2)
13. Implementation work packages (the dispatch table: who owns which files)

## 1. Package tree and file ownership

Every file has exactly one owning workstream. Two workstreams never write the
same file. Directory `__init__.py` files are owned by the workstream that owns
the directory.

**Shared packages: `__init__.py` is a docstring and nothing else.** Three
directories hold files owned by more than one workstream
(`runner/` is A09 plus A10 and A11, `tournament/` is A19 plus A20 and A23,
`gateway/` is A13 plus A14). In those packages, and as a uniform rule in every
sub-package of `pxe`, `__init__.py` contains a module docstring and **no import
and no re-export**. The single exception is the top level `src/pxe/__init__.py`,
which carries the docstring plus `__version__` and the three version constants,
and nothing else. Submodules always import by full path
(`from pxe.runner.state import MatchState`), never from the package
(`from pxe.runner import MatchState`). This is what stops A10 from needing to
edit A09's file to publish a name, and it is also what stops
`runner/__init__.py` re-exporting `match_runner` from creating an import cycle
with `observation_builder`, which imports `runner.state`.

Two files in this repository have **no owner because they are inputs, not
produced content**: `PRD_Prediction_Exchange v2.md` (the signed off French
specification, quoted and never rewritten, see section 12.0) and
`docs/reviews/*.md` (the frozen adversarial reviews this document answers).
Both are exempt from the em-dash scan of section 10 for that reason, and
neither may be edited by any workstream.

**The UI directory is `web/`, not `ui/`.** It is named after the PRD module
(`web-ui`) and it is what `.gitignore`, `pyproject.toml`'s `extend-exclude` and
the CI `web` job all reference. An earlier draft of this document said `ui/`;
every occurrence has been corrected and A24 owns exactly one directory.

Workstream ownership summary:

## 2. Cross-cutting conventions

### 2.1 Units. No floats for money, ever.

| Concept | Type | Unit | Suffix | Range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Money | `int` | cents | `_cents` | `>= 0` for cash, signed for deltas |
| Price | `int` | cents | none (`price`, `ref_price`) | `1..99`, tick 1 |
| Quantity | `int` | contracts | `_qty` or `qty` | `>= 1` for an order, signed for a position |
| Probability | `int` | parts per million | `_ppm` | `0..1_000_000` |
| Latent / signal value | `int` | thousandths | `_milli` | signed |
| Fee rate | `int` | basis points | `_bps` | `0..200` |
| Ratio / descriptor | `int` | parts per million | `_ppm` | `0..1_000_000` |
| Duration in ticks | `int` | ticks | `_tick`, `_ticks` | `>= 0` |

A YES contract pays exactly `100` cents, a NO contract `0`. Floats exist in
exactly two places: the agent boundary (`p_yes` in an action, `p_yes` and
signal values in an observation) and statistical helpers in
`metrics`/`ratings`/`integrity` that never write into a journal. Conversions go
through `pxe.types.ppm_from_prob` / `prob_from_ppm` / `milli_from_float`.

**Rounding.** `round()` is banker's rounding and is banned everywhere,
including inside the anchor modules. Use `pxe.types.round_half_up`. Fees use
floor division so they never create money.

**Signed ratios.** `//` on a signed numerator rounds toward minus infinity,
which overstates every loss by up to one unit and understates every gain. Any
`*_bps` or `*_ppm` ratio that can be negative (`pnl_pct_bps`,
`max_drawdown_bps`, any PnL derived descriptor) is computed with
`pxe.types.bps_ratio(numerator_cents, denominator_cents)`, which rounds half
away from zero so a gain and the mirror loss report the same magnitude. Plain
`//` on a signed value is a bug, not a style choice.

**PRD reading:** the PRD's "Cash initial 10 000" is read as 10 000 contract
units, therefore `initial_cash_cents = 1_000_000`. See section 12.

### 2.2 Identifier formats

| Entity | Format | Regex | Assigned by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Market | `M1`..`M8` | `^M[1-9][0-9]?$` | `world-gen`, 1 based index |
| Agent seat | `A1`..`A8` | `^A[1-9][0-9]?$` | orchestrator, 1 based seat |
| Market maker account | `MM` | literal | reserved |
| Fee vault account | `FEES` | literal | reserved |
| Order | `o-000001` | `^o-[0-9]{6}$` | exchange, per match counter from 1 |
| Trade | `t-000001` | `^t-[0-9]{6}$` | exchange, per match counter from 1 |
| News | `n-<tick:03d>-<idx:02d>` | | info-engine |
| Signal | `s-<tick:03d>-<agent>-<idx:02d>` | | info-engine |
| Incident | `i-0001` | `^i-[0-9]{4}$` | integrity |
| Match | `m-<template>-<seed>-<run:02d>` | `^m-[a-z0-9_]+-[0-9]{1,20}-[0-9]{2}$` | orchestrator |
| Tournament | `T-<name>-<nnnn>` | at most 24 chars, no `#` | orchestrator, caller supplied |
| Match task | `<tournament_id>#r<rr>-g<ggg>-<template_id>-<seed>` | `^[^#]{1,24}#.+$` | orchestrator |
| Harness key | `<harness_id>@<version>+<config_hash[:8]>` | `^[a-z0-9_.-]+@[^+]+\+[0-9a-f]{8}$` | `pxe.types.harness_key` |

No id is ever derived from a clock, a UUID or a memory address. Factories live
in `pxe.types` (`make_order_id`, `make_trade_id`, ...).

**`#` is reserved in a tournament id, and the task id prefix is a contract.**
`MatchTask` carries no `tournament_id` field while `Store.pending_tasks(tournament_id)`
is scoped by one, so the store reads the tournament off the task id prefix.
`TournamentOrchestrator.__init__` therefore raises `InvalidConfigError` on a
tournament id holding `#` and on one longer than 24 characters, which is what
keeps the whole task id inside the store's `String(64)` column. A task id with no
`#` belongs to the empty tournament. See ruling R109's neighbour in section 12.3.

**Agent and account are two concepts and therefore two regexes.**
`pxe.types.RE_AGENT_ID` is `^A[1-9][0-9]?$` (ranked seats only) and
`pxe.types.RE_ACCOUNT_ID` is `^(A[1-9][0-9]?|MM|FEES)$` (seats plus the two
reserved accounts). `schemas/observation.v1.json` `$defs.agentId` mirrors
**`RE_AGENT_ID` exactly**, because `MM` and `FEES` never receive an
observation and never post a message; there is deliberately no `accountId`
definition in that schema, since nothing in an observation is an account id.
`tests/test_schemas.py::test_agent_id_pattern_is_the_ranked_agent_regex`
compares the two strings, so the drift that made the schema say
`^(A[1-9][0-9]?|MM)$` while the code said something else cannot come back.

**The harness key and the harness hash have exactly one producer**, and it is
not the gateway: `pxe.types.harness_key(harness)` and
`pxe.types.harness_config_hash(harness)` (section 7.1). `HarnessConfig.key` is
a thin property over the first, and `HarnessConfig.__post_init__` fills
`config_hash` with the second when it is left empty, so `config_hash` is never
the empty string in a journal. Four workstreams consume the key
(`MatchTask.harness_keys`, `RatingRecord.harness_key`, `EliteCell.harness_key`,
`HallOfFame.freeze`, `collect_failures(harness_key=...)`); none of them builds
it. The `+<config_hash[:8]>` suffix is deliberate: without it two different
system prompts shipped under the same `version` tag share one TrueSkill
rating, one MAP-Elites cell and one Hall of Fame slot, and T3.4's "frozen
versions replayable" becomes unverifiable. See decision 24.

### 2.3 Canonical ordering

Any time a collection is iterated in a way that can influence output, it must be
in one of these canonical orders:

* **Markets**: ascending numeric suffix (`M1 < M2 < ... < M10`). Use
  `pxe.types.sorted_ids`.
* **Ranked agents**: ascending numeric suffix (`A1 < A2 < ... < A10`). Use
  `pxe.types.sorted_ids`.
* **Accounts**: see the normative paragraph below. Use
  `pxe.types.sorted_account_ids`.
* **Resting orders inside a book side**: `Order.priority_key`, that is best
  price first, then lowest `seq` first (FR-5.4.1).
* **An agent's own order intents**: exactly the order submitted by the agent.
* **Events**: `seq` ascending.

Forbidden everywhere in the engine: iterating a `set`, a `dict` built from a
`set`, or relying on `dict` insertion order to derive output. A `set` may only be
used for membership tests and must never be iterated.

#### Every `Mapping` argument is iterated through a sort helper (normative)

A public signature that takes a `Mapping[str, X]` and produces output from it
**must** iterate `sorted_ids(mapping.keys())` (market ids, ranked agent ids) or
`sorted_account_ids(mapping.keys())` (account ids), never `mapping.items()`,
never `for k in mapping`. A `dict` a caller built in a different order is a
different journal, and nothing in the type system catches it. The mappings this
rule applies to, exhaustively, are:

| Signature | Helper |
|---|---|
| `InfoEngine(*, profiles: Mapping[str, InfoProfile], ...)` (7.6) | `sorted_ids` |
| `ScriptedGateway(*, agents: Mapping[str, ScriptedAgent])` (7.16) | `sorted_ids` |
| `ClaudeCliGateway(*, harnesses: Mapping[str, HarnessConfig], ...)` (7.16) | `sorted_ids` |
| `AccountBook.equity_cents(..., ref_prices: Mapping[str, int])` (7.9) | `sorted_ids` |
| `AccountBook.check_invariants(*, ref_prices, book_view)` (7.9) | `sorted_ids` |
| `Exchange.book_view() -> Mapping[str, tuple[Order, ...]]` (7.8) | `sorted_ids` at every consumer |
| `RatingService.update(..., harness_of: Mapping[str, str])` (7.19) | `sorted_ids` |
| `resolve_predictions(*, declared, previous, open_market_ids)` (7.14) | `sorted_ids(open_market_ids)` |
| `MatchState.last_prediction_ppm`, `MatchState.outcomes` (7.12) | `sorted_ids` |

Adding a `Mapping` to a public signature means adding a row here in the same
commit. The one legal exception is a mapping used purely for lookup, where no
loop over its keys ever reaches the output.

#### The canonical account order (normative, stated exactly once)

> **The canonical account order is: ranked agents by ascending numeric suffix
> (`A1 < A2 < ... < A10`), then `MM`, then `FEES`.**

This is the only statement of it in this document. `SettlementApplied` and
`PositionSnapshot` are emitted once per account, so this order *is* the journal
byte order and therefore the AC-P1 hash. Rules:

1. There is exactly one implementation, `pxe.types.sorted_account_ids(ids)`.
   `AccountBook.account_ids()` returns exactly `sorted_account_ids(...)` and
   every per account loop in the engine iterates exactly that tuple.
2. `pxe.types.sorted_ids` is for market ids and ranked agent ids only. It
   **raises** `InvalidConfigError` when handed `MM` or `FEES`, so the helper
   cannot produce the wrong order by accident: lexicographically `FEES` sorts
   before `MM`, which is not the canonical order.
3. Any other spelling of the order found in a docstring, a comment or a review
   is stale and must be corrected to this one.

### 2.4 Errors

`pxe.errors.PxeError` is the root; every subclass carries a stable `code`. The
`code` values are re-exported as `pxe.types.RejectReason` members for everything
that can appear in a journal.

**The mapping is a function, not a convention.** `pxe.types.reject_reason_of(error)`
is the one converter: it returns `RejectReason(error.code)` and raises
`InvalidConfigError` on an unmapped code, which is a contract bug (add the
member) and never an agent behaviour. A05 filling `OrderRejected.reason` from a
caught `ExchangeError`, A11 filling `AgentActionRejected.reason` and A13
filling `AgentReply.error` all go through it. The relation is **not** a
bijection and does not need to be: many `RejectReason` members
(`INVALID_PRICE`, `MISSING_FIELD`, `DUPLICATE_PREDICTION`, `TALKING_MODE_OFF`,
...) are produced by `validate_action` as values and have no exception, because
validation never raises on agent input. What is required, and what
`tests/test_types_boundaries.py::test_every_exchange_error_maps` asserts, is that every
`ExchangeError` and `GatewayError` subclass has a `RejectReason`.

* Library code never raises bare `Exception`/`ValueError`/`AssertionError`.
* The engine never catches `PxeError` broadly. An `InvariantViolationError`
  aborts the match: it is an engine bug, never an agent behaviour, and it must
  never be downgraded into an integrity incident.
* Only `pxe.gateway` swallows errors, and only to implement the FR-5.1.1 "no
  action" fallback.
* Agent misbehaviour is **never** an exception at the engine boundary. It
  produces an `OrderRejected` or `AgentActionRejected` event and the match
  continues.

### 2.5 Logging

`logging.getLogger("pxe.<module>")`. The library never configures handlers; the
CLI does. Log records carry structured `extra` fields (`match_id`, `tick`,
`agent_id`) and never contain a raw model output longer than 200 characters.
**Logging is never a substitute for an event**: anything a metric or the UI needs
is an event, not a log line.

### 2.6 Configuration objects

All configuration is a frozen dataclass in `pxe.types`
(`MatchConfig`, `MMConfig`, `GatewayConfig`, `HarnessConfig`, `InfoProfile`,
`ScenarioSpec`). TOML/CLI parsing happens only in `pxe.cli` and
`pxe.tournament.orchestrator`, which build those dataclasses and pass them down.
No module reads an environment variable, a file or `sys.argv` on its own.

`MatchConfig` is journalled in `MatchStarted.config`, encoded by exactly one
function: `pxe.types.config_to_journal_dict(config)`, with
`config_from_journal_dict` as its inverse (FR-5.1.3 needs the inverse, or
`replay_journal` cannot rebuild a `MatchState`). Nobody calls
`dataclasses.asdict` on a config to build an event. Adding a field with a new
default changes every journal hash: bump `ENGINE_VERSION` when you do.

**`MatchConfig` may not contain a float.** Not one, not ever. `canonical_json`
rejects floats structurally, so a single float field crashes `MatchStarted`,
that is the first event of every match. Anything wall clock, cost or provider
dependent (timeouts, budgets, retries, concurrency) belongs to `GatewayConfig`.
`tests/test_types.py::test_match_config_is_journal_encodable` asserts both
`canonical_json(asdict(MatchConfig()))` and
`canonical_json(config_to_journal_dict(MatchConfig()))` succeed, and
`tests/test_types.py::test_match_config_has_no_float_field` walks the
annotations. `MatchConfig()` is constructible with no argument (`seed`
defaults to `0`) precisely so those tests, and the golden fixtures, can be
written without a fixture.

`GatewayConfig` is **not** journalled: it is wall clock and cost dependent. It
owns `timeout_s`, the per agent budget, the FR-6.2.3 token caps and the retry
policy. No timeout value appears anywhere in a journal, so two runs of the same
seed with different timeouts produce the same hash.

#### `MatchConfig` wins over `ScenarioSpec`, and disagreement is fatal

`ticks_total`, `talking_mode` and `liquidity_profile_name` exist on both
objects: `generate_world(...)` writes the scenario copies, the orchestrator's
TOML writes the config copies, and `MatchRunner.__init__(*, config, world, ...)`
receives both. The rule, stated once:

> **`MatchConfig` is authoritative. The `ScenarioSpec` copies are
> informational, and `MatchRunner.__init__` raises `InvalidConfigError` when
> they disagree.**

Concretely the runner refuses to start when any of these holds:

```
config.ticks_total            != world.scenario.ticks_total
config.talking_mode           != world.scenario.talking_mode
config.liquidity_profile_name != world.scenario.liquidity_profile_name
config.mm                     != liquidity_profile(config.liquidity_profile_name).mm
config.n_markets              != len(world.scenario.markets)
```

The last two catch the nastier version of the same bug: `MatchConfig.mm` is an
`MMConfig` that can silently disagree with the preset `liquidity_profile_name`
names, and the market maker is built from `config.mm`, so the journal would
record one preset and the book would show another.
`test_match_runner.py::test_config_and_scenario_agree` pins all five.

### 2.7 Purity boundary

| Layer | May call | Forbidden |
|---|---|---|
| `types`, `events`, `rng`, `errors` | stdlib only | everything else |
| `world`, `info`, `exchange`, `mm`, `oracle`, `runner`, `metrics`, `integrity` | anchor modules, each other per the dependency arrows | clock, network, filesystem, `random` module state, `asyncio`, LLM |
| `journal`, `store` | filesystem, database | LLM |
| `gateway` | subprocess, `asyncio`, clock | mutating engine state |
| `api`, `cli`, `tournament`, `evolve` | everything | breaking determinism of a match |

The engine is synchronous. The runner obtains agent **replies** through
`AgentGateway.collect_actions(...) -> tuple[AgentReply, ...]`, a **synchronous**
method whose default implementation drives the async one with `asyncio.run`.
This single call is the only place where the pure world touches the impure one.
The gateway hands back raw payloads and never validates them; the runner turns
them into `AgentAction` objects through `validate_action`, which is the only
call site (sections 5 P2 and 7.16).

---

## 3. Determinism contract (O1, FR-5.1.4, AC-P1)

### 3.1 One root seed

A match has exactly one integer seed, `MatchConfig.seed`, an unsigned **63**
bit integer (`0 <= seed < pxe.types.SEED_SPACE`, that is `2**63`). Everything
random in the match derives from it through one `pxe.rng.RngTree`.

**Why 63 and not 64** (wave-3 correction, ruling R95). Every seed is persisted:
`scenario_instance.seed`, `match.seed`, `match_task.seed`. Section 7.20 makes
those `BigInteger`, which is a **signed** 64 bit column on SQLite and on
Postgres alike, and SQLite raises `OverflowError` on any value at or above
`2**63`. A 64 bit seed space therefore made about half of every
`HeldoutBank.reserve` draw unstorable, and the tournament that drew one died on
its first `save_task` with AC-P5 uncheckable. The bound now lives in one
constant, `pxe.types.SEED_SPACE`, and `MatchConfig.__post_init__`,
`generate_world`, `HeldoutBank.reserve`, `FailureHallOfFame.record` and
`TournamentOrchestrator._validate` all read it.
`test_store.py::test_the_largest_legal_seed_reaches_every_seed_column` pins the
top of the range and, as its own control, pins that one bit more really is
refused by the column; `test_heldout.py::test_every_sealed_seed_survives_the_store`
seals sixty-four seeds and stores every one of them.

`pxe.rng.RngTree` still accepts the whole unsigned 64 bit space, and
deliberately: a tree root derived inside the engine
(`derive_seed(...) % 2**64` for the tournament tree) is never written anywhere,
so nothing narrows it and narrowing it would move the scheduling stream.

One tree is built per match and its sub-trees are handed to components so their
substream namespaces cannot collide:

```python
root = RngTree(config.seed)
world_rng   = RngTree(config.seed).child("world")   # rebuilt inside generate_world
info_rng    = root.child("info")      # runner builds it, gives it to InfoEngine
mm_rng      = root.child("mm")        # runner builds it, gives it to the market maker
runner_rng  = root                    # runner uses the root namespace
agent_rng   = root.child(f"agent/{agent_id}")   # given to each scripted agent
```

Three of those five are handed over explicitly and two are not, and the
difference is in the signatures of section 7:

* `InfoEngine(*, world, profiles, rng, ticks_total)` and
  `ReferenceMarketMaker(*, config, market_ids, rng)` take an `RngTree`, and the
  **runner** builds both objects and passes `rng.child("info")` and
  `rng.child("mm")` (section 7.12, "what the runner constructs").
* Each scripted agent takes a plain `random.Random`, namely
  `root.child(f"agent/{agent_id}").substream(f"agent.{agent_id}")`, built by
  whoever constructs the agents (`run_match`, the CLI, the orchestrator).
* `generate_world(*, template_id, seed, ...)` takes the **seed and not a
  tree**, and builds `RngTree(seed).child("world")` itself before calling
  `WorldTemplate.build(..., rng=<that tree>)`. That is the same tree
  `root.child("world")` would be, so the world stays a function of the seed
  alone, and world generation needs no live object from the caller. Do not
  pass a tree to `generate_world`: there is no parameter for one.
* `Oracle(*, world)` takes **no** `RngTree` at all and there is no registered
  `oracle.*` substream: resolution reads the latent process the world already
  froze, so the oracle draws nothing.

**Who builds it (normative).** The **caller** builds `RngTree(config.seed)`,
not the runner. `run_match`, `pxe.cli` and `TournamentOrchestrator` are the
callers; they must, because `generate_world` and the scripted agents are
constructed *before* the `MatchRunner` that would otherwise own the tree, and
`MatchRunner.__init__` receives an already built `gateway` wrapping already
built agents. The tree is then passed in explicitly:

```python
rng = RngTree(config.seed)
world = generate_world(template_id=..., seed=config.seed, ticks_total=config.ticks_total, ...)
agents = {aid: make_baseline(name, agent_id=aid, config=config,
                             rng=rng.child(f"agent/{aid}").substream(f"agent.{aid}"))
          for aid in sorted_ids(seat_ids)}
runner = MatchRunner(config=config, world=world, agents=specs,
                     gateway=ScriptedGateway(agents=agents), journal=journal,
                     match_id=match_id, rng=rng)
```

`MatchRunner` takes `rng: RngTree` as a required keyword argument and uses it
for `runner.tick_shuffle.<tick>` and for the two sub-trees it hands to the
components it builds itself, the info engine (`rng.child("info")`) and the
market maker (`rng.child("mm")`). It never constructs an `RngTree` of its own:
two trees built from the same seed in two places is how the same substream gets
consumed twice.

**Reuse across matches is reset, not luck.** Immediately after emitting
`MatchStarted` and before tick 1, the runner calls, for every scripted seat in
ascending `agent_id`:

```python
agent.reset(config=config, rng=rng.child(f"agent/{agent_id}").substream(f"agent.{agent_id}"))
```

`ScriptedAgent.reset` is therefore not optional decoration: an orchestrator that
reuses agent objects across two matches (which it does) leaves internal state
behind otherwise, and O1 dies silently.
`test_determinism.py::test_reused_agent_objects_replay_identically` runs two
consecutive matches over the *same* agent instances and asserts the two journals
are byte identical to two fresh runs.

A tournament has its own `RngTree(tournament_seed)` used only for scheduling and
profile permutation; match seeds are values it produces, and a match never sees
the tournament tree.

### 3.2 Derivation

`derive_seed(root_seed, name) = blake2b_256(uint64_be(root_seed) || 0x1f || utf8(qualified_name))`
interpreted as a big endian integer, where `qualified_name` is
`"<namespace>/<name>"` when the tree has a namespace and `"<name>"` otherwise.
That integer seeds a `random.Random`.

`hash()` is never used: it is salted per process. `uuid`, `secrets`, `time`,
`os.urandom` and `numpy.random`'s global state are banned inside the engine.
`numpy` is allowed only when seeded from a substream
(`np.random.default_rng(tree.seed_for(name) % 2**64)`).

**Only `getrandbits` is consumed.** `random.Random.shuffle`, `.random`,
`.randrange`, `.choice`, `.gauss` and `.betavariate` are banned in the engine:
their algorithms are CPython implementation details (already changed across
releases) and the distribution helpers call the platform libm, whose `log`,
`exp` and `cos` are not bit identical between glibc, msvcrt and macOS. A latent
value landing within one ulp of a `_milli` boundary would then quantise
differently on two machines and the world would diverge, which is exactly what
AC-P1 forbids.

Therefore `pxe.rng` implements the permutation (Fisher-Yates over
`_randbelow`), the uniform, the Bernoulli, the normal (Acklam inverse CDF), the
log normal, the Beta (Marsaglia-Tsang) and its own `log` and `exp` on top of
`getrandbits` plus operations IEEE-754 requires to be correctly rounded
(`+ - * /`, `math.sqrt`) or exact (`math.frexp`, `math.ldexp`, `math.floor`).
The algorithm is therefore versioned by us, in `pxe.rng.RNG_ALGORITHM_VERSION`,
and not by the CPython release. Changing anything in that section of `rng.py`
moves every golden hash: bump `RNG_ALGORITHM_VERSION` and `ENGINE_VERSION` in
the same commit and regenerate `tests/golden/`.

### 3.3 Registered substreams

Every name must be in `pxe.rng.SUBSTREAMS` (exact) or match a family in
`pxe.rng.SUBSTREAM_PREFIXES`. Unregistered names raise `UnknownSubstreamError`.
**Adding randomness means adding a name to that registry in the same commit.**

Exact names:

```
world.template      world.latent      world.markets     world.correlation
world.resolution    world.outcome     world.prior
info.schedule       info.news         info.noise        info.signals
info.profiles       mm.tiebreak
tournament.pairing  tournament.latin_square  tournament.seeds  tournament.heldout
metrics.bootstrap   integrity.bootstrap      elites.mutation   test.scratch
```

There is deliberately **no** `runner.agent_order`: the runner's only source of
randomness is the per tick family `runner.tick_shuffle.<tick>` below. A second
registered but unused runner name is an invitation to a second, differently
seeded shuffle.

Parametric families (`prefix` + `.` + non empty suffix):

```
runner.tick_shuffle.<tick>     the P3 fairness shuffle, FR-5.1.5
info.signals.tick.<tick>       per tick private signal draw
info.news.tick.<tick>          per tick news draw
agent.<agent_id>               one scripted agent's own generator
world.market.<market_id>       per market latent draw
test.<anything>                test scaffolding only
```

### 3.4 The P3 fairness shuffle (FR-5.1.5)

This is the single normative spelling. It appears here and, identically, in
section 5 P3 step 10. Nowhere else.

```python
seats = sorted_ids(state.ranked_agent_ids())      # ALL seats, frozen included
order = [a
         for a in shuffle_seeded(
             runner_rng.fresh_substream(f"runner.tick_shuffle.{tick}"), seats)
         if a not in state.frozen_agent_ids]
```

Two properties make this correct, and both are lost if you shuffle the filtered
list instead:

* `fresh_substream` (not `substream`) so the shuffle of tick `t` depends on
  `(seed, t)` alone and never on how many draws earlier ticks consumed.
* **The full seat list is shuffled, then filtered.** Shuffling
  `active_ranked_agent_ids` would make the permutation depend on which agents
  are frozen, that is on agent behaviour, so RNG consumption would differ
  between two runs whose only difference is a bankruptcy. Filtering afterwards
  keeps the permutation a function of `(seed, tick)` alone, which is exactly
  what `test_determinism.py::test_tick_shuffle_depends_only_on_seed_and_tick`
  (FR-5.1.5) asserts.

The market maker is **not** in the list: it requotes before the shuffle
(FR-5.8.1).

### 3.5 What is forbidden in a journal

Anything that varies between two runs of the same seed:

* wall clock timestamps, durations, latencies;
* provider costs, token counts, retry counts, model ids of a *failed* call;
* memory addresses, `repr` of objects, `id()`;
* floats (structurally rejected by `canonical_json`);
* iteration order of a `set` or a `dict` built from one.

Those live in `runs/<match_id>/llm_trace.jsonl` and
`runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl`, which are explicitly outside the journal
hash.

**No float-derived value enters the journal at all.** In particular
`ObservationBuilt` carries `agent_id`, `obs_version`, `n_markets`, `n_news` and
`n_signals` and **not** `obs_hash` / `obs_bytes`: those are properties of the
observation renderer (A10), which is free to recompact text and depth, and they
are derived through `stable_json`, the one encoder that accepts floats.
Journalling them would put a presentation module inside AC-P1 and route floats
into the hash chain. They are two of the five keys of every line of `observations.jsonl` instead
(`agent_id`, `tick`, `obs_hash`, `obs_bytes`, `payload`, section 7.13), which
keeps the audit trail without the coupling. "First two" would be wrong:
`stable_json` sorts object keys like `canonical_json` does, so no writer
chooses a key order. See decision 11.

### 3.6 AC-P1 procedure

`pxe match verify --template <t> --seed <s> --repeat 2` runs the same match
twice in two fresh processes with `PYTHONHASHSEED` unset and different values,
and compares `journal_hash`. The golden fixtures in `tests/golden/` freeze the
expected hash for three reference scenarios; `tests/test_determinism.py` fails
the build if any of them moves.

### 3.7 What AC-P1 actually covers

AC-P1 ("same seed, two machines, bit identical journals") is a claim about
**scripted** matches. It is not, and cannot be, a claim about LLM matches:

* `AgentTimedOut` is a journal event, and whether it fires is a wall clock
  decision (`GatewayConfig.timeout_s`) and a budget decision
  (`BudgetTracker`). A slow provider run and a fast one on the same seed
  diverge from the first timeout onwards and every following `seq` shifts.
* `AgentActionReceived.rationale` is verbatim model text, which is not a
  function of the seed.

So, normatively:

| Match kind | Guarantee | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| Scripted (all agents in `pxe.agents`) | re-running the same seed produces a bit identical journal, on any machine | `pxe match verify`, `tests/golden/` |
| LLM (any agent behind `ClaudeCliGateway`) | the engine's decisions are a deterministic function of the actions it received; the recorded journal replays bit identically through `replay_journal` | `test_replay.py::test_replay_from_journal_only` |

`pxe match verify` refuses to run against an LLM harness rather than reporting
a spurious determinism failure (CLI exit code `1`, not `2`).

---

## 4. Event sourcing contract (FR-5.1.3)

### 4.1 The rule

**The journal alone replays a match.** `pxe.runner.replay.replay_journal(events)`
rebuilds the full `MatchState` and every metric without the world generator, the
info engine, the agents or the gateway. Anything a projection needs must
therefore be *in* an event, not recomputed from a component.

Corollary: agent actions are journalled verbatim (they are not derivable), while
observations are pure projections and only their digest is journalled.

### 4.2 Canonical serialisation

One event per line:

```
line = canonical_json(event.to_dict()) + "\n"
```

`canonical_json` uses `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"),
ensure_ascii=False, allow_nan=False)` over a structure containing only `dict`,
`list`, `str`, `int`, `bool` and `None`. It **raises**
`NonCanonicalValueError` on any float, set, or unsupported type. Object keys are
sorted by code point. Files are UTF-8, no BOM, LF line endings.

`stable_json` is the sibling used for **agent facing** payloads (observations,
prompts). It allows finite floats and rounds them to 6 decimals through an
integer (`floor(x * 1e6 + 0.5) / 1e6`, never the banned builtin `round`). It is
never used to write the journal, and nothing it produces enters the journal
hash.

**LF is contractual, not prose.** Every writer of a journal file opens it as:

```python
open(path, mode, encoding=pxe.events.JOURNAL_ENCODING, newline=pxe.events.JOURNAL_NEWLINE)
# that is: encoding="utf-8", newline="\n"
```

`mode` is `"w"` for a fresh file and `"a"` for an append. Reading uses
`open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")` so a stray `\r` is not
swallowed by universal newline translation. The primary development platform
here is Windows, where the default text mode silently turns `"\n"` into
`"\r\n"`: the in-memory `journal_hash(events)` would still be right, the bytes
on disk would not, and AC-P1 would only fail once two machines compared files.
`journal_hash_from_lines` therefore **raises** `NonCanonicalValueError` on any
line containing a carriage return instead of normalising it away.

Two tests, in two files, cover the two halves of that claim, and they are not
the same test:

* `tests/test_events.py::test_written_lines_hash_equals_journal_hash` (A01,
  `determinism`, landed) writes `event_to_line(...)` by hand with those exact
  `open()` arguments and asserts
  `blake2b(path.read_bytes(), digest_size=32).hexdigest() == journal_hash(events)`
  and no carriage return is present in the bytes. It pins the **encoder**.
* `tests/test_journal.py::test_file_bytes_hash_equals_journal_hash` (A02,
  `determinism`) makes the same assertion against a real `Journal`, with
  `journal.hash()` on the right hand side. It pins the **writer**, and it is
  the one AC-P1 and T1.2 cite.

### 4.3 Journal hash (the AC-P1 artefact)

```
journal_hash = blake2b(digest_size=32) over the concatenation of every line, hex
```

Because a line is exactly what is written to disk, the digest of the events
equals the digest of the `journal.jsonl` bytes.
`pxe.events.journal_hash(events)` and `journal_hash_from_lines(lines)` must
always agree.

### 4.4 Ordering guarantees

* `seq` starts at 1 and increases by exactly **1**; `verify_journal` enforces it.
* `MatchStarted` is `seq == 1` with `tick == 0`; `MatchEnded` is the last event.
* Events are grouped by ascending `tick`, and inside a tick by phase order
  P1, P2, P3, P4 (section 5). Two `tick` values are not real ticks: `0` for
  `MatchStarted`, and `config.ticks_total + 1` for every finalisation event
  (the last resolutions, their settlements, and `MatchEnded`). See section 5.0
  and finalisation step 17.
* Inside a phase, order is fixed by the per phase rules of section 5. Where a
  loop is over accounts or markets, it uses the canonical order of section 2.3.
* `TickStarted` is the first event of each tick.

### 4.5 Event catalogue

Envelope on every event: `seq: int`, `type: str`, `match_id: str`, `tick: int`.
Every payload field below is in addition to those four.

| Event | Phase | Emitted per | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| `match_started` | pre | once | `seed`, `engine_version`, `obs_version`, `action_version`, `scenario_template_id`, `scenario_template_version`, `ticks_total`, `initial_cash_cents`, `config`, `markets[]`, `agents[]`, `mm_config` |
| `tick_started` | P1 | tick | `open_market_ids[]` |
| `news_published` | P1 | news item | `news_id`, `market_ids[]`, `headline`, `body`, `impact`, `is_noise`, `origin` |
| `signal_delivered` | P1 | signal | `signal_id`, `agent_id`, `market_id`, `kind`, `value_milli`, `precision_ppm` |
| `market_resolved` | P1 | resolved market | `market_id`, `outcome`, `payout_cents`, `resolution_tick`, `latent_value_milli` |
| `market_cancelled` | P1 | cancelled market | `market_id`, `reason` |
| `settlement_applied` | P1 | (market, account) | `market_id`, `account_id`, `mode`, `position_qty`, `cash_delta_cents`, `cash_before_cents`, `cash_after_cents`, `released_collateral_cents` |
| `observation_built` | P1 | agent | `agent_id`, `obs_version`, `n_markets`, `n_news`, `n_signals` (no `obs_hash`, no `obs_bytes`: section 3.5) |
| `agent_action_received` | P2 | agent | `agent_id`, `action_version`, `source`, `predictions[]`, `orders[]`, `message_public`, `rationale`, `n_rejected` |
| `agent_action_rejected` | P2 | rejected item | `agent_id`, `scope`, `item_index`, `reason`, `detail` |
| `agent_timed_out` | P2 | failing agent | `agent_id`, `reason` |
| `prediction_recorded` | P2 | (agent, open market) | `agent_id`, `market_id`, `p_yes_ppm`, `carried` |
| `message_posted` | P2 | message | `agent_id`, `text`, `deliver_tick` |
| `mm_quoted` | P3 | open market | `market_id`, `ref_price`, `anchor_price`, `bid_price`, `ask_price`, `quote_qty`, `effective_spread`, `inventory_qty`, `skew_cents`, `widened`, `widen_until_tick` |
| `order_placed` | P3 | accepted order | `order_id`, `agent_id`, `market_id`, `side`, `requested_type`, `price`, `qty`, `tif`, `reserved_cents`, `ref_price`, `active_orders_after` |
| `order_rejected` | P3 | refused intent | `agent_id`, `market_id`, `side`, `requested_type`, `price`, `qty`, `reason`, `detail`, `item_index` |
| `order_cancelled` | P3 (also P1) | order leaving the book | `order_id`, `agent_id`, `market_id`, `side`, `price`, `remaining_qty`, `reason`, `released_cents` |
| `trade_executed` | P3 | execution | `trade_id`, `market_id`, `price`, `qty`, `maker_order_id`, `maker_agent_id`, `maker_side`, `taker_order_id`, `taker_agent_id`, `taker_side`, `taker_fee_cents`, `maker_cash_delta_cents`, `taker_cash_delta_cents`, `maker_position_after`, `taker_position_after` |
| `stp_cancelled` | P3 | prevented self trade | `agent_id`, `market_id`, `incoming_order_id`, `resting_order_id`, `cancelled_qty`, `cancel_seq` (no money: see P3 step 11) |
| `agent_frozen` | P4 | bankruptcy | `agent_id`, `equity_cents`, `cancelled_order_ids[]` |
| `mark_to_market` | P4 | open market | `market_id`, `ref_price`, `ref_source`, `best_bid`, `best_ask`, `mid_price`, `last_price`, `bid_depth_qty`, `ask_depth_qty`, `tick_volume_qty` |
| `position_snapshot` | P4 | account | `account_id`, `cash_cents`, `reserved_cents`, `free_cash_cents`, `equity_cents`, `frozen`, `positions[]`, `resting_order_count` |
| `incident_raised` | any | technical incident only | `incident_id`, `kind`, `severity`, `agent_ids[]`, `market_ids[]`, `score_ppm`, `detector_version`, `detail` |
| `match_ended` | post | once | `reason`, `final_tick`, `rankings[]`, `mm_pnl_cents`, `fees_collected_cents`, `event_count` |

**One emitter per event, exactly one.** An event with two possible emitters is
an event emitted twice or not at all. The table is exhaustive; a module not
named here never calls `journal.emit` for that type.

| Event | The one module that emits it |
|---|---|
| `match_started`, `tick_started`, `agent_timed_out`, `agent_action_rejected`, `agent_action_received`, `prediction_recorded`, `message_posted`, `agent_frozen`, `mark_to_market`, `position_snapshot`, `match_ended` | `runner/match_runner.py` (A09) |
| `news_published` (info engine items at P1 step 2, resolution items at P1 step 4d, cancellation items at P1 step 5) | `runner/match_runner.py` (A09) |
| `signal_delivered` | `runner/match_runner.py` (A09) |
| `observation_built` | `runner/match_runner.py` (A09), from what `build_observation` returned |
| `order_placed`, `order_rejected`, `order_cancelled`, `trade_executed`, `stp_cancelled` | `exchange/exchange.py` (A05) |
| `market_resolved`, `market_cancelled` | `oracle/resolver.py` (A08) |
| `settlement_applied` | `oracle/settlement.py` (A08) |
| `mm_quoted` | `mm/market_maker.py` (A07) |
| `incident_raised` (`IncidentKind.TECHNICAL` only) | whichever module detected the technical fault, through the journal it already holds |

Consequences worth stating because they were each wrong once in this document:
`AccountBook` (A06) emits **nothing** and holds no `Journal`; `Oracle.resolve`
emits `MarketResolved` and delegates `SettlementApplied` to
`oracle.settlement.settle`, and the runner emits neither; the runner emits the
resolution `NewsPublished` and the oracle does not.

**One event per money movement, exactly one.** Every cent that moves is
reported by exactly one event, so a projection that sums a field never double
counts:

| Movement | The one event that carries it |
|---|---|
| collateral released when an order leaves the book, for any reason (agent cancel, IOC residual, MM requote, market resolved, STP, freeze) | `order_cancelled.released_cents` |
| cash and fee moved by an execution | `trade_executed.*_cash_delta_cents` and `taker_fee_cents` |
| cash moved by a resolution or an unwind | `settlement_applied.cash_delta_cents` |

`stp_cancelled`, `agent_frozen.cancelled_order_ids` and `mm_quoted` are cross
references only. They carry no money field.

**Integrity incidents are not journalled.** Detectors run offline over a finished
journal and **write** `runs/<match_id>/incidents.jsonl`, replacing it whole.
Bumping a detector must never change a journal hash. The engine emits
`incident_raised` only for `IncidentKind.TECHNICAL`.

"Write" and not "append", which an earlier draft said: `run_detectors` numbers
its output `i-0001`, `i-0002`, ... from one on every call (section 2.2 makes the
id positional), so appending a second run would leave two generations of alerts
sharing one id space, and nothing in the file distinguishes them. `Store.save_incidents`
replaces a match's rows for the same reason. See ruling R83.

### 4.6 Artefacts on disk

```
runs/<match_id>/
  journal.jsonl        the journal. Hashed. Reproducible.        (A02)
  observations.jsonl   obs_hash, obs_bytes, stable_json payload  (A10)
  llm_trace.jsonl      cost, latency, tokens, retries, raw text  (A14)
  incidents.jsonl      integrity detector output                 (A18)
  metrics.json         projected metrics, write_metrics()         (A15)
  meta.json            match_id, seed, journal_hash, versions    (A09 + A21)
```

Only `journal.jsonl` is hashed and only it is required to replay.

**`meta.json` is the one artefact with two writers, and that is deliberate.**
`MatchRunner` writes the file next to the journal it has just produced
(finalisation step 19), and it cannot go through `pxe.store.files.write_meta`,
because `runner` sits to the **left** of `store` on the dependency arrow and
importing rightward is what section 7 forbids. A21 therefore owns the generic
encoder and the only reader (`write_meta` / `read_meta`, section 7.20), and both
producers use `canonical_json` plus the section 4.2 `open()` arguments, so the
bytes are identical. That claim is a test and not a hope:
`test_store.py::test_read_meta_reads_the_meta_the_runner_actually_wrote` plays a
real match, reads the runner's file with `read_meta` and re-encodes it with
`write_meta`, asserting the two byte strings are equal. Every other artefact has
exactly one writer, named in the tree above.

---

## 5. Tick phase contract (PRD section 5.1)

`MatchRunner.run()` is exactly:

```
emit MatchStarted                       # seq 1, tick 0
reset every scripted agent              # section 3.1
for tick in 1..T:
    P1 = phase_p1_diffusion(tick)   -> observations
    P2 = phase_p2_decision(tick, observations) -> actions
    P3 = phase_p3_execution(tick, actions)
    P4 = phase_p4_close(tick)
finalise()                              # virtual tick T + 1: resolve what is
                                        # still open, rank, emit MatchEnded
```

Calling a phase out of order raises `PhaseOrderError`.

### 5.0 What `resolution_tick` means (normative, stated exactly once)

> **A market whose `resolution_tick` is `r` is open and tradable for the whole
> of tick `r` (it appears in the tick `r` observation, agents may trade it in
> P3 of tick `r`, and it produces a `PredictionRecorded` term at tick `r`); it
> is resolved in P1 of tick `r + 1`, at step 4, before that tick's
> observations are built; and when `r == ticks_total` that resolution happens
> in finalisation, which runs at the virtual tick `ticks_total + 1`.**

Everything else follows from that one sentence and must not be restated
differently anywhere:

* `Oracle.due_market_ids(tick)` returns the still `OPEN` markets whose
  `resolution_tick < tick`, ascending. At tick `t` it therefore resolves the
  markets scheduled for `t - 1`. It returns `()` at tick 1.
* `TickStarted.open_market_ids` is the set of markets that will be **tradable
  in P3 of this tick**, that is `sorted_ids(state.open_market_ids())` minus
  `oracle.due_market_ids(tick)` **and minus the market ids of
  `oracle.due_cancellations(tick)`**. It is computed at step 1 and the
  resolutions themselves are emitted at step 4; `TickStarted` stays the first
  event of the tick (section 4.4) and never advertises a market no agent can
  touch. The step 3 signal filter uses exactly the same set.

  Both subtractions are required, and an earlier draft of this list named only
  the first, which contradicted P1 step 5 in the same document ("the market is
  not tradable on the tick it is cancelled"). A cancellation fires at tick `c`,
  so the market is closed before P3 of tick `c` runs: leaving it in the set made
  `TickStarted` advertise a market no agent can touch **and** let a
  `SignalDelivered` through for a market the observation does not carry, which
  is precisely the dangling reference the step 3 filter exists to prevent. The
  bug was invisible because all three shipped templates produce
  `cancellations = ()`, so no golden hash moved when it was fixed;
  `test_match_runner.py::test_a_cancelled_market_is_not_tradable_on_its_cancellation_tick`
  now pins it against a hand built `ScenarioSpec`.
* The envelope `tick` of `MarketResolved` is `r + 1`; its payload
  `resolution_tick` is `r`. They differ by one on purpose, and a projection
  keying on the payload field gets the scheduled tick.
* PRD section 7.2 ("a market stops counting strictly **after** its resolution
  tick") is satisfied literally: an agent that played every tick produces
  exactly `resolution_tick` Brier terms per market, and the resolution tick
  term (the most informative one) is included. See section 9.
* `markets` in an observation may still legally be **empty**: FR-5.2.3 lets
  every market have `resolution_tick < ticks_total`, so from tick
  `max(resolution_tick) + 1` onwards there is nothing open. The observation is
  still built and still emitted with `n_markets = 0`
  (`schemas/observation.v1.json` sets `minItems: 0` for exactly this), P2 skips
  the gateway call for every agent that tick, and no `PredictionRecorded` is
  emitted. Section 8.1 and section 9 repeat only the consequence, never the
  rule.
* The earlier draft resolved at the *start* of tick `r`, which made the market
  untradable on its own resolution tick, dropped its Brier term, and (when
  every market resolved at `T`) produced an empty observation at `T` that the
  schema then rejected. `test_oracle.py::test_market_is_tradable_on_its_resolution_tick`
  and `test_metrics_calibration.py::test_n_terms_equals_resolution_tick` pin
  the current rule.

### P1 Diffusion (pure)

Ordered engine calls:

0. `exchange.begin_tick(tick)`. This resets the per tick counters that
   `MarkToMarket.tick_volume_qty` reads in P4 step 12. It emits nothing and it
   is the first thing the runner does, before `TickStarted`. Without it
   `tick_volume` accumulates over the whole match, which no test would catch
   until the UI showed a monotonically rising "volume this tick".
1. `journal.emit(TickStarted, tick=tick, open_market_ids=<tradable this tick>)`,
   as defined in section 5.0.
2. `info.news_for_tick(tick)` -> for each item in publication order:
   `emit(NewsPublished, origin="info_engine")`. **No `mm.note_news` call here:**
   there is exactly one, at step 5b, after every news item of the tick exists.
3. `info.signals_for_tick(tick)` -> for each signal sorted by
   `(canonical seat rank, signal_id)`: `emit(SignalDelivered)`. That is
   `sorted_ids` order on the agent id, then the signal id; never lexicographic
   order on the agent id.

   "Sorted by `(agent_id, signal_id)`" was the earlier spelling and it
   contradicted section 2.3 at ten seats (`A10 < A2` lexicographically, `A2 <
   A10` canonically). The two coincide for `A1`..`A9`, which is every seat count
   PRD section 5.7 allows, so no journal moves; the spelling is corrected because
   section 2.3 is the single statement of the ordering and a second, different
   one in section 5 is how the drift starts.

   **A signal about a market that is not tradable this tick is dropped, not
   journalled.** The runner filters `signals_for_tick(tick)` against the same
   set `TickStarted.open_market_ids` advertised at step 1 (section 5.0), and a
   signal whose `market_id` is absent from it is discarded before emission:
   no `SignalDelivered`, no line in the observation, no row in the projection.
   `InfoProfile.delay_ticks` makes this routine rather than exceptional (a
   delayed signal about a market that matured in the meantime), and all three
   consequences of keeping it are bad: the agent is handed a `market_id` that
   the observation's `markets` array does not contain (so the LLM sees a
   dangling reference and the fidelity test of section 7.13 becomes
   ambiguous), it cannot act on it, and `reaction_latency_milli` (signal to
   next trade on that market) is undefined for a market nobody can trade,
   which silently skews an MAP-Elites axis.

   The filter lives in the **runner** and not in `InfoEngine`: the engine is
   pure and is deliberately not told which markets have resolved
   (`InfoEngine.__init__` receives `world`, `profiles`, an `RngTree` and
   `ticks_total`, none of which is a live view of engine state), so making it status aware would hand a pure component a live
   view of engine state. `signals_for_tick` therefore stays a pure function of
   `(world, profiles, seed, tick)`, which is what makes it replayable, and the
   drop is a runner decision.

   Because the drop happens after the draw, the RNG consumption of
   `info.signals.tick.<tick>` is **not** a function of which markets are open:
   the same seed consumes the same draws whether a signal survives or not.
   Filtering inside the engine, before the draw, would have made the info
   stream depend on resolution history and broken FR-5.1.4.
   `test_info_engine.py::test_no_signal_for_a_resolved_market` (runner side)
   and `test_info_engine.py::test_signal_draw_is_independent_of_resolutions`
   (engine side) pin both halves.
4. For each `market_id` in `oracle.due_market_ids(tick)`, ascending, **one
   call**:

   ```python
   report = oracle.resolve(tick=tick, market_id=mid, exchange=exchange,
                           accounts=accounts, journal=journal)
   ```

   `Oracle.resolve` performs sub-steps a, b, c and e itself and returns a
   `ResolutionReport`. The runner performs only sub-step d. Implementing a, b,
   c or e in the runner as well produces two `cancel_all` passes and two
   `MarketResolved` events from the same tick, which is exactly what happened
   when this step was written as five runner bullets.

   a. (oracle) `exchange.cancel_all(tick=tick, market_id=...,
      reason=CancelReason.MARKET_RESOLVED)` -> one `OrderCancelled` per resting
      order, in `priority_key` order, bids then asks. This runs **before** the
      settlement so the order collateral is released before the position leg is
      applied, which is the only ordering that keeps I4 true throughout.
   b. (oracle) `emit(MarketResolved)`, with envelope `tick = r + 1` and payload
      `resolution_tick = r` (section 5.0).
   c. (oracle) `oracle.settlement.settle(tick=..., market_id=..., outcome=...,
      accounts=..., journal=..., mode="resolution")`, which mutates the ledger
      through `accounts.apply_settlement` and emits one `SettlementApplied` per
      returned line, in canonical account order, skipping accounts flat on that
      market with a null cost basis. `AccountBook` never emits an event: it has
      no journal handle, and giving it one would put the emission loop in two
      workstreams at once.
   d. (**runner**) `emit(NewsPublished, origin="resolution", impact="high")`
      built from `info.resolution_news(tick=tick, market_id=..., outcome=...,
      index=...)`, announcing the outcome so agents learn it in the same channel
      as any other news. The runner owns this one because the item comes from
      the info engine, which the oracle does not hold, and because it must be
      handed to the market maker together with the rest of the tick's news at
      step 5b.
   e. (oracle) `exchange.close_market(tick=tick, market_id=...,
      status=MarketStatus.RESOLVED)`.
5. Scenario scripted cancellations due at this tick
   (`oracle.due_cancellations(tick)`, from `ScenarioSpec.cancellations`, the
   only source of a cancellation per FR-5.4.5) follow the same shape through
   `oracle.cancel_market(...)`, which emits `MarketCancelled` instead of
   `MarketResolved` and settles with `mode="unwind"` (section 6.4). A
   cancellation scheduled at tick `c` fires in P1 of tick `c`, not `c + 1`: it
   is a scripted intervention, not a scheduled maturity, and the market is not
   tradable on the tick it is cancelled.

   As at step 4d, the **runner** then emits
   `NewsPublished(origin="cancellation", impact="high")` built from
   `info.cancellation_news(tick=tick, market_id=..., reason=..., index=...)`,
   so agents learn about a cancellation through the same channel as any other
   news and the item joins the single `note_news` handover of step 5b.
   `NewsPublished.origin` therefore has exactly three values,
   `"info_engine"`, `"resolution"` and `"cancellation"`, which is what
   `NewsDto.origin` mirrors in section 7.21. The oracle emits neither news
   item: it does not hold the info engine.

   **5b. One news handover to the market maker, one call site.** After every
   `NewsPublished` of this tick has been emitted (info engine items from step 2,
   resolution items from step 4d, cancellation items from step 5), the runner
   calls exactly once:

   ```python
   mm.note_news(tick=tick, items=<every NewsItem published this tick, in emission order>)
   ```

   Splitting this into a call at step 2 and another at step 4d was how decision
   12 ("the market maker widens after every resolution") became untrue while
   `test_market_maker.py::test_post_news_widening` still passed against a
   behaviour the runner never triggered.
6. For each ranked, non frozen agent in ascending `agent_id`:
   `observation_builder.build_observation(...)` then `emit(ObservationBuilt)`.
   When no market is open the observation is still built and still emitted,
   with `n_markets = 0` (`schemas/observation.v1.json` allows an empty
   `markets` array on purpose, see FR-5.2.3 and section 5.0), and P2 skips the
   gateway call for every agent that tick.

### P2 Decision (the only impure step)

7. `replies = gateway.collect_actions(tick=tick, observations=obs, config=config)`.

   **The gateway returns `tuple[AgentReply, ...]`, never `tuple[AgentAction, ...]`.**
   One reply per observation, **sorted by `agent_id`** regardless of completion
   order. The gateway runs agents in parallel with a timeout and never
   validates: it has no `open_market_ids`, no `resting_order_ids` and no
   `MatchConfig` semantics, and if it produced `AgentAction` objects the
   `Rejection` list would be built inside an impure module and thrown away,
   which makes FR-6.2.2 unimplementable and leaves `AgentTimedOut.reason` with
   nothing to carry. `AgentReply` carries the raw payload, the failure reason,
   the latency, the cost and the token counts (section 7.16); everything except
   `raw`, `source` and `error` is wall clock or provider dependent and goes to
   `llm_trace.jsonl`, never to the journal.

   When there is no open market this call is skipped entirely and
   `replies == ()` (section 5.0): no gateway call, no provider cost, no event
   other than the `ObservationBuilt` already emitted at step 6.

8. For each reply in ascending `agent_id`:
   a. if `reply.error is not None` (timeout, provider error, budget, malformed
      response after the last retry): `emit(AgentTimedOut, reason=reply.error)`,
      and treat `reply.raw` as `None` for the rest of the block. The reason is
      a `RejectReason` value and it comes from `AgentReply.error`, which is the
      only place it exists.
   b. `outcome = validate_action(reply.raw, agent_id=reply.agent_id, tick=tick,
      config=config, open_market_ids=state.open_market_ids(),
      resting_order_ids=state.resting_order_ids(reply.agent_id),
      source=reply.source)`. `MatchState.resting_order_ids` (section 7.12) is
      the one accessor: an earlier draft called an unowned
      `accounts_resting_ids(...)` here, and `AccountBook` deliberately exposes
      a resting order **count** and not a list, because the ids live in the
      book and not in the ledger. Then, for each `outcome.rejections` entry in item
      order: `emit(AgentActionRejected)`; then `emit(AgentActionReceived)` with
      the payload `types.action_to_journal_dict(outcome.action,
      n_rejected=len(outcome.rejections))` (FR-6.2.2 truncation).
      **Validation happens here and only here.** The runner is the single call
      site of `validate_action`; no gateway ever calls it.
   c. resolve predictions with carry (FR-6.2.4) and, for each **open** market in
      ascending order, `emit(PredictionRecorded)`.
   d. if a public message survived and talking mode is on:
      `emit(MessagePosted, deliver_tick=tick+1)`.

   Steps a to d happen **inside that agent's block**, in that order: the
   agent's rejections, then its `AgentActionReceived`, then all of its
   `PredictionRecorded`, then its `MessagePosted`. `PredictionRecorded` is not
   a single global block at the end of P2. The relative order shifts every
   following `seq`, so it is normative and it is repeated in the
   `PredictionRecorded` docstring.

   `phase_p2_decision` returns `tuple[AgentAction, ...]` (the validated
   `outcome.action` of each reply, ascending by `agent_id`), which is what P3
   consumes. The `AgentReply` objects do not leave P2.

A frozen agent (FR-5.5.5) is still called? **No.** Frozen agents are skipped in
P1 step 6 and P2 entirely: no observation, no gateway call, no prediction. Their
Brier stops accumulating at the freeze tick (documented in section 9).

### P3 Execution (pure)

9. Market maker requote, **before the shuffle** (FR-5.8.1):

   ```python
   mm.requote(tick=tick, exchange=exchange,
              inventory=accounts.inventory_view(MM_ACCOUNT_ID), journal=journal)
   ```

   For each open market ascending: cancel every resting `MM` order on that
   market with
   `exchange.cancel_all(tick=tick, agent_id=MM_ACCOUNT_ID, market_id=mid,
   reason=CancelReason.MM_REQUOTE)` (the **exchange** emits the
   `OrderCancelled` events, one per order in `priority_key` order, because it
   is the single emitter of that event, section 4.5), then `emit(MMQuoted)`,
   then submit the new bid and the new ask in that order. A market maker quote that crosses
   a resting agent order executes normally and produces trades.

   The `inventory` argument is how FR-5.8.3 becomes implementable: inventory
   lives in the ledger (`AccountBook.position("MM", m)`), the market maker has
   no accounts handle, and `Exchange` exposes no position accessor, so without
   it A07 either reaches into a private attribute or keeps a second inventory
   count that drifts from A06's and breaks I10. `InventoryView` is a frozen
   snapshot taken once, at the top of the requote, before any of this tick's
   market maker orders exist.

   **A market maker quote can be rejected, and `MMQuoted` does not say so.**
   `MM` submits through `Exchange.submit` like anybody else, so the section 6.1
   collateral check applies to it and can emit `OrderRejected(agent_id="MM")`.

   `MMQuoted` carries the quote the market maker **decided**, and it is emitted
   before either side is submitted, which is the order this step prescribes. A
   **suppressed** side is a decision (the FR-5.8.3 inventory cap, or a clamp
   that would invert the book), it is known before submission, and it is
   journalled as `null`. A **rejected** side is an outcome, it is known only
   after `Exchange.submit` has returned, and it is journalled as the
   `OrderRejected(agent_id="MM")` the exchange emits: the already written
   `MMQuoted` keeps its non null price. The two are different quantities and the
   field does not conflate them.

   An earlier draft of this step said a rejected side leaves the "already
   emitted `MMQuoted`" null, which is not implementable in the order the same
   paragraph prescribes: the emission precedes the submission that produces the
   rejection. Making it true would mean moving the emission after both submits,
   which shifts every following `seq` and moves all three golden hashes, in
   order to encode an outcome the journal already carries once. The sentence was
   the bug, not the code.

   Consequence for AC-P9, which is why this matters: "two sided on at least
   95 % of ticks" is a claim about the **book**, not about `MMQuoted`, so it is
   measured from `MarkToMarket.best_bid` / `.best_ask` (the book at the close of
   the tick) and cross checked by asserting zero
   `OrderRejected(agent_id="MM")`. Rejection is the only silent way AC-P9 can
   fail, which is why `mm_initial_cash_cents` defaults to ten times an agent's
   cash (decision 16) and why
   `test_market_maker.py::test_guaranteed_liquidity_with_mute_agents` asserts
   that zero over the whole match.
10. The fairness shuffle, exactly as written in section 3.4: shuffle the
    **full** seat list `sorted_ids(state.ranked_agent_ids())`, then drop the
    frozen agents from the result. Never shuffle the already filtered list.

    ```python
    order = [a
             for a in shuffle_seeded(
                 rng.fresh_substream(f"runner.tick_shuffle.{tick}"),
                 sorted_ids(state.ranked_agent_ids()))
             if a not in state.frozen_agent_ids]
    ```

11. For each agent in that order, apply **its whole action block contiguously**,
    in the exact order of `action.orders`. "Atomic" means *not interleaved with
    another agent*, not all-or-nothing: each intent is independently accepted or
    rejected. Per intent:
    * `op == "cancel"` -> `exchange.cancel(...)`;
    * `op == "place"` -> `exchange.submit(...)`, which emits, in this order:
      1. `OrderPlaced`;
      2. for each resting order of the same agent that the incoming order would
         have crossed, in `priority_key` order: one
         `OrderCancelled(reason=CancelReason.STP, released_cents=...)`
         immediately followed by one `STPCancelled(cancel_seq=<seq of that
         OrderCancelled>)`. The resting order leaves the book through the
         `OrderCancelled`, which is the single event carrying the released
         collateral (section 4.5). `STPCancelled` carries no money field, so a
         projection summing releases cannot credit the same collateral twice;
      3. one `TradeExecuted` per fill, in fill order;
      4. for an `IOC` residual, `OrderCancelled(reason=IOC_RESIDUAL)`.

    The same rule applies to the freeze path: `AgentFrozen.cancelled_order_ids`
    is a cross reference, and the money moves in the `OrderCancelled` events
    emitted just before it.

### P4 Close (pure)

12. For each open market ascending: `emit(MarkToMarket)` and push `ref_price`
    into `ref_history` (bounded to `config.ref_history_len`).
13. For each account in canonical order: `emit(PositionSnapshot)`.
14. Bankruptcy check (FR-5.5.5). The PRD's trigger is "cash **et collateral
    libres** epuises", that is *free cash exhausted*, and that is the predicate
    used here. A ranked, non frozen agent is frozen when either branch holds,
    evaluated in ascending `agent_id`:

    ```
    free_cash_cents(a) <= config.bankruptcy_free_cash_floor_cents
        and accounts.resting_order_count(a) == 0
    or  equity_cents(a) <= config.bankruptcy_equity_floor_cents
    ```

    Both floors default to `0`. The first branch is the PRD rule and is the one
    that actually fires: with zero free cash the agent can neither place a buy
    (needs `price * qty`) nor a sell (needs `(100 - price) * qty`, no netting,
    decision 4), so it cannot act at all; the resting order clause is there
    because an agent that still has orders on the book can free collateral by
    cancelling them next tick and is therefore not stuck. The second branch is
    a defensive tripwire only: given I4 and I9, `equity >= free_cash >= 0`
    always, so an equity floor of `0` is unreachable on its own. Shipping only
    the equity branch, as an earlier draft of this document did, made
    `AgentFrozen`, `CancelReason.AGENT_FROZEN`, `active_agent_ids()` and
    decisions 9 and 13 dead code.

    On a freeze: `exchange.cancel_all(tick=tick, agent_id=a,
    reason=CancelReason.AGENT_FROZEN)`, which emits one `OrderCancelled` per
    resting order in `priority_key` order, markets ascending (those events
    carry the released collateral, section 4.5), then `accounts.freeze(a)`,
    then `emit(AgentFrozen)` with `cancelled_order_ids` set to exactly what
    `cancel_all` returned. The runner never emits `OrderCancelled` itself. A freeze at tick `t` takes effect
    from tick `t + 1`. A freeze is permanent.
    Tests: `test_accounts.py::test_freeze_triggers_when_free_cash_exhausted`
    and `test_match_runner.py::test_bankruptcy_freezes_agent`, both of which
    must reach the state through real order flow, never by forging an
    `AccountState`.
15. `accounts.check_invariants(ref_prices=state.ref_prices(), book_view=exchange.book_view())`
    -> raises `InvariantViolationError` on any breach of section 6. Both
    arguments are keyword-only and both are **required**. This runs on
    **every** tick, not only in tests.
16. `journal.flush()`.

### Finalisation

17. Finalisation runs at the **virtual tick `T + 1`** (section 5.0): every event
    it emits carries `tick = config.ticks_total + 1`, which keeps section 4.4's
    "events grouped by ascending tick" true and makes finalisation events
    trivially separable in a projection. There is no P1..P4 at `T + 1`, no
    observation and no gateway call.

    Every market still `OPEN` is resolved there, through the same single
    `oracle.resolve(tick=T+1, ...)` call as P1 step 4, in ascending
    `market_id`. This is the **normal** path, not a defensive one: with the PRD
    default every market has `resolution_tick == T`, so every match settles
    here. `test_e2e_scripted_match.py::test_default_scenario_settles_at_finalisation`
    asserts it.

    Finalisation emits **no** `NewsPublished` and makes **no** `mm.note_news`
    call: sub-step d and step 5b exist so that agents and the market maker
    learn an outcome in time to act on it, and at `T + 1` there is no P2 and
    no P3 for anybody to act in. `Oracle.resolve` behaves identically; only the
    runner's two extra steps are skipped.

    `CancelReason.MATCH_ENDED` is **not** used on this path: every market is
    resolved, so every resting order leaves through
    `CancelReason.MARKET_RESOLVED`. It is reserved for an aborted match, where
    `MatchEnded.reason` is not `"completed"` and open markets are never
    resolved.
18. `accounts.check_final_invariant()` (I11), then ranking is computed from
    cash after settlement, never from MTM (FR-5.5.4). `pnl_pct_bps` uses
    `bps_ratio`, never `//` (section 2.1). Ties share the lowest rank and are
    broken for display only by ascending `agent_id`.
19. `emit(MatchEnded)`, `journal.close()`, write `meta.json`.

---

## 6. Accounting invariants (FR-5.5.3)

### 6.1 Collateral formulas (FR-5.5.1, FR-5.5.2)

Per order, computed on the **unfilled** quantity, released on cancel,
transformed on execution:

```
collateral(order) = price * remaining_qty            if side == buy
                  = (100 - price) * remaining_qty     if side == sell
```

Per market position, because a short has already received `price * qty` in cash:

```
collateral(position) = 100 * max(0, -net_qty)
```

Per account, with no cross market netting (FR-5.5.2):

```
reserved_cents(a) = sum over resting orders of collateral(order)
                  + sum over markets of collateral(position)
free_cash_cents(a) = cash_cents(a) - reserved_cents(a)
```

Pre-trade check for a submitted order, evaluated **before** matching, against the
order's own limit price (a market order uses its band converted price):

```
accept  iff  free_cash_cents(a) >= collateral(order) + max_taker_fee_cents(fee_bps, qty)
```

where `max_taker_fee_cents(bps, qty) = (bps * 100 * qty) // 10_000`, the worst
case. Rejection is all-or-nothing for that single intent
(`RejectReason.INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL`); the rest of the agent's block still
runs. Any part of the collateral not consumed by the fills is released
immediately after matching.

**Fee arithmetic has exactly one owner.** `pxe.types.taker_fee_cents` and
`pxe.types.max_taker_fee_cents` are the only implementations.
`pxe.exchange.fees.taker_fee_for(config, *, price, qty)` and
`reserve_fee_for(config, *, qty)` are one-line delegations that read
`config.taker_fee_bps` and call them; they may not re-derive, re-round or
re-clamp anything. Two roundings of the same fee is how a closed system starts
creating money.

**The fee is per fill, and `MatchPlan.fee_cents` is informational.** The fee
charged and journalled is `taker_fee_cents(config.taker_fee_bps, fill.price,
fill.qty)`, computed once per fill and carried by that fill's
`TradeExecuted.taker_fee_cents`. `MatchPlan.fee_cents` (A05) is defined as the
**sum of those per fill fees** and is a convenience for the planner; nothing
charges it. The distinction is not cosmetic: `taker_fee_cents` floors, so for a
marketable order walking two price levels `floor(f1) + floor(f2)` differs from
`floor(f1 + f2)` by up to a cent, and charging the aggregate while journalling
the parts breaks I5 (`maker_delta + taker_delta + taker_fee == 0` per trade) on
exactly the trades a fuzz test is least likely to produce.

**STP release versus the solvency check: the order is normative.** The
pre-trade check above is evaluated against `free_cash_cents(a)` **before** any
STP cancellation is applied. Collateral released by an STP cancellation is
never available to the order that caused it. Concretely: an agent resting a
sell of 100 at 60 (reserved `40 * 100 = 4000`) that then sends a buy of 100 at
61 (needing `61 * 100 = 6100`) is rejected for `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` when
it has under 6100 free, even though the STP cancel the same submission triggers
would have freed 4000 first. The other reading (scan the book for self crosses,
cancel, then check) is equally natural and produces a different journal from
the same seed, which is why this sentence exists.
`tests/test_exchange_stp.py::test_stp_release_is_not_available_to_the_incoming_order`
pins it.

### 6.2 The checkable predicates

Every predicate below has one owner, one call site and arguments sufficient to
evaluate it. Three of them are event-level facts, not state facts, and cannot
be checked from a snapshot; pretending otherwise (an earlier draft claimed one
method "asserts all of these") silently reduced the per tick check to a subset
and let `test_invariants.py` pass vacuously, which section 10 forbids.

| Id | Predicate | Why | Checked by | Checked when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I1** | for every market `m`: `sum over all accounts of position_qty(a, m) == 0` | closed system, FR-5.5.3 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I2** | `sum over all accounts of cash_cents(a) == len(agent_ids) * config.initial_cash_cents + config.mm_initial_cash_cents`, where `agent_ids` is the seat list the book was built with | cash is conserved, FR-5.5.3 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I3** | for every account: `reserved_cents(a)` equals the formula of 6.1 recomputed from `book_view` and the positions | no drift between the ledger and the book | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I4** | for every account: `0 <= reserved_cents(a) <= cash_cents(a)` and `free_cash_cents(a) >= 0` | no negative balance, FR-5.5.5 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I5** | for every trade: `maker_cash_delta + taker_cash_delta + taker_fee == 0` and the fee lands in `FEES` | executions are zero sum, FR-5.5.3 | `AccountBook.check_trade_invariant` | inside `apply_trade`, per trade |
| **I6** | for every settlement group: `sum of cash_delta_cents over accounts == 0`, the `FEES` line included | settlements are zero sum, FR-5.5.3 | `AccountBook.check_settlement_invariant` | inside `apply_settlement`, per group |
| **I7** | for every market: `sum of positive positions == -sum of negative positions` | algebraically **equivalent** to I1, kept as a second spelling and not as an independent check: see the note below the table | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I8** | for every resting order in `book_view`: `1 <= remaining_qty <= qty`, `1 <= price <= 99`, status is resting, and the owner's per market resting count is `<= config.max_active_orders_per_market` | FR-5.4.2, FR-5.4.5 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I9** | for every account: `cash_cents(a) >= 0` | FR-5.5.5 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I10** | for every market: `abs(position_qty(MM, m)) <= config.mm.inventory_max` | FR-5.8.3 | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |
| **I11** | after the final settlement: every position is flat, so `equity_cents == cash_cents` for everyone | ranking is settled, not marked, FR-5.5.4 | `AccountBook.check_final_invariant` | finalisation step 18 |
| **I12** | for every market `m`: `sum over all accounts of cost_basis_cents(a, m) == 0` | a cancellation unwind (FR-5.4.5) is only zero sum if cost basis is, and cost basis is the only thing that makes an unwind computable | `AccountBook.check_invariants` | every P4 |

How the missing data reaches the checker: `AccountBook.__init__` takes the whole
`MatchConfig`, so `initial_cash_cents`, `mm_initial_cash_cents`,
`max_active_orders_per_market` and `mm.inventory_max` are all in hand (I2, I8,
I10). The market maker's inventory is just `position(MM_ACCOUNT_ID, m).qty`,
which the book already holds, so no extra argument is needed. `book_view` is
supplied by the caller and is **required**, never `None`: an optional book view
means I3 and I8 silently do not run.

**I2's left hand side is the seat count the book holds, not a config field.**
`AccountBook.__init__` receives `agent_ids`, not `n_agents`, and the two can
disagree (a caller that seats five agents under a `MatchConfig(n_agents=6)`).
The normative reading is the one the book can actually evaluate: the number of
ranked seats it was built with, exposed as
`AccountBook.initial_total_cash_cents()`. `MatchRunner` builds the book from
`[s.agent_id for s in agents if s.ranked]`, so the two agree in every engine
path; a disagreement is a caller bug that I2 is not the right place to catch.

**I7 is not an independent check, and this document used to claim it was.**
`sum of positive positions == -sum of negative positions` is algebraically the
same statement as `sum of all positions == 0`, so no reachable state breaches I7
without breaching I1 first, and the "independent check" wording promised a
second opinion that cannot exist. It is kept as a second **spelling**, because
it is the form a reader checks a settlement group against by hand, and
`test_invariants.py` calls the I7 helper directly so the helper is still proven
to have teeth rather than being dead code behind I1.

`mm_initial_cash_cents` comes from `MatchConfig` and from nowhere else. It
defaults to ten times an agent's cash so the market maker is never the binding
liquidity constraint, `__post_init__` enforces
`mm_initial_cash_cents >= initial_cash_cents`, and it is journalled inside
`MatchStarted.config`, so `replay_journal` and `MatchProjection` can both
re-evaluate I2 and recompute `mm_pnl_cents` from the journal alone. The `FEES`
account starts at 0 and is included in the I2 sum.

### 6.3 Property tests

`tests/test_invariants.py` (A06) drives a hypothesis strategy generating random
but *valid* order flows against `Exchange` + `AccountBook` and asserts I1..I12
after every operation. It is marked `property` and `slow`. Anti-vacuous rule
(section 10) applies with teeth here: the test asserts that at least one trade,
one cancellation and one settlement actually occurred in the generated flow
before it claims the invariants held.

### 6.4 Market cancellation restores the cash (FR-5.4.5)

FR-5.4.5 is literal: "l'annulation denoue toutes les executions et restitue le
cash". `apply_settlement(market_id=..., outcome=None, mode="unwind")` therefore
produces, in canonical account order:

* **one line per account** holding a position, a cost basis **or a paid taker
  fee** on that market. Its `cash_delta_cents` is the account's whole refund,
  folded into that single line:

  ```
  cash_delta_cents = cost_basis_cents(a, m) + fees_paid_cents(a, m)
  ```

  with `released_collateral_cents` covering the position leg;
* **one final `FEES` line** with
  `cash_delta_cents = -sum over accounts of fees_paid_cents(a, m)`, which is the
  vault giving back every taker fee it collected on that market. Without it the
  unwind is not integral: an agent that traded and was then cancelled would be
  strictly poorer than before the market existed, by the fees it paid. With the
  default `taker_fee_bps = 0` the bug is invisible, which is exactly why it is
  written down.

Three things about that shape were ambiguous in an earlier draft and are now
fixed, because each one changes the number of `SettlementApplied` events and
therefore the journal:

1. **One line per account, not two.** "one final `FEES` line ... plus one line
   per account receiving its own refund" read either as a fee line per account
   on top of its basis line, or as one folded line each. It is **folded**: an
   account gets exactly one `SettlementApplied` per unwound market, carrying
   basis plus fee refund together.
2. **An account that is flat with no basis but paid a fee still gets a line.**
   The same paragraph used to list the accounts as those "holding a position or a
   cost basis", which omits an agent that opened and closed a position on that
   market and paid taker fees doing it. Omitting it leaves its fee unrefunded and
   the unwind non integral, contradicting the paragraph's own claim.
3. **The `FEES` line is emitted even when it is zero.** With the default
   `taker_fee_bps = 0` every unwind group carries a `FEES` line with
   `cash_delta_cents = 0`. It is emitted unconditionally so the group shape does
   not depend on a config value: a projection counting settlement lines expects
   `len(accounts_with_something) + 1` for an unwind, always. An unwind of a
   market no account ever touched produces **no** lines at all, not a lone zero
   `FEES` line, because there is no group to close.
