A **resolution** never emits a `FEES` line: fees are not refunded when a market
resolves normally.

`AccountBook` therefore tracks `fees_paid_cents` per `(account_id, market_id)`,
exposed as `fees_paid_cents(account_id, market_id)`. I6 is checked over the
whole group including the `FEES` lines.

---

## 7. Module map and public APIs

Only the names below are public. Anything else is private to its module and may
change. Every signature is literal: argument names are part of the contract
because most of them are keyword-only.

Dependency direction (a module may import only from modules to its left):

```
types/events/rng/errors  <-  journal  <-  world, info
                                      <-  exchange(book, matching) <- accounts
                                      <-  mm, oracle
                                      <-  runner  <-  agents, gateway
                                      <-  metrics <-  integrity
                                      <-  store   <-  tournament <- api, cli, evolve
```

**`store` does not import `tournament`.** The arrow used to read
`tournament <- store`, which was impossible to honour: `TournamentOrchestrator`
takes a `Store`, and `Store.save_task(task: MatchTask)` /
`save_ratings(records: Sequence[RatingRecord])` take tournament types, so each
package imported the other. The fix is that the four **pure data carriers**
crossing that boundary (`MatchTask`, `TournamentConfig`, `TournamentResult`,
`RatingRecord`) live in `pxe.types` (A01), not in `pxe.tournament`. Both sides
import leftward from `pxe.types` and the cycle disappears. `pxe.tournament`
keeps only behaviour (`TournamentOrchestrator`, `RatingService`,
`EliteArchive`, `HallOfFame`, `HeldoutBank`, the pairing functions and the
report builder). `pxe.evolve` imports both and is imported by neither.

A module may re-export a carrier for readability
(`from pxe.types import MatchTask`) but must never redefine one.

### 7.1 `pxe.types` (A01, done)

Enums: `Side`, `OrderType`, `TimeInForce`, `OrderStatus`, `MarketStatus`,
`Outcome`, `NewsImpact`, `SignalKind`, `InfoProfileKind`, `LiquidityProfileName`,
`AccountKind`, `AgentSource`, `RejectReason`, `CancelReason`, `IncidentKind`,
`TournamentFormat`.

Frozen dataclasses: `MarketSpec`, `BookLevel`, `BookSnapshot`, `Order`, `Trade`,
`Position`, `AccountState`, `MarketState`, `NewsItem`, `NewsPlanItem`, `Signal`,
`InfoProfile`, `PredictionIntent`, `OrderIntent`, `AgentAction`,
`PublicMessage`, `ObservationLimits`, `MarketObservation`, `Observation`,
`InventoryView`, `MMConfig`, `LiquidityProfile`, `MatchConfig`, `ScenarioSpec`,
`HarnessConfig`, `GatewayConfig`, `AgentSpec`, `MatchRanking`, `MatchResult`,
`Incident`, `RatingRecord`, `MatchTask`, `TournamentConfig`,
`TournamentResult`.

There is deliberately **no** `Prediction` dataclass. One concept, three names
(`types.Prediction`, `events.PredictionRecorded`, the projection row) was one
name too many: the event is the record, and the projection row is
`metrics.projection.PredictionRow` (section 7.17).

`MatchRanking` deliberately carries **no** `brier_ppm`. Brier is defined in
section 9 over a `MatchProjection` and computed by A16, which is strictly
downstream of the runner; putting it on the ranking would force
`pxe.runner` to import `pxe.metrics` against the dependency arrow, or to
reimplement the metric, which is two definitions of the headline calibration
number and exactly what AC-P4 forbids. Reports join `MatchRanking` and
`CalibrationMetrics` on `agent_id`.

Helpers:

```python
def round_half_up(value: float) -> int
def ppm_from_prob(p: float) -> int
def prob_from_ppm(ppm: int) -> float
def milli_from_float(value: float) -> int
def float_from_milli(value_milli: int) -> float
def bps_ratio(numerator_cents: int, denominator_cents: int) -> int
    """Signed ratio in basis points, half away from zero. The only legal way to
    compute pnl_pct_bps, max_drawdown_bps and any signed *_bps value."""
def clamp_price(price: int) -> int
def taker_fee_cents(fee_bps: int, price: int, qty: int) -> int
def max_taker_fee_cents(fee_bps: int, qty: int) -> int
def order_collateral_cents(side: Side, price: int, remaining_qty: int) -> int
def position_collateral_cents(net_qty: int) -> int
def brier_term_ppm(p_yes_ppm: int, outcome: Outcome) -> int
def make_order_id(seq: int) -> str
def make_trade_id(seq: int) -> str
def make_market_id(index: int) -> str
def make_agent_id(index: int) -> str
def make_news_id(tick: int, index: int) -> str
def make_signal_id(tick: int, agent_id: str, index: int) -> str
def make_incident_id(seq: int) -> str

    """Markets and ranked agents only. Raises InvalidConfigError on MM or FEES."""

    """The one implementation of the canonical account order (section 2.3):
    ranked agents ascending, then MM, then FEES."""

    """The one encoder of MatchStarted.config. Ints, strs and bools only."""
def config_from_journal_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> MatchConfig
    """Its inverse. Without it replay_journal (FR-5.1.3) cannot be written."""
def market_spec_to_dict(spec: MarketSpec) -> dict[str, Any]
    """The one encoder of one MatchStarted.markets entry."""

def scenario_to_journal_dict(scenario: ScenarioSpec) -> dict[str, Any]
def scenario_from_journal_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> ScenarioSpec
def liquidity_profile(name: str | LiquidityProfileName) -> LiquidityProfile
```

Agent intent encoding. There are **two shapes and they differ**, so there are
two functions and not one; guessing which is which is how A09 and A13 end up
writing two mappings that disagree on a key name or on how `None` is encoded,
which moves the journal hash.

```python
def order_intent_to_dict(intent: OrderIntent) -> dict[str, Any]
    """Wire AND journal shape (identical for orders). Seven keys, always all
    seven, nulls kept. The key is "type", the spelling of
    schemas/action.v1.json, never "order_type"."""
def order_intent_from_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> OrderIntent
def prediction_intent_to_dict(intent: PredictionIntent) -> dict[str, Any]
    """JOURNAL shape: {"market_id", "p_yes_ppm"}. Float free."""
def prediction_intent_to_wire_dict(intent: PredictionIntent) -> dict[str, Any]
    """WIRE shape: {"market_id", "p_yes"}. Carries a float, never journalled."""
def action_to_payload(action: AgentAction) -> dict[str, Any]
    """schemas/action.v1.json shape. What ScriptedGateway puts in
    AgentReply.raw so scripted and LLM agents enter validate_action through the
    same door (section 7.16)."""
def action_to_journal_dict(action: AgentAction, *, n_rejected: int = 0) -> dict[str, Any]
    """The one producer of the AgentActionReceived payload. Float free."""
```

Harness identity, error mapping and incident detail:

```python
def harness_config_hash(harness: HarnessConfig) -> str
    """blake2b-256 over the canonical JSON of (harness_id, version, kind,
    model, system_prompt, params), 16 hex chars. HarnessConfig.__post_init__
    fills config_hash with it when empty, so a journal never carries ""."""
def harness_key(harness: HarnessConfig) -> str
    """f"{harness_id}@{version}+{config_hash[:8]}" (section 2.2). The one
    producer; ratings, elites, Halls of Fame and MatchTask all consume it."""
def reject_reason_of(error: PxeError) -> RejectReason
    """Section 2.4. Raises InvalidConfigError on an unmapped code."""
def incident_detail_to_dict(detail: Sequence[tuple[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any]
def incident_detail_from_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> tuple[tuple[str, Any], ...]
    """The only crossing between types.Incident.detail (ordered pairs, the
    shape a detector builds) and IncidentRaised.detail (a mapping, which
    canonical_json sorts and therefore makes byte stable). A18 writes no
    conversion of its own."""
```

Constants: `ENGINE_VERSION`, `OBS_VERSION`, `ACTION_VERSION`, `PRICE_MIN`,
`PRICE_MAX`, `PAYOUT_YES_CENTS`, `PAYOUT_NO_CENTS`, `PPM_ONE`, `MILLI_ONE`,

Compiled identifier patterns, also public because the schemas mirror them and

### 7.2 `pxe.events` (A01, done)

```python
class EventType(StrEnum): ...
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Event:
    seq: int
    match_id: str
    tick: int
    TYPE: ClassVar[EventType]
    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]
    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Event

def canonical_json(payload: Any) -> str
def stable_json(payload: Any) -> str
def payload_hash(payload: Any) -> str
def event_from_dict(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Event
def event_to_line(event: Event) -> str
def event_from_line(line: str) -> Event
def journal_hash(events: Iterable[Event]) -> str
def journal_hash_from_lines(lines: Iterable[str]) -> str
    """Raises NonCanonicalValueError on any line containing a carriage return."""
```

Concrete events: the 24 classes listed in section 4.5.

### 7.3 `pxe.rng` (A01, done)

```python
class RngTree:
    def __init__(self, root_seed: int, namespace: str = "") -> None
    @property
    def root_seed(self) -> int
    @property
    def namespace(self) -> str
    def substream(self, name: str) -> random.Random
    def fresh_substream(self, name: str) -> random.Random
    def child(self, namespace: str) -> RngTree
    def seed_for(self, name: str) -> int

def derive_seed(root_seed: int, name: str) -> int
def is_registered_substream(name: str) -> bool
def shuffle_seeded(rng: random.Random, items: Sequence[T]) -> list[T]
def choice(rng: random.Random, items: Sequence[T]) -> T
def choices_weighted(rng, items: Sequence[T], weights: Sequence[float], k: int = 1) -> list[T]
def sample_without_replacement(rng, items: Sequence[T], k: int) -> list[T]
def random_unit(rng) -> float                 # [0, 1) on the 2**-53 grid
def random_open_unit(rng) -> float            # (0, 1), safe for log and quantiles
def normal(rng, mu: float = 0.0, sigma: float = 1.0) -> float
def lognormal(rng, mu: float = 0.0, sigma: float = 1.0) -> float
def bernoulli(rng, p: float) -> bool
def randint(rng, low: int, high: int) -> int
def uniform(rng, low: float = 0.0, high: float = 1.0) -> float
def beta(rng, alpha: float, beta_param: float) -> float
def stable_key(*parts: Any) -> str
def ordered(items: Iterable[T]) -> list[T]
SUBSTREAMS: frozenset[str]
SUBSTREAM_PREFIXES: frozenset[str]
RNG_ALGORITHM_VERSION: str
```

Every one of these consumes the generator through `getrandbits` only, and every
transcendental they need (`log`, `exp`, the normal quantile) is implemented
inside `pxe.rng` in fixed order IEEE-754 arithmetic. No engine module may call
`rng.random()`, `rng.shuffle(...)`, `rng.gauss(...)`, `rng.betavariate(...)`,
`rng.choice(...)` or `rng.randrange(...)` directly (section 3.2). `pxe.rng`
exports exactly the names in `__all__`; with `no_implicit_reexport` on, a name
missing there is a type error at the import site.

### 7.4 `pxe.journal` (A02)

```python
E = TypeVar("E", bound=Event)

class Journal:
    """Append only event log. The only writer of journal.jsonl.

    The file is opened exactly as
    open(path, "w", encoding=JOURNAL_ENCODING, newline=JOURNAL_NEWLINE)
    (that is encoding="utf-8", newline="\n"). Never the text mode default:
    on Windows it turns "\n" into "\r\n" and the bytes on disk stop matching
    journal_hash(). Reads use newline="\n" too, so a stray "\r" is not
    swallowed by universal newline translation.
    """
    def __init__(self, match_id: str, path: Path | None = None, *, buffer_size: int = 256) -> None
    @property
    def match_id(self) -> str
    @property
    def path(self) -> Path | None
    @property
    def next_seq(self) -> int
    @property
    def events(self) -> tuple[Event, ...]
    def emit(self, event_cls: type[E], *, tick: int, **payload: Any) -> E
        """Build the event with the next seq and this match_id, append, return it."""
    def append(self, event: Event) -> Event
        """Append a pre-built event. Raises JournalHashMismatchError on a seq gap."""
    def flush(self) -> None
    def close(self) -> None
    def hash(self) -> str
    def __enter__(self) -> Journal
    def __exit__(self, *exc: Any) -> None

def read_journal(path: Path) -> tuple[Event, ...]
    """Opens with encoding=JOURNAL_ENCODING, newline=JOURNAL_NEWLINE."""
def write_journal(path: Path, events: Sequence[Event]) -> str
    """Write and return the journal hash. Same open() arguments as Journal."""
def verify_journal(events: Sequence[Event]) -> None
    """Raise JournalHashMismatchError if seq, tick ordering or envelope is broken."""
def iter_ticks(events: Sequence[Event]) -> Iterator[tuple[int, tuple[Event, ...]]]
def filter_events(events: Sequence[Event], *types: type[Event]) -> tuple[Event, ...]
```

### 7.5 `pxe.world` (A03)

```python
# pxe/world/latent.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LatentProcess:
    keys: tuple[str, ...]
    values_milli: tuple[tuple[int, ...], ...]   # [key_index][tick], tick 0..T
    def value_milli(self, key: str, tick: int) -> int
    def probability_ppm(self, key: str, tick: int) -> int
    def final_probability_ppm(self, key: str) -> int

# pxe/world/generator.py
#
# World is DECLARED in pxe/world/templates/base.py and re-exported here, inside
# generator.py's __all__ (ruling R8): a template must construct a World and this
# module must import the templates for its registry, which is a cycle, and ruff
# PLC0415 closes the lazy-import escape. `from pxe.world.generator import World`
# is unchanged and legal under no_implicit_reexport.
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class World:
    scenario: ScenarioSpec
    latent: LatentProcess
    outcomes: tuple[tuple[str, Outcome], ...]         # sorted by market_id
    news_plan: tuple[NewsPlanItem, ...]      # pxe.types, sorted by (tick, market_ids)
    def outcome(self, market_id: str) -> Outcome
    def market_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]

class WorldTemplate(Protocol):
    template_id: str
    template_version: str
    default_ticks: int
    default_markets: int              # declared metadata; generate_world never reads it (R9)
    min_markets: int                  # >= 2. election 2, harvest 2, league 3 (R12)
    max_markets: int                  # <= 8. election 8, harvest 6, league 8 (R12)
    def build(self, *, seed: int, ticks_total: int, n_markets: int, rng: RngTree) -> World: ...

def list_templates() -> tuple[str, ...]
def get_template(template_id: str) -> WorldTemplate
def generate_world(
    *,
    template_id: str,
    seed: int,
    ticks_total: int = 48,
    n_markets: int = 5,
    liquidity: LiquidityProfileName = LiquidityProfileName.STANDARD,
    talking_mode: bool = False,
    held_out: bool = False,
) -> World
def outcome_frequency(template_id: str, *, draws: int = 10_000, base_seed: int = 0,
                      n_markets: int = 5) -> dict[str, int]
    """FR-5.2.2: returns YES frequency in ppm per market, for the statistical test.

    Draw i uses seed base_seed + i, for i in 0..draws - 1. That sequence is
    contractual (ruling R11), not an implementation detail: the function returns
    frequencies only, so a caller checking convergence has to reproduce the same
    seeds to compute the reference latent probabilities, and `pxe world stats`
    (section 7.22) depends on it."""
```

#### The number of markets is a parameter, in three places (FR-5.2.1)

PRD section 5.7 lists "Marches par monde | 5 | 2-8", which makes the market
count a tunable difficulty axis and not a template constant. It therefore
appears, with the same value, in exactly three places, and the runner refuses
to start when they disagree (section 2.6):

1. `MatchConfig.n_markets: int = 5`, validated `2 <= n_markets <= 8` in
   `__post_init__`. This is the authoritative copy and it is journalled inside
   `MatchStarted.config`, so a projection knows the sweep point of every run.
2. `generate_world(..., n_markets=...)`, forwarded verbatim to
   `WorldTemplate.build(..., n_markets=...)`. A template raises
   `InvalidConfigError` when the request is outside its own
   `[min_markets, max_markets]`, and a silent clamp would produce a world whose
   market count disagrees with the journalled config. The shipped ranges are
   **`election` 2..8, `harvest` 2..6, `league` 3..8** (ruling R12): FR-5.2.1's
   "2 to 8" is the range of the feature, not a promise that every template spans
   it, since a league table cannot be expressed with two teams. A consumer
   sweeping market counts reads `min_markets` / `max_markets` off the template.
3. `len(ScenarioSpec.markets)`, validated `2 <= len(markets) <= 8` in
   `ScenarioSpec.__post_init__` as it already is.

`MatchRunner.__init__` asserts `config.n_markets == len(world.scenario.markets)`
(section 2.6, fifth equality). `test_world_gen.py::test_market_count_is_a_parameter`
sweeps `n_markets` over 2..8 on all three templates and asserts, for each
template, success with exactly that many markets and at least one correlated
pair **inside** that template's declared range and `InvalidConfigError`
**outside** it; `test_world_gen.py::test_market_count_out_of_range_raises`
pins 1 and 9.

Templates: `election` (`ElectionTemplate`), `harvest` (`HarvestTemplate`),
`league` (`LeagueTemplate`), each exposing `template_id`, `template_version`.

`news_plan` is a tuple of `pxe.types.NewsPlanItem`, a named frozen dataclass
`(tick: int, market_ids: tuple[str, ...], impact: NewsImpact, is_noise: bool)`,
and **not** a positional 4-tuple carrying a single `market_id`. Two reasons,
both load bearing. First, the whole point of FR-5.2.1 is correlated markets: a
news item that can only name one market can never carry the coherence signal
the PRD asks agents to arbitrage, and `NewsItem.market_ids` (which the item
becomes) is already a tuple. Second, the plan crosses the A03 to A04 boundary,
and a positional tuple across a workstream boundary has no field names for a
reviewer to check.

### 7.6 `pxe.info` (A04)

```python
# pxe/info/noise.py
# Published because the Bayesian baseline needs the inverse to weight a signal
# (FR-5.3.1): precision_ppm = s / (s + sigma) with s = LATENT_SCALE_MILLI, and
# with only the forward direction available the information channel is unusable
# by the agent it exists for. See ruling R17.
def precision_ppm_for_sigma(sigma_milli: int) -> int
def sigma_milli_for_precision(precision_ppm: int) -> int
def threshold_milli(observed_milli: int) -> int
    """A THRESHOLD signal value: magnitude is a probability level in
    thousandths, sign is the direction of the bound (ruling R15)."""

#: The information engine's own share of the observation news budget. The schema
#: caps `news` at 20 and the runner adds one item per market resolved and
#: cancelled into the same array, so 12 leaves 8 for a maximal 8 market world
#: (ruling R18).
NEWS_MAX_PER_TICK = 12

# pxe/info/profiles.py
def build_profile(kind: InfoProfileKind, *, market_ids: Sequence[str], focus_market_id: str | None = None) -> InfoProfile
    """Raises InvalidConfigError when focus_market_id is passed for a kind that
    is not a specialist (ruling R20)."""
def default_profile_kinds(n_agents: int) -> tuple[InfoProfileKind, ...]

# pxe/info/engine.py
class InfoEngine:
    def __init__(self, *, world: World, profiles: Mapping[str, InfoProfile], rng: RngTree, ticks_total: int) -> None
    def news_for_tick(self, tick: int) -> tuple[NewsItem, ...]
        """Deterministic, in publication order, ids from make_news_id."""
    def signals_for_tick(self, tick: int) -> tuple[Signal, ...]
        """Sorted by (canonical seat rank, signal_id), that is sorted_ids order
        on the agent id and then the signal id. NOT lexicographic on agent_id:
        the two disagree at ten seats (A10 < A2) and section 2.3 is the single
        statement of the ordering. Honours delay_ticks and focus markets."""
    def resolution_news(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, outcome: Outcome, index: int) -> NewsItem
        """Impact is always HIGH; origin is set by the runner (P1 step 4d)."""
    def cancellation_news(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, reason: str, index: int) -> NewsItem
        """Impact is always HIGH; origin is set by the runner (P1 step 5).
        Without it NewsPublished.origin == "cancellation" and NewsDto.origin's
        third value have no producer."""
    def high_impact_market_ids(self, tick: int) -> tuple[str, ...]
```

All three tick accessors raise `InvalidConfigError` for `tick < 1` and return
empty for `tick > ticks_total` (ruling R19): tick 0 is `MatchStarted` and has no
diffusion, and a tick past the horizon is finalisation, which publishes no news.

`InfoEngine` is pure: two instances built from the same world, profiles and seed
answer identically for any tick, in any call order.

`profiles` is a `Mapping` and `signals_for_tick` loops over agents, so section
2.3 applies literally: **iterate `sorted_ids(profiles.keys())`, never
`profiles.items()`**. A caller that builds the mapping in seat order and a
caller that builds it in harness order would otherwise draw the per agent
signals in a different order out of the same substream and produce two
different journals from one seed.

### 7.7 `pxe.exchange.book` and `pxe.exchange.matching` (A05)

```python
# book.py
class OrderBook:
    def __init__(self, market_id: str) -> None
    @property
    def market_id(self) -> str
    def best_bid(self) -> int | None
    def best_ask(self) -> int | None
    def mid_price(self) -> int | None            # round_half_up((bid + ask) / 2), None if one sided
    def last_price(self) -> int | None
    def set_last_price(self, price: int) -> None
    def depth(self, side: Side, levels: int = DEPTH_LEVELS) -> tuple[BookLevel, ...]
    def total_qty(self, side: Side) -> int
    def resting_orders(self) -> tuple[Order, ...]      # bids in priority order, then asks
    def orders_of(self, agent_id: str) -> tuple[Order, ...]
    def count_of(self, agent_id: str) -> int
    def get(self, order_id: str) -> Order | None
    def add(self, order: Order) -> None
    def remove(self, order_id: str) -> Order
    def reduce(self, order_id: str, qty: int) -> Order
    def snapshot(self, ref_price: int, levels: int = DEPTH_LEVELS) -> BookSnapshot
    def is_empty(self) -> bool

# matching.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Fill:
    maker_order_id: str
    maker_agent_id: str
    maker_side: Side
    price: int
    qty: int

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MatchPlan:
    fills: tuple[Fill, ...]
    stp_order_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    residual_qty: int
    notional_cents: int
    fee_cents: int      # sum of the per fill fees, informational (section 6.1)

def plan_match(book: OrderBook, *, agent_id: str, side: Side, limit_price: int, qty: int, fee_bps: int) -> MatchPlan
    """Pure. Walks price-time priority, marks own resting orders for STP cancellation
    (FR-5.4.4) and keeps walking. Never mutates the book."""
def reference_price(book: OrderBook, prior_price: int) -> tuple[int, str]
    """FR-5.4.6. Returns (price, source) with source in {"mid", "last", "prior"}."""
def band_limit_price(ref_price: int, side: Side, band_cents: int) -> int
    """FR-5.4.3. buy -> clamp(ref + band), sell -> clamp(ref - band)."""
```

### 7.8 `pxe.exchange.exchange` (A05)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OrderResult:
    accepted: bool
    order_id: str | None
    filled_qty: int
    resting_qty: int
    reason: RejectReason | None
    trades: tuple[Trade, ...]

class Exchange:
    """CLOB facade. Owns order and trade counters, emits every order/trade event."""
    def __init__(self, *, config: MatchConfig, scenario: ScenarioSpec, accounts: AccountBook, journal: Journal) -> None
    def open_market_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]
    def status(self, market_id: str) -> MarketStatus
    def book(self, market_id: str) -> OrderBook
    def reference_price(self, market_id: str) -> tuple[int, str]
    def market_state(self, market_id: str) -> MarketState
    def snapshot(self, market_id: str) -> BookSnapshot
    def book_view(self) -> Mapping[str, tuple[Order, ...]]
        """Every resting order, keyed by market_id, each tuple in priority_key
        order. This is the argument P4 step 15 hands to
        AccountBook.check_invariants, which is how I3 and I8 become checkable."""
    def submit(self, *, tick: int, agent_id: str, intent: OrderIntent, item_index: int) -> OrderResult
    def cancel(self, *, tick: int, agent_id: str, order_id: str, reason: CancelReason, item_index: int = -1) -> OrderResult
    def cancel_all(self, *, tick: int, reason: CancelReason, agent_id: str | None = None, market_id: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, ...]
    def close_market(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, status: MarketStatus) -> None
    def tick_volume(self, market_id: str) -> int
    def begin_tick(self, tick: int) -> None      # resets per tick counters
    def push_ref_history(self, market_id: str, ref_price: int) -> None
    def ref_history(self, market_id: str) -> tuple[int, ...]
```

Call sites that are part of the contract, because a method with no caller is a
method implemented wrong:

* `begin_tick(tick)` is called by the runner as **P1 step 0**, before
  `TickStarted`. It is the only thing that resets `tick_volume`, which P4 step
  12 puts in `MarkToMarket.tick_volume_qty`.
* `close_market(...)` is called by `Oracle.resolve` and `Oracle.cancel_market`
  only (section 7.11). The runner never calls it, and neither does anything
  else: market status transitions have one owner.
* `cancel_all(...)` has exactly three callers and no more: `Oracle`
  (resolution and cancellation, `market_id=` form), `ReferenceMarketMaker.requote`
  (`agent_id=MM, market_id=` form, P3 step 9) and the runner's freeze path
  (`agent_id=` form, P4 step 14). It returns the cancelled order ids in
  emission order, which is what `AgentFrozen.cancelled_order_ids` carries.
* `book_view()` is called by the runner at P4 step 15 to feed
  `AccountBook.check_invariants`. Every consumer iterates it through
  `sorted_ids(view.keys())` (section 2.3).

### 7.9 `pxe.exchange.accounts` and `pxe.exchange.fees` (A06)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SettlementLine:
    account_id: str
    market_id: str
    mode: str                    # "resolution" | "unwind"
    position_qty: int
    cash_delta_cents: int
    cash_before_cents: int
    cash_after_cents: int
    released_collateral_cents: int

class AccountBook:
    def __init__(self, *, agent_ids: Sequence[str], market_ids: Sequence[str],
                 config: MatchConfig) -> None
        """Takes the whole config, not a handful of scalars: the invariants it
        owns need initial_cash_cents, mm_initial_cash_cents, taker_fee_bps,
        max_active_orders_per_market and mm.inventory_max, and passing them
        one by one is how I8 and I10 silently stopped being checkable."""
    def account_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]        # sorted_account_ids(...), section 2.3
    def ranked_agent_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]
    def state(self, account_id: str) -> AccountState
    def states(self) -> tuple[AccountState, ...]
    def cash_cents(self, account_id: str) -> int
    def reserved_cents(self, account_id: str) -> int
    def free_cash_cents(self, account_id: str) -> int
    def position(self, account_id: str, market_id: str) -> Position
    def inventory_view(self, account_id: str) -> InventoryView
        """A frozen read only snapshot of one account: cash, reserved, free
        cash and the signed net position on every market of the match, sorted
        by market_id. This is what the market maker receives at P3 step 9
        (FR-5.8.3 is expressed on inventory, and mm has no accounts handle).
        Handing out the AccountBook itself instead would give a pure quoting
        component a mutation handle on the ledger and would make pxe.mm import
        pxe.exchange.accounts; InventoryView lives in pxe.types so neither
        imports the other."""
    def is_frozen(self, account_id: str) -> bool
    def freeze(self, account_id: str) -> None
    def can_afford(self, *, account_id: str, collateral_cents: int, fee_cents: int) -> bool
    def reserve_order(self, *, account_id: str, order: Order) -> int
    def release_order(self, *, account_id: str, order: Order, released_qty: int) -> int
    def resting_order_count(self, account_id: str) -> int
    def fees_paid_cents(self, account_id: str, market_id: str) -> int
        """Taker fees this account paid on this market. Needed by the FR-5.4.5
        unwind refund (section 6.4) and by PerformanceMetrics.fees_paid_cents."""
    def apply_trade(self, trade: Trade) -> tuple[int, int]
        """Returns (maker_cash_delta_cents, taker_cash_delta_cents). Routes the
        fee to FEES, accumulates fees_paid_cents, and checks I5 before
        returning."""
    def apply_settlement(self, *, market_id: str, outcome: Outcome | None, mode: str = "resolution") -> tuple[SettlementLine, ...]
        """outcome None means an unwind (FR-5.4.5, section 6.4). Lines in
        canonical account order, FEES last. Checks I6 before returning.
        Mutates the ledger only: it never emits an event (section 7.11 owns the
        emission loop)."""
    def equity_cents(self, account_id: str, ref_prices: Mapping[str, int]) -> int
    def total_cash_cents(self) -> int
    def initial_total_cash_cents(self) -> int
    def check_invariants(self, *, ref_prices: Mapping[str, int],
                         book_view: Mapping[str, Sequence[Order]]) -> None
        """The per tick state check. Both arguments are required, never None:
        an optional book view means I3 and I8 silently do not run. Raises
        InvariantViolationError naming the first breach among
        I1, I2, I3, I4, I7, I8, I9, I10, I12."""
    def check_trade_invariant(self, *, trade: Trade, maker_cash_delta_cents: int,
                              taker_cash_delta_cents: int, fee_cents: int) -> None
        """I5, at the moment the trade is applied."""
    def check_settlement_invariant(self, lines: Sequence[SettlementLine]) -> None
        """I6, over one settlement group."""
    def check_final_invariant(self) -> None
        """I11, at finalisation step 18."""

# fees.py: thin delegation only, no arithmetic of its own (section 6.1)
def taker_fee_for(config: MatchConfig, *, price: int, qty: int) -> int
    """return taker_fee_cents(config.taker_fee_bps, price, qty)"""
def reserve_fee_for(config: MatchConfig, *, qty: int) -> int
    """return max_taker_fee_cents(config.taker_fee_bps, qty)"""
```

### 7.10 `pxe.mm` (A07)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Quote:
    market_id: str
    ref_price: int
    anchor_price: int
    bid_price: int | None
    ask_price: int | None
    quote_qty: int
    effective_spread: int | None
    inventory_qty: int
    skew_cents: int
    widened: bool
    widen_until_tick: int

class ReferenceMarketMaker:
    ACCOUNT_ID: ClassVar[str] = MM_ACCOUNT_ID
    def __init__(self, *, config: MMConfig, market_ids: Sequence[str], rng: RngTree) -> None
    def note_news(self, *, tick: int, items: Sequence[NewsItem]) -> None
        """FR-5.8.4: a HIGH impact item sets widen_until_tick = tick + w for its markets.
        The market maker reads the impact tag only, never the content (FR-5.8.2)."""
    def is_widened(self, market_id: str, tick: int) -> bool
    def compute_quote(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, ref_price: int, inventory_qty: int) -> Quote
    def requote(self, *, tick: int, exchange: Exchange, inventory: InventoryView,
                journal: Journal) -> tuple[Quote, ...]
        """Cancel/replace every quote on every open market, ascending market_id.

        inventory is the snapshot of the MM account taken by the runner just
        before the call (AccountBook.inventory_view(MM_ACCOUNT_ID)); it is what
        feeds compute_quote's inventory_qty and the FR-5.8.3 cap. The market
        maker never tracks inventory itself: a second count drifts from the
        ledger and breaks I10.

        A quote submitted through Exchange.submit is subject to the section 6.1
        collateral check and can be rejected, which emits
        OrderRejected(agent_id="MM") and leaves that side of the already
        emitted MMQuoted null. See P3 step 9."""

# pxe/mm/profiles.py
def mm_config_for(name: str | LiquidityProfileName) -> MMConfig

# The T2.6 liquidity study (ruling R34). A journal-only reducer: pxe.mm may not
# import pxe.exchange.accounts (section 13) and Exchange.__init__ needs an
# AccountBook, so pxe.mm cannot run a match. render_* returns a string and the
# caller writes docs/MM_LIQUIDITY_COST.md (section 2.7 forbids the filesystem
# here).
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LiquidityCostRow: ...
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LiquidityStudy: ...
def liquidity_cost_row(events: Sequence[Event], *, profile: str) -> LiquidityCostRow
def build_liquidity_study(rows: Sequence[LiquidityCostRow]) -> LiquidityStudy
def render_liquidity_study(study: LiquidityStudy) -> str
```

Quoting arithmetic, normative:

There is one implementation of the half spread,
`MMConfig.half_spread_cents(*, widened: bool = False)`. It is a **method**, not
a property, on purpose: FR-5.8.4 multiplies the spread during the widening
window, and a property that could only see `base_spread_cents` was a trap that
silently produced a market maker that never widens.

```
half      = ceil(spread / 2) with spread = base_spread * (widen_multiplier if widened else 1)
skew      = round_half_up(-k * inventory_qty / I_max)          # FR-5.8.3, sign opposite to inventory
anchor    = clamp_price(ref_price + skew)
bid       = clamp_price(anchor - half)     suppressed if inventory_qty + q > I_max
ask       = clamp_price(anchor + half)     suppressed if inventory_qty - q < -I_max
```

A side is also suppressed when clamping would make `bid >= ask`. Suppressed sides
are journalled as `null` in `MMQuoted`.

### 7.11 `pxe.oracle` (A08)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ResolutionReport:
    market_id: str
    outcome: Outcome | None            # None for a cancellation
    payout_cents: int
    cancelled_order_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    settlements: tuple[SettlementLine, ...]

class Oracle:
    def __init__(self, *, world: World) -> None
    def due_market_ids(self, tick: int) -> tuple[str, ...]
        """Still-OPEN markets whose resolution_tick < tick, ascending
        (section 5.0). () at tick 1. At the virtual tick ticks_total + 1 this
        returns everything left, which is the normal finalisation path.

        "Still-OPEN" needs no exchange handle and there is none in the
        signature: `Oracle` is the only thing that ever closes a market
        (`close_market` has exactly one caller, section 7.8), so it knows
        which markets it has already resolved or cancelled from its own
        history. Reading a status off the exchange instead would let a
        second, disagreeing view of market state exist."""
    def due_cancellations(self, tick: int) -> tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]
        """(market_id, reason) pairs scheduled by ScenarioSpec.cancellations for
        exactly this tick, ascending market_id. A cancellation fires at tick c,
        not c + 1: it is a scripted intervention, not a maturity (FR-5.4.5)."""
    def outcome(self, market_id: str) -> Outcome
    def resolve(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, exchange: Exchange,
                accounts: AccountBook, journal: Journal) -> ResolutionReport
        """Performs P1 step 4 sub-steps a, b, c and e, in that order, and emits
        OrderCancelled (through Exchange), MarketResolved and, through
        settlement.settle, one SettlementApplied per line. It does NOT emit the
        resolution NewsPublished: that is sub-step d and belongs to the runner,
        which owns the info engine and the single note_news handover (P1 step
        5b)."""
    def cancel_market(self, *, tick: int, market_id: str, reason: str, exchange: Exchange,
                      accounts: AccountBook, journal: Journal) -> ResolutionReport
        """Same shape, emitting MarketCancelled and settling with
        mode="unwind" (section 6.4)."""

# pxe/oracle/settlement.py
def settle(*, tick: int, market_id: str, outcome: Outcome | None, accounts: AccountBook,
           journal: Journal, mode: str = "resolution") -> tuple[SettlementLine, ...]
    """The one and only emitter of SettlementApplied. Mutates the ledger through
    accounts.apply_settlement (which emits nothing) and emits one event per
    returned line, in canonical account order."""
```

### 7.12 `pxe.runner` (A09)

```python
# pxe/runner/state.py
@dataclass
class MatchState:
    config: MatchConfig
    scenario: ScenarioSpec
    match_id: str
    tick: int
    accounts: AccountBook
    exchange: Exchange
    outcomes: dict[str, Outcome]
    last_prediction_ppm: dict[tuple[str, str], int]   # (agent_id, market_id) -> ppm
    pending_messages: tuple[PublicMessage, ...]
    frozen_agent_ids: frozenset[str]
    def open_market_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]
    def ref_prices(self) -> dict[str, int]
    def ranked_agent_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]     # every seat, frozen included, ascending
    def active_agent_ids(self) -> tuple[str, ...]     # ranked, not frozen, ascending
    def resting_order_ids(self, agent_id: str) -> tuple[str, ...]
    # Every order id this account currently rests, over every market, in
    # priority_key order per market and markets ascending. Derived from
    # exchange.book_view(); it is the ONE argument P2 step 8b hands to
    # validate_action(resting_order_ids=...). AccountBook exposes only
    # resting_order_count, because the ids live in the book, not the ledger.
    # ranked_agent_ids() is the list the P3 shuffle consumes (section 3.4);
    # frozen agents are removed AFTER the shuffle, never before.
    # active_agent_ids() is for P1 step 6 and P2, where no RNG ordering is at
    # stake.

# pxe/runner/match_runner.py
class MatchRunner:
    def __init__(self, *, config: MatchConfig, world: World, agents: Sequence[AgentSpec],
                 gateway: AgentGateway, journal: Journal, match_id: str,
                 rng: RngTree, obs_path: Path | None = None) -> None
        """rng is built by the caller, not here (section 3.1): the world and the
        scripted agents are constructed before this object exists and already
        need their sub-trees.

        obs_path is where P1 step 6 appends runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl
        through write_observation (section 7.13); None writes no observation
        artefact. It is keyword-only with a default because the file is outside
        AC-P1: it is not hashed and not required to replay, so a unit test that
        only wants a journal omits it and no golden hash moves. run_match passes
        out_dir / "observations.jsonl". See ruling R39.

        Raises InvalidConfigError when config and world.scenario disagree on
        ticks_total, talking_mode, liquidity_profile_name, mm or n_markets
        (section 2.6). MatchConfig is authoritative; the scenario copies are
        informational."""
    def run(self) -> MatchResult
    def run_tick(self, tick: int) -> None
    def phase_p1_diffusion(self, tick: int) -> tuple[Observation, ...]
    def phase_p2_decision(self, tick: int, observations: Sequence[Observation]) -> tuple[AgentAction, ...]
        """Calls gateway.collect_actions, which returns AgentReply objects,
        validates each one here (the single call site of validate_action) and
        returns the surviving AgentAction of each agent, ascending by agent_id.
        AgentReply never leaves this method."""
    def phase_p3_execution(self, tick: int, actions: Sequence[AgentAction]) -> None
    def phase_p4_close(self, tick: int) -> None
    @property
    def state(self) -> MatchState

def run_match(*, config: MatchConfig, world: World, agents: Sequence[AgentSpec],
              gateway: AgentGateway, out_dir: Path, rng: RngTree,
              match_id: str | None = None) -> MatchResult

# pxe/runner/replay.py
def replay_journal(events: Sequence[Event]) -> MatchState
    """FR-5.1.3: rebuild the terminal state from the journal alone."""
def replay_to_tick(events: Sequence[Event], tick: int) -> MatchState
def verify_replay(events: Sequence[Event]) -> None
    """Raises ReplayMismatchError if the replayed state disagrees with the
    PositionSnapshot and MarkToMarket events in the journal."""
```

**How `replay_journal` actually rebuilds a `MatchState`**, since "rebuild it
from the journal" is not a specification:

| `MatchState` field | Rebuilt from |
|---|---|
| `config` | `types.config_from_journal_dict(match_started.config)` |
| `scenario` | `ScenarioSpec(template_id=match_started.scenario_template_id, template_version=..., seed=..., ticks_total=..., markets=tuple(types.market_spec_from_dict(m) for m in match_started.markets))` |
| `match_id`, `tick` | the event envelopes |
| `accounts` | `AccountBook(agent_ids=..., market_ids=..., config=config)` then replay of `TradeExecuted`, `OrderPlaced`, `OrderCancelled`, `SettlementApplied` in `seq` order |
| `exchange` | `Exchange(config=..., scenario=..., accounts=..., journal=<in-memory>)` fed the same order events |
| `outcomes` | `MarketResolved.outcome` |
| `last_prediction_ppm` | `PredictionRecorded` |
| `pending_messages` | `MessagePosted` with `deliver_tick > final_tick` |
| `frozen_agent_ids` | `AgentFrozen` |

**What `MatchRunner.__init__` constructs, and what it receives.** Five
collaborators are not in the signature, which means the runner builds them and
nothing else may build a second copy:

| Collaborator | Built by the runner as | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `AccountBook` | `AccountBook(agent_ids=[s.agent_id for s in agents if s.ranked], market_ids=world.market_ids(), config=config)` | the `MM` and `FEES` accounts are created by `AccountBook` itself, never passed in |
| `Exchange` | `Exchange(config=config, scenario=world.scenario, accounts=<that book>, journal=journal)` | owns the order and trade counters |
| `InfoEngine` | `InfoEngine(world=world, profiles={s.agent_id: s.info_profile for s in agents if s.ranked}, rng=rng.child("info"), ticks_total=config.ticks_total)` | the mapping is iterated only through `sorted_ids` (section 2.3) |
| `ReferenceMarketMaker` | `ReferenceMarketMaker(config=config.mm, market_ids=world.market_ids(), rng=rng.child("mm"))` | built from `config.mm`, never from the scenario's preset name (section 2.6) |
| `Oracle` | `Oracle(world=world)` | takes no `RngTree` (section 3.1) |

`config`, `world`, `agents`, `gateway`, `journal`, `match_id` and `rng` are the
seven things the runner is *given*. Everything else in the engine is the
runner's to build, exactly once.

The rebuilt `scenario` carries `correlations=()`, `cancellations=()`,
`held_out=False` and `notes=""`. Those four are world generation metadata that
no engine decision reads after `MatchStarted`, so they are deliberately not
journalled and deliberately not recovered; the cancellations that actually
happened are in the journal as `MarketCancelled` events, which is what a
projection uses. `verify_replay` compares positions, cash and reference prices
against `PositionSnapshot` and `MarkToMarket`; it never compares scenario
metadata.

### 7.13 `pxe.runner.observation_builder` (A10)

```python
def build_observation(*, config: MatchConfig, state: MatchState, agent_id: str, tick: int,
                      news: Sequence[NewsItem], signals: Sequence[Signal],
                      messages: Sequence[PublicMessage]) -> Observation
def observation_to_json(obs: Observation) -> dict[str, Any]
    """Exactly the shape of schemas/observation.v1.json."""
def observation_hash(obs: Observation) -> str          # payload_hash(observation_to_json(obs))
def observation_bytes(obs: Observation) -> int
    """Both are written to runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl only. Neither is
    journalled: see section 3.5 and decision 11. A10 may therefore recompact
    the observation without moving a single golden journal hash."""
def estimate_tokens(payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> int
    """Deterministic heuristic: ceil(len(stable_json(payload)) / 3.6). Used by the
    T2.4 budget test only, never by the engine."""
def validate_observation(payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None   # raises ObservationValidationError
def write_observation(path: Path, obs: Observation) -> None
    """Append one line to runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl:
    {"agent_id", "tick", "obs_hash", "obs_bytes", "payload"} through
    stable_json. The one writer of that artefact (section 4.6); without it the
    file has an owner in table 1 and no function anywhere. Opened with
    encoding="utf-8", newline="\n" like every other artefact."""
```

Fidelity rule (T2.4): the observation contains **100 %** of the agent's resting
orders and **100 %** of its non flat positions on open markets. Compaction is
allowed only on depth (3 levels), `ref_history` length and text lengths.

### 7.14 `pxe.runner.action_validator` and `schema_registry` (A11)

```python
# schema_registry.py
def load_schema(name: str) -> dict[str, Any]         # "observation.v1" | "action.v1"
def action_schema() -> dict[str, Any]
def observation_schema() -> dict[str, Any]
def validator_for(name: str) -> Draft202012Validator  # cached

# action_validator.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Rejection:
    scope: str                 # "action" | "order" | "prediction" | "message"
    item_index: int | None
    reason: RejectReason
    detail: str

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ValidationOutcome:
    action: AgentAction
    rejections: tuple[Rejection, ...]

def validate_action(raw: Any, *, agent_id: str, tick: int, config: MatchConfig,
                    open_market_ids: Sequence[str], resting_order_ids: Sequence[str],
                    source: AgentSource) -> ValidationOutcome
    """FR-6.2.2. Never raises on agent input: a fully invalid payload yields
    AgentAction.no_action plus one Rejection with scope 'action'."""

def resolve_predictions(*, declared: Mapping[str, int], previous: Mapping[str, int],
                        open_market_ids: Sequence[str]) -> tuple[tuple[str, int, bool], ...]
    """FR-6.2.4. Returns (market_id, p_yes_ppm, carried) for every open market,
    ascending. Default when nothing was ever declared: 500_000 ppm."""

def sanitise_message(text: str | None, *, max_chars: int) -> str | None
    """FR-5.6.2: strip control characters, collapse whitespace, escape backticks
    and angle brackets, truncate to max_chars. Never interprets the content."""
```

Semantic rules the schema deliberately does **not** encode (see section 12):

| Condition | Reject reason | What survives |
|---|---|---|
| the payload is not an object, or fails `schemas/action.v1.json` outright | `SCHEMA_INVALID` | nothing: `AgentAction.no_action` plus one `scope="action"` rejection |
| a well formed payload the validator cannot interpret at all (unknown `action_version`) | `ACTION_INVALID` | nothing, same shape as above |
| `op == "place"` and `market_id`, `side`, `type` or `qty` is null | `MISSING_FIELD` | that entry is dropped |
| `op == "place"`, `type == "limit"` and `price` is null | `MISSING_FIELD` | that entry is dropped |
| `op == "place"`, `type == "market"` and `price` is not null | `INVALID_PRICE` | that entry is dropped |
| `price` outside `PRICE_MIN..PRICE_MAX` on a limit | `INVALID_PRICE` | that entry is dropped |
| `qty` outside `1..MAX_ORDER_QTY` | `INVALID_QTY` | that entry is dropped |
| `side` is not a `Side` value | `INVALID_SIDE` | that entry is dropped |
| `type` is not an `OrderType` value | `INVALID_TYPE` | that entry is dropped |
| `op` is neither `"place"` nor `"cancel"` | `INVALID_ORDER` | that entry is dropped |
| `op == "cancel"` and `order_id` is null | `MISSING_FIELD` | that entry is dropped |
| `market_id` not in `open_market_ids` | `UNKNOWN_MARKET` | that entry is dropped |
| `order_id` not in `resting_order_ids` of this agent | `UNKNOWN_ORDER` | that entry is dropped |
| more than `config.max_orders_per_action` entries | `TOO_MANY_ORDERS_IN_ACTION` | the first `max_orders_per_action` are kept, the rest dropped |
| more than `MAX_PREDICTIONS_PER_ACTION` `predictions` entries | `TOO_MANY_PREDICTIONS` | the first 8 are kept, the rest dropped |
| duplicate `market_id` in `predictions` | `DUPLICATE_PREDICTION` | the first occurrence is kept |
| `p_yes` is not a number in `[0, 1]` | `INVALID_PROBABILITY` | that entry is dropped, so FR-6.2.4 carries the previous value |
| `message_public` longer than `config.message_max_chars` | `MESSAGE_TOO_LONG` | the message is **truncated** by `sanitise_message`, not dropped |
| `message_public` non null while talking mode is off | `TALKING_MODE_OFF` | the message is dropped |

That table is exhaustive over the `RejectReason` members `validate_action` may
produce. The remaining members belong to two other owners and never appear in
an `AgentActionRejected`: `MARKET_NOT_OPEN`, `NOT_ORDER_OWNER`,
`ORDER_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` and `AGENT_FROZEN` are P3
`OrderRejected` reasons emitted by `Exchange`, and `AGENT_TIMEOUT`,
`PROVIDER_ERROR`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` and `MALFORMED_RESPONSE` are
`AgentReply.error` values that become `AgentTimedOut.reason` (section 7.16).
Between the three lists every one of the 25 members has exactly one producer.

**P2 validation is structural and advisory; P3 liveness is authoritative.**
Both phases look at markets and orders, and without this rule two workstreams
emit two different events for one situation and an integrity detector or the UI
double counts. Normatively:

* `validate_action` never rejects on **liveness**. `open_market_ids` is used to
  catch a market that does not exist or is already resolved *at the time the
  observation was built*, and `resting_order_ids` only to catch an `order_id`
  the agent never owned. Both are a tick-old snapshot by the time P3 runs: the
  market maker requote and every agent earlier in the shuffle may have filled
  or STP-cancelled an order that was resting when P2 looked.
* Only `Exchange` emits `OrderRejected`, and only in P3. A cancel of an order
  that has legitimately gone away between P2 and P3 yields
  `OrderRejected(reason=UNKNOWN_ORDER)` in P3, **not** `AgentActionRejected` in
  P2. Likewise `MARKET_NOT_OPEN`, `NOT_ORDER_OWNER`,
  `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` and `ORDER_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` are P3-only reasons.
* Only the runner emits `AgentActionRejected`, and only in P2, and only for the
  structural and semantic rules in the table above.
* Consequence for metrics and integrity: the number of *intents an agent got
  wrong* is `AgentActionRejected`; the number of *orders the book refused* is
  `OrderRejected`. They are different quantities and are never summed.
  `test_action_validator.py::test_liveness_is_not_validated_in_p2` and
  `test_match_runner.py::test_stale_cancel_is_rejected_by_the_exchange` pin it.

### 7.15 `pxe.agents` (A12)

```python
# pxe/agents/base.py holds all three names. The six baselines live one per file
# under pxe/agents/baselines/ and are reached only through make_baseline, so
# nothing outside A12 imports a baseline module by name (section 1: no
# re-export in __init__.py).
class ScriptedAgent(Protocol):
    agent_id: str
    name: ClassVar[str]
    def reset(self, *, config: MatchConfig, rng: random.Random) -> None: ...
    def act(self, observation: Observation) -> AgentAction: ...

BASELINES: tuple[str, ...] = ("fundamentalist", "momentum", "noise", "zero_intelligence", "bayesian", "mute")

def make_baseline(name: str, *, agent_id: str, rng: random.Random, config: MatchConfig) -> ScriptedAgent
```

Six names, six files: `baselines/fundamentalist.py`, `momentum.py`,
`noise.py`, `zero_intelligence.py`, `bayesian.py` and `mute.py`.
`tests/conftest.py::scripted_gateway` imports `make_baseline` from
`pxe.agents.base` and asks for `"mute"`, so that file is not optional
decoration: without it the shared fixture and AC-P9 have no population.

A scripted agent draws only from the `random.Random` it is given
(`root.child(f"agent/{agent_id}").substream(f"agent.{agent_id}")`). It never
imports the world, the latent process or another agent's state.

`reset` has a defined call site and it is not optional: the runner calls
`agent.reset(config=config, rng=<that same substream>)` for every scripted seat
in ascending `agent_id`, immediately after `MatchStarted` and before tick 1
(section 3.1). It must clear **all** per match state, including the generator:
an orchestrator reuses agent objects across matches, and an agent that keeps a
half consumed `random.Random` or a stale price history makes the second match a
function of the first. `test_determinism.py::test_reused_agent_objects_replay_identically`
is the test that catches it, and it is the only one that can.

`ScriptedGateway` calls `act` and nothing else; it wraps the returned
`AgentAction` with `types.action_to_payload(...)` into `AgentReply.raw`
(section 7.16, rule 2). A scripted agent therefore never bypasses
`validate_action`, which is what makes `test_action_fuzz.py` and the baselines
exercise the same code path.

`bayesian` is the FR-5.3.1 reference: it uses its private signals. `mute` returns
predictions only and no orders; it is used by AC-P9.

### 7.16 `pxe.gateway` (A13, A14)

```python
# protocol.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AgentReply:
    """Everything one agent produced for one tick. The gateway's only output.

    Journalled: agent_id, source, error, and raw once it has been validated.
    Not journalled, written to llm_trace.jsonl instead: raw_text, cost_usd,
    latency_ms, the token counts and attempts (section 3.5).
    """
    agent_id: str
    raw: Mapping[str, Any] | None   # the parsed JSON payload, or None on failure
    raw_text: str | None            # verbatim provider text, for the trace only
    source: AgentSource
    error: RejectReason | None      # None on success; the reason AgentTimedOut carries
    cost_usd: float
    latency_ms: int
    input_tokens: int
    output_tokens: int
    cached_input_tokens: int
    attempts: int

class AgentGateway(Protocol):
    def collect_actions(self, *, tick: int, observations: Sequence[Observation],
                        config: MatchConfig) -> tuple[AgentReply, ...]: ...
    async def acollect_actions(self, *, tick: int, observations: Sequence[Observation],
                               config: MatchConfig) -> tuple[AgentReply, ...]: ...
    def reset_agents(self, *, config: MatchConfig, rng: RngTree) -> None: ...
        """Reset every scripted seat behind this gateway (section 3.1).

        Called by the runner exactly once per match, after MatchStarted and
        before tick 1. The gateway builds the per seat generator itself,
        rng.child(f"agent/{agent_id}").substream(f"agent.{agent_id}"), because
        only it knows which seats it holds. A gateway with no scripted state
        implements it as a no-op. See ruling R59: without this hook the runner
        had to reflect on private attributes, which cannot see an agent stored
        under a name it does not guess, and a silently skipped reset kills O1
        without failing a test."""
    def close(self) -> None: ...

class BaseGateway(AgentGateway):
    """Implements the synchronous bridge: collect_actions runs acollect_actions
    with asyncio.run and sorts replies by agent_id. Subclasses implement
    acall_agent only.

    __init__(*, gateway_config=None, budget=None), with frozen class level
    defaults so a subclass that never calls super().__init__ still works
    (ruling R63). reset_agents is a no-op here: a provider backed gateway has no
    scripted state to clear."""
    async def acall_agent(self, *, agent_id: str, observation: Observation, tick: int) -> AgentReply: ...

# scripted.py
class ScriptedGateway(BaseGateway):
    def __init__(self, *, agents: Mapping[str, ScriptedAgent]) -> None
        """agents is iterated only through sorted_ids(agents.keys())
        (section 2.3)."""
    def reset_agents(self, *, config: MatchConfig, rng: RngTree) -> None
        """Resets every seated agent, ascending (section 3.1, ruling R59)."""

class CompositeGateway(BaseGateway):
    def __init__(self, *, gateways: Sequence[tuple[tuple[str, ...], AgentGateway]]) -> None
    def reset_agents(self, *, config: MatchConfig, rng: RngTree) -> None
        """Delegates to every sub-gateway, in table order."""

# budget.py
class BudgetTracker:
    def __init__(self, config: GatewayConfig) -> None
    def check(self, *, scope: str, amount_usd: float) -> None    # raises BudgetExceededError
    def check_tokens(self, *, input_tokens: int, output_tokens: int) -> None
        """FR-6.2.3's other half. Raises BudgetExceededError against
        GatewayConfig.max_input_tokens_per_call / max_output_tokens_per_call
        before the call is sent, and against max_tokens_per_match after it."""
    def record(self, reply: AgentReply, *, tick: int | None = None) -> None
        """The tick keyword is the ruling R60 extension: AgentReply carries no
        tick, so tick_tokens below is otherwise unimplementable. A14 may keep
        calling record(reply) literally; BaseGateway.acollect_actions calls
        note_tick(tick) once per tick so attribution still works."""
    def note_tick(self, tick: int) -> None
    def spent_usd(self, scope: str = "match") -> float
    def remaining_usd(self, scope: str = "match") -> float
    def spent_tokens(self, scope: str = "match") -> tuple[int, int]   # (input, output)
    def tick_tokens(self, agent_id: str, tick: int) -> tuple[int, int]
        """What one agent spent on one tick. See the per tick rule below."""

# claude_cli.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ClaudeCliResult:
    is_error: bool
    result_text: str
    total_cost_usd: float
    duration_ms: int
    input_tokens: int
    output_tokens: int
    cache_creation_input_tokens: int
    cache_read_input_tokens: int

class ClaudeCliGateway(BaseGateway):
    def __init__(self, *, harnesses: Mapping[str, HarnessConfig], gateway_config: GatewayConfig,
                 budget: BudgetTracker, trace_path: Path | None = None) -> None
        """harnesses is iterated only through sorted_ids(harnesses.keys())
        (section 2.3)."""
    async def acall_agent(self, *, agent_id: str, observation: Observation, tick: int) -> AgentReply

def claude_executable() -> str
    """'claude.cmd' on Windows, 'claude' elsewhere, resolved at runtime."""
def build_cli_argv(*, prompt: str, system_prompt: str, model: str,
                   schema: Mapping[str, Any], max_budget_usd: float) -> list[str]
def parse_cli_stdout(stdout: str) -> ClaudeCliResult   # raises MalformedResponseError

DEFAULT_MODEL: str

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class LlmTraceEntry:
    """One line of runs/<match_id>/llm_trace.jsonl (section 4.6, ruling R68)."""
    tick: int
    agent_id: str
    model: str
    attempt: int
    ok: bool
    cost_usd: float
    duration_ms: int
    input_tokens: int
    output_tokens: int
    cached_input_tokens: int
    error: str | None
    text: str

def write_llm_trace(path: Path, entry: LlmTraceEntry) -> None
    """Append one line. Section 4.6 gives A14 the file and section 7.16 gave it
    no writer, which is ruling R68. Cost, latency and token counts are exactly
    what section 3.5 forbids in a journal, so this file is the only place they
    exist, and it is outside the journal hash by construction."""

# prompt.py
PROMPT_TEMPLATE_VERSION: str

def build_system_prompt(harness: HarnessConfig, *, config: MatchConfig) -> str
def build_user_prompt(observation_json: Mapping[str, Any]) -> str
```

**A14 reads `pxe.runner.observation_builder` and `pxe.runner.schema_registry`,
and section 13 now says so** (ruling R69). `build_user_prompt` renders what
`observation_to_json` produced and `build_cli_argv(schema=...)` is handed what
`action_schema()` loaded; both have exactly one owner, and re-implementing
either inside the gateway would create a second observation renderer or a
second schema loader. The dependency arrow already allowed it (gateway sits to
the right of runner); the "May read" column was simply short.

**The gateway boundary, normatively.** This is the one seam between the pure
world and the impure one, and it was the single most contradictory part of an
earlier draft. Four rules, and they are not negotiable:

1. **The gateway returns replies, never actions.** `collect_actions` and
   `acollect_actions` return `tuple[AgentReply, ...]`, one per observation,
   sorted by `agent_id`. There is no `AgentAction` anywhere in
   `pxe.gateway`'s public API.
2. **`AgentReply.raw` is always a JSON compatible `Mapping` matching
   `schemas/action.v1.json`, or `None`.** Never an `AgentAction`, never a
   string, never a list. `ClaudeCliGateway` puts the parsed provider JSON
   there; `ScriptedGateway` puts `types.action_to_payload(agent.act(obs))`
   there, so a scripted agent and an LLM agent reach `validate_action` through
   the same door and A11 has one shape to handle instead of two. A scripted
   agent that produces an unrepresentable action is a bug in A12, not a case
   for A11.
3. **The gateway never validates and never rejects.** It has no
   `open_market_ids`, no `resting_order_ids` and no `MatchConfig` semantics,
   and validation there would build the `Rejection` list inside an impure
   module and discard it, which makes FR-6.2.2 unimplementable. Its only
   judgement is "did the call succeed": on a non zero exit, a parse failure, a
   provider schema failure, a timeout or a budget breach, after `retries`
   attempts, it returns `raw=None`, `source=FALLBACK` and the matching
   `RejectReason` in `error` (FR-5.1.1). `error` is exactly what
   `AgentTimedOut.reason` carries; `AgentAction` has no field for it, which is
   the second reason this type exists.
4. **`raw_text`, `cost_usd`, `latency_ms`, the token counts and `attempts`
   never reach the journal.** They are wall clock and provider dependent
   (section 3.5) and go to `runs/<match_id>/llm_trace.jsonl`, written by A14.
   `BudgetTracker.record(reply)` reads them.

`build_cli_argv` returns exactly, in this order:

```python
[claude_executable(), "-p", prompt, "--model", model, "--output-format", "json",
 "--system-prompt", system_prompt, "--json-schema", json.dumps(schema),
 "--allowed-tools", "", "--disable-slash-commands", "--strict-mcp-config",
 "--setting-sources", "", "--max-budget-usd", f"{max_budget_usd:.4f}"]
```

Invoked with `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` (no shell). `--bare` is never
passed: it needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which is not how this environment
authenticates. On a non zero return code, `is_error`, a parse failure, a schema
failure or a budget breach, the gateway retries up to `retries` times and then
returns an `AgentReply` with `raw=None`, `source=FALLBACK` and the matching
`RejectReason` (FR-5.1.1).

**Cost model.** One call per agent per tick, covering all markets at once
(measured: about 0.027 USD and 6 s per call, about 35k cached input tokens of
unavoidable CLI overhead). Budget caps are mandatory.

#### FR-6.2.3: the token budget is *per tick*, and here is why per call is it

FR-6.2.3 reads "Le harness a droit a un budget de tokens et un timeout **par
tick**, configures par tournoi". Two halves, two mechanisms, and neither is
optional:

* the **timeout** per tick is `GatewayConfig.timeout_s`, applied by
  `BaseGateway` to each `acall_agent`;
* the **token budget** per tick is
  `GatewayConfig.max_input_tokens_per_call` and
  `.max_output_tokens_per_call`, applied by `BudgetTracker.check_tokens`.

"Per call" and "per tick" are the same quantity here **because decision 14
fixes exactly one provider call per agent per tick**, covering every market.
That equality is a consequence of a decision, not a coincidence, so it is
written down: the day someone adds a retry that is charged separately, or a
second call per tick, the per tick budget stops being the per call budget and
this paragraph is the thing that has to change with it. `tick_tokens(agent_id,
tick)` exists so the assertion can be made on the tick and not on the call:
`test_budget.py::test_token_cap_enforced` asserts a rejected oversized call,
and `::test_per_tick_tokens_equal_per_call_tokens` asserts the equality above
over a whole match, which is what fails loudly if a second call ever appears.

Both caps are enforced **before** the call is sent (the input side from the
rendered prompt, the output side by passing the limit to the provider), so a
breach costs nothing. A breach is not an exception at the engine boundary
(section 2.4): the gateway catches `BudgetExceededError`, returns
`AgentReply(raw=None, source=FALLBACK, error=RejectReason.BUDGET_EXCEEDED)`,
and the runner emits `AgentTimedOut` and carries the agent's predictions
(FR-5.1.1, FR-6.2.4). The match never stops because one agent got greedy.

`GatewayConfig.max_tokens_per_match` (`0` means unlimited) is the cumulative
guard on top, and it is the one that bounds a runaway match rather than a
runaway tick.

**Where the caps come from.** `TournamentConfig.gateway` is a `GatewayConfig`
built by the orchestrator from its TOML, so "configures par tournoi" is
literal: no per match override exists, and `MatchConfig` deliberately carries
none of this (section 2.6, it would put a provider setting in the journal).

### 7.17 `pxe.metrics` (A15, A16, A17)

```python
# projection.py

# The five row types MatchProjection is made of. A15 owns the module; A16, A17,
# A18, A22 and A25 all code against these exact field names. Every money field
# is cents, every probability is ppm, every ratio is ppm, every duration is
# ticks. Rows are sorted as stated and that order is part of the contract,
# because bootstrap resampling in metrics and integrity consumes them in order.

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TradeRecord:
    """One TradeExecuted. Sorted by (tick, trade_id)."""
    trade_id: str
    tick: int
    market_id: str
    price: int
    qty: int
    maker_order_id: str
    maker_agent_id: str
    maker_side: str                 # "buy" | "sell"
    taker_order_id: str
    taker_agent_id: str
    taker_side: str
    taker_fee_cents: int
    maker_cash_delta_cents: int
    taker_cash_delta_cents: int

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OrderRecord:
    """One order's whole life, folded from OrderPlaced + OrderCancelled +
    TradeExecuted. Sorted by (placed_tick, order_id). An order still resting at
    the end of the match has cancelled_tick None and cancel_reason None."""
    order_id: str
    agent_id: str
    market_id: str
    side: str                       # "buy" | "sell"
    requested_type: str             # "limit" | "market"
    price: int                      # the banded limit price for a market order
    qty: int                        # originally submitted quantity
    placed_tick: int
    filled_qty: int
    cancelled_tick: int | None
    cancel_reason: str | None       # a CancelReason value
    reserved_cents: int
    released_cents: int

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PredictionRow:
    """One PredictionRecorded. Sorted by (tick, agent_id, market_id).

    Named PredictionRow and not PredictionRecord on purpose: the event is
    PredictionRecorded and there is no types.Prediction, so one concept keeps
    exactly two names, the event and the row."""
    agent_id: str
    market_id: str
    tick: int
    p_yes_ppm: int
    carried: bool

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SignalRecord:
    """One SignalDelivered. Sorted by (tick, agent_id, signal_id)."""
    signal_id: str
    tick: int
    agent_id: str
    market_id: str
    kind: str                       # a SignalKind value
    value_milli: int
    precision_ppm: int

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MessageRecord:
    """One MessagePosted. Sorted by (tick, agent_id)."""
    agent_id: str
    tick: int
    text: str
    deliver_tick: int

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Highlight:
    """One annotated marking event of the match (PRD section 9). Sorted by
    (tick, kind, market_id). This is the whole data contract behind the UI's
    "evenements marquants annotes" and behind AC-P7 ("a newcomer identifies the
    winner and a reason in under 30 s"): without it A24 has to re-derive
    interestingness from raw events in the browser.

    kind is one of "big_trade" (notional above 3x the match median),
    "early_resolution" (a market resolving before ticks_total),
    "integrity_alert" (one incident from incidents.jsonl) or "bankruptcy".

    magnitude_cents per kind, because two of the four had no definition
    (ruling R73): "big_trade" is the trade notional (price * qty);
    "bankruptcy" is the frozen seat's equity at the freeze;
    "early_resolution" is the sum of the positive SettlementApplied
    cash_delta_cents of that market's resolution group, that is the money the
    resolution paid out; "integrity_alert" is 0, because an incident carries a
    score_ppm and no cents, and the score goes in the label. The tick of an
    "early_resolution" is the envelope tick of MarketResolved, that is
    resolution_tick + 1 (section 5.0), which is the tick frame the API attaches
    it to.

    The "big_trade" threshold is 3x the LOWER median of the match's trade
    notionals, so an even trade count needs no tie-break and no float decides
    what a spectator sees (ruling R74).

    label is the one sentence a spectator reads. It is built here and not in
    the browser, because AC-P7 ("a newcomer names the winner and a reason in
    under 30 s") is exactly the claim that a reason is available without a
    chart, and section 7.21 serves this field verbatim as Highlight.label.
    """
    tick: int
    kind: str
    agent_ids: tuple[str, ...]      # sorted, may be empty
    market_id: str | None
    magnitude_cents: int
    label: str

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MatchProjection:
    match_id: str
    seed: int
    ticks_total: int
    agent_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    market_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    outcomes: tuple[tuple[str, Outcome], ...]        # resolved markets only, ascending
    resolution_ticks: tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]    # EVERY market, the SCHEDULED tick
    equity_cents: tuple[tuple[str, tuple[int, ...]], ...]     # per agent, per tick
    cash_cents: tuple[tuple[str, tuple[int, ...]], ...]
    ref_price: tuple[tuple[str, tuple[int, ...]], ...]        # per market, per tick
    trades: tuple[TradeRecord, ...]
    orders: tuple[OrderRecord, ...]
    predictions: tuple[PredictionRow, ...]
    signals: tuple[SignalRecord, ...]
    messages: tuple[MessageRecord, ...]
    highlights: tuple[Highlight, ...]
    mm_pnl_cents: int
    fees_collected_cents: int
    initial_cash_cents: int
    def agent_index(self, agent_id: str) -> int
    def equity_series(self, agent_id: str) -> tuple[int, ...]

def project(events: Sequence[Event], *, incidents: Sequence[Incident] = ()) -> MatchProjection
    """The one and only journal reader for metrics. Every metric module consumes
    a MatchProjection, never raw events, never engine state.

    `incidents` is the one thing the journal cannot supply: integrity
    incidents are deliberately not journalled (section 4.5, decision 10), and
    Highlight.kind == "integrity_alert" needs them. It defaults to () so
    `project(events)` stays a pure function of the journal and a detector
    version bump can never move a metric; callers that have the file pass it
    (`Store.load_projection` passes `self.load_incidents(match_id=...)`, and
    the API therefore gets alert highlights while
    `test_metrics_decoupling.py` does not)."""

# performance.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PerformanceMetrics:
    agent_id: str
    pnl_cents: int
    pnl_bps: int
    sharpe_milli: int
    max_drawdown_cents: int
    max_drawdown_bps: int
    volume_qty: int
    trade_count: int
    maker_trade_count: int
    fees_paid_cents: int

def compute_performance(projection: MatchProjection) -> tuple[PerformanceMetrics, ...]
def final_ranking(projection: MatchProjection) -> tuple[MatchRanking, ...]

# calibration.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CalibrationMetrics:
    agent_id: str
    brier_ppm: int
    n_terms: int
    n_carried: int

def compute_calibration(projection: MatchProjection) -> tuple[CalibrationMetrics, ...]
def reliability_curve(projection: MatchProjection, agent_id: str, *, n_bins: int = 10) -> tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...]
    """(bin_upper_ppm, n_predictions, observed_yes_ppm), exactly n_bins rows,
    empty bins included so two agents' curves are index aligned (ruling R79).

    A prediction lands in bin min(n_bins - 1, p_yes_ppm * n_bins // PPM_ONE),
    so every edge is exclusive except the upper edge of the last bin, which is
    closed. observed_yes_ppm of an empty bin is 0. The curve partitions exactly
    the terms the Brier averages, because both go through the same counted-term
    filter: a curve over a different population than the score it illustrates
    would be worse than no curve."""

# behavioral.py
DESCRIPTOR_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "maker_ratio_ppm", "reaction_latency_milli", "holding_horizon_milli",
    "herfindahl_ppm", "leverage_ppm", "message_intensity_ppm")

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BehavioralDescriptors:
    agent_id: str
    maker_ratio_ppm: int
    reaction_latency_milli: int
    holding_horizon_milli: int
    herfindahl_ppm: int
    leverage_ppm: int
    message_intensity_ppm: int
    def as_tuple(self) -> tuple[int, ...]

def compute_descriptors(projection: MatchProjection) -> tuple[BehavioralDescriptors, ...]

# aggregate.py (A15). A separate module and not projection.py, because it
# imports performance, calibration and behavioral, all three of which import
# projection: putting it there would make the metrics package import itself.
# It is not __init__.py either (section 1: shared __init__.py files hold a
# docstring and nothing else).
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MatchMetrics:
    match_id: str
    performance: tuple[PerformanceMetrics, ...]     # sorted by agent_id
    calibration: tuple[CalibrationMetrics, ...]     # sorted by agent_id
    descriptors: tuple[BehavioralDescriptors, ...]  # sorted by agent_id
    mm_pnl_cents: int
    fees_collected_cents: int

def compute_all(projection: MatchProjection) -> MatchMetrics
def write_metrics(path: Path, metrics: MatchMetrics) -> None
    """The one writer of runs/<match_id>/metrics.json (section 4.6). Uses
    stable_json and the usual open() arguments. The file carries a
    "format": "pxe.metrics/1" marker so read_metrics can refuse a foreign
    file (ruling R76)."""
def read_metrics(path: Path) -> MatchMetrics
    """What GET /api/matches/{id}/metrics serves. The API never recomputes
    engine logic, so without this function that endpoint has no source."""
```

**`MatchMetrics` has no `reliability` field and `MatchMetricsPayload` does, on
purpose** (ruling R75). Section 7.21's calibration block carries a reliability
curve per agent; `metrics.json` carries the four `CalibrationMetrics` fields and
nothing else. The two statements "the API serves `metrics.json`" and "the
payload has a reliability curve" cannot both be literal, so the rule is: the
metrics route reads `metrics.json` for every number **and** calls
`reliability_curve(projection, agent_id)` per agent for the curve. That is not
"recomputing engine logic" (rule 1 of section 7.21): `reliability_curve` is a
`pxe.metrics` function over a `MatchProjection` the route already holds, and
storing ten bins per agent in the file would freeze `n_bins` into an artefact.
`test_api.py::test_metrics_payload_matches_the_file` is what keeps the shared
half honest.

### 7.18 `pxe.integrity` (A18)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DetectorConfig:
    collusion_threshold_ppm: int = 700_000
    off_market_sigma_milli: int = 3_000
    spoofing_cancel_ratio_ppm: int = 900_000
    mismatch_threshold_ppm: int = 300_000
    min_trades: int = 5

class Detector(Protocol):
    name: ClassVar[str]
    version: ClassVar[str]
    def run(self, projection: MatchProjection, config: DetectorConfig) -> tuple[Incident, ...]: ...

DETECTORS: tuple[Detector, ...]

def run_detectors(projection: MatchProjection, *, config: DetectorConfig | None = None,
                  detectors: Sequence[Detector] | None = None) -> tuple[Incident, ...]
def collusion_index(projection: MatchProjection) -> tuple[tuple[str, str, int], ...]
    """(agent_a, agent_b, index_ppm) sorted by pair, a < b."""
def write_incidents(path: Path, incidents: Sequence[Incident]) -> None
    """One JSON object per line, detail encoded with
    types.incident_detail_to_dict. A18 never writes a conversion of its own:
    types.Incident.detail is an ordered tuple of pairs and IncidentRaised.detail
    is a mapping, and pxe.types owns both directions (section 7.1).

    It REPLACES the file; see section 4.5 and ruling R83."""

# cheaters.py: scripted agents for the calibration bench (T5.5)
def make_cheater(name: str, *, agent_id: str, partner_id: str | None, rng: random.Random,
                 config: MatchConfig) -> ScriptedAgent
    """Raises InvalidConfigError when a cheater that needs an accomplice gets
    none, or gets itself (ruling R88, consistent with R56)."""
CHEATERS: tuple[str, ...] = ("colluder", "washer", "spoofer", "liar")
```

#### What a detector score is, and what a severity is (rulings R85 to R87)

Three things section 7.18 left to the implementer, now fixed:

1. **The dispersion scale of `off_market_sigma_milli` is a rescaled median
   absolute deviation about the reference price, not a standard deviation.**
   "Beyond k standard deviations" is not implementable with a squared
   estimator: the outlier being measured inflates the estimate it is measured
   against, and a bench case shows it exactly (24 prints 45 cents away from the
   reference pushed the 3-sigma band out to 60 cents and masked themselves). A
   MAD about the reference, rescaled by `1.4826` so `k` keeps its normal-theory
   meaning, is breakdown-robust and is what `OffMarketTransferDetector` uses.
2. **`Incident.severity` grades uniformly, off the score and its own
   threshold**: `"high"` at twice the threshold or at `score_ppm >= 900_000`,
   `"medium"` at 1.2x the threshold, `"low"` otherwise. One rule for five
   detectors, so a severity means the same thing whichever raised it.
3. **`score_ppm` is "comparable to the detector threshold" and for
   `off_market_transfer` that comparison is against `off_market_sigma_milli *
   1000`**, because that threshold is in *milli* and the score is a multiple of
   sigma expressed in ppm. It may therefore exceed `1_000_000`: a ten sigma
   print scores `10_000_000`. That is the one documented exception to section
   2.1's `0..1_000_000` ppm range, and it is an exception because the quantity
   is a ratio of dispersions and not a probability.

`min_trades` is the only materiality knob, so `OffMarketTransferDetector`
applies it to the count of off-market prints per `(pair, market)`: a single
one-shot transfer therefore raises no incident. That is a stated limit and not
an oversight - honest matches produce one or two isolated off-market prints,
which is where the whole residual false positive rate would otherwise come
from.

`collusion_index(projection)` returns **only the pairs that traded with each
other**, `a < b`, ascending. A full matrix over eight seats is 28 rows of which
most are structurally zero, and the caller cannot tell "no shared trade" from
"shared trades with no pattern" if both read `0`.

`integrity.bootstrap` stays in the section 3.3 registry and stays unused: no
detector draws randomness, and an unused registered name is cheaper than a name
missing on the day a confidence interval is added to an alert.

### 7.19 `pxe.tournament` (A19, A20, A23)

`MatchTask`, `TournamentConfig`, `TournamentResult` and `RatingRecord` are
**not** defined here. They are pure data carriers that `pxe.store` persists and
`pxe.tournament` consumes, so defining them on either side makes the two
packages import each other; they live in `pxe.types` (section 7.1) and both
