sides import leftward. Every signature below refers to those types by name.

```python
# latin_square.py
def latin_square(n: int) -> tuple[tuple[int, ...], ...]
    """The cyclic Latin square of order n: row i is (i, i+1, ..., n-1, 0, ..., i-1).
    Deterministic, no RNG, and the only construction used here."""
def expand_profile_kinds(kinds: Sequence[InfoProfileKind], n_seats: int) -> tuple[InfoProfileKind, ...]
    """Cycle `kinds` in declaration order up to length n_seats. See below."""
def assign_profiles(*, agent_ids: Sequence[str], profile_kinds: Sequence[InfoProfileKind],
                    seed_index: int) -> tuple[tuple[str, InfoProfileKind], ...]
    """Rotate the expanded kind vector left by seed_index and zip it with
    sorted_ids(agent_ids). Sorted by agent_id."""
def verify_balance(assignments: Sequence[Sequence[tuple[str, InfoProfileKind]]]) -> BalanceReport
    """FR-5.3.2. Returns a report, never a bare bool: with some seat counts
    exact balance is arithmetically impossible and a False would read as a bug.
    See the normative rule below."""

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BalanceReport:
    exact: bool                                       # every seat saw every kind equally often
    counts: tuple[tuple[str, tuple[int, ...]], ...]    # agent_id -> count per kind, kind order fixed
    max_deviation: int                                 # distance to [floor(mean), ceil(mean)], 0 when even
    seeds: int
    seats: int
    def __bool__(self) -> bool: ...                    # returns self.exact

# scheduling.py (A19). PRD section 8 names three formats; plan() alone can only
# express the two that do not depend on standings.
def round_robin_pairings(*, harness_keys: Sequence[str],
                         agents_per_match: int) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]
def swiss_pairings(*, standings: Sequence[RatingRecord], agents_per_match: int,
                   played: Sequence[frozenset[str]],
                   rng: random.Random) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]
    """The round index is DERIVED and not an argument (ruling R90):
    len(played) // groups_per_round, which is exact because a Swiss round always
    seats (n - n % agents_per_match) / agents_per_match groups. Step 4's bye
    rotation needs it and adding a parameter would have widened the signature
    every caller already writes."""
def exhibition_pairings(*, harness_keys: Sequence[str], challengers: Sequence[str],
                        agents_per_match: int = 0) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]
    """agents_per_match is the preamble rule 2 extension (ruling R91): the
    normative body below is `tuple(k for k in harness_keys if k != c)[:
    agents_per_match - 1]`, which needs the number the two-argument signature
    does not carry. 0 means "the whole field", so the contracted call is
    unchanged."""
def format_pairings(format: TournamentFormat, *, harness_keys: Sequence[str],
                    agents_per_match: int, standings: Sequence[RatingRecord],
                    played: Sequence[frozenset[str]], challengers: Sequence[str],
                    rng: random.Random) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]
    """The one dispatcher TournamentOrchestrator.plan_round calls, so the
    orchestrator holds no `if format ==` chain of its own."""
```

#### The three pairing algorithms, normatively (PRD section 8, T3.1)

All three return a tuple of **seat groups**. A group is a tuple of exactly
`agents_per_match` harness keys and the position inside the group **is** the
seat: `group[0]` sits at `A1`. Groups come back in the order they must be
played. Nothing here reads a clock and only Swiss touches an RNG.

**Round robin** (small populations). Every combination of `agents_per_match`
distinct harness keys, exactly once:

```
keys   = tuple(sorted(harness_keys))            # lexicographic, stable
groups = tuple(itertools.combinations(keys, agents_per_match))
```

`itertools.combinations` over a sorted input is lexicographically ordered and
deterministic, which is why it is named rather than described. The count is
`C(len(keys), agents_per_match)`, and `TournamentConfig.__post_init__` raises
`InvalidConfigError` when `len(harnesses) < agents_per_match`. Seat assignment
inside a group is that sorted order; seat *fairness* is not this function job,
it belongs to the Latin square (FR-5.3.2) and the P3 shuffle (FR-5.1.5).
`test_tournament.py::test_round_robin_covers_every_matchup` asserts every
combination appears exactly once and that two calls are byte identical.

**Swiss** (large populations). One round at a time, which is the entire reason
`plan_round` exists:

1. Sort `standings` by `(-mu, sigma, harness_key)`: strongest first, then the
   more uncertain player first (it gains the most information from a match),
   then the key as a final tiebreak so the order never depends on the caller
   input order.
2. Walk that list from the top, greedily taking the next
   `agents_per_match - 1` still unpaired keys whose resulting `frozenset` is
   not in `played`. A key already placed this round is unavailable.
3. If no admissible group can be formed from the current head (every candidate
   set has been played), **allow a repeat and take the candidate group with the
   fewest prior meetings.** An earlier draft spelled this as two relaxations,
   "(a) allow one repeat, choosing the candidate group with the fewest prior
   meetings; (b) allow any repeat", and they are the same rule: no input
   produces different output under the two readings, because "fewest prior
   meetings" already prefers one repeat over two. Ruling R92. Never leave a
   player unpaired and never silently shorten a group.
4. When `len(standings) % agents_per_match != 0` the tail (fewer than
   `agents_per_match` keys) receives a **bye**: it plays no match this round
   and its sigma is untouched. Byes rotate: round `r` takes its tail from the
   standings rotated left by `r * remainder`, so no key gets a second bye
   before every key has had one.
5. `rng` is `tournament_rng.fresh_substream("tournament.pairing")` and is used
   for exactly one thing: breaking a tie between two candidate groups that
   have the same "fewest prior meetings" count at step 3. `fresh_substream`
   and not `substream`, so round `r` pairing does not depend on how many draws
   round `r - 1` consumed.

Tests: `test_tournament.py::test_swiss_pairs_by_standing_without_rematch`
(the top two keys of the standings share a group in round 1, and no round 2
group repeats a `played` set) and `::test_swiss_byes_rotate` (over
`ceil(n / remainder)` rounds no key has two byes).

**Exhibition** (a challenger against a fixed field). Each key in `challengers`
plays one match against the field:

```
field  = tuple(k for k in harness_keys if k != c)[: agents_per_match - 1]
groups = tuple((c,) + field_for(c) for c in challengers)
```

The challenger is always seat `A1`, which makes an exhibition report readable
without a legend. `field` keeps the order the caller gave (normally the Hall
of Fame or the background baselines): this function never re-sorts it, because
"the field" is a curated list and sorting it would silently change who plays.
Exhibition matches are **not rated**: `TournamentOrchestrator.run` skips
`RatingService.update` for a tournament whose format is
`TournamentFormat.EXHIBITION`, so a novelty match cannot move a leaderboard.
`test_tournament.py::test_exhibition_puts_the_challenger_in_seat_one` and
`::test_exhibition_does_not_move_ratings` pin it.

#### FR-5.3.2 Latin square: what "an equal number of times" degrades to

FR-5.3.2 asks that "chaque harness occupe chaque profil un nombre egal de
fois". PRD section 5.7 sets the default seed count to 3 and the seat count to
4..8, and `InfoProfileKind` has exactly **three** members (`GENERALIST`,
`SPECIALIST`, `DELAYED`). Exact balance is therefore impossible in the general
case: a Latin square of order 6 needs 6 symbols, and with 5 or 7 seats no
cycling of 3 kinds can give every seat the same multiset. Writing
"`verify_balance` returns `True`" as an acceptance criterion would have made
AC-P3 fail on configurations the PRD itself allows. The concrete rule, in
full:

1. **Expansion.** `profile_kinds` is expanded to length `n = len(agent_ids)`
   by cycling `tuple(InfoProfileKind)` in **declaration order**:
   `expand_profile_kinds` returns
   `tuple(kinds[i % len(kinds)] for i in range(n))`. For 6 seats and the three
   default kinds that is
   `(GENERALIST, SPECIALIST, DELAYED, GENERALIST, SPECIALIST, DELAYED)`.
   Declaration order and not sorted order, so adding a fourth kind later
   changes the vector in one predictable place.
2. **Rotation.** `assign_profiles(..., seed_index=k)` rotates that vector left
   by `k % n` and zips it with `sorted_ids(agent_ids)`. Over `n` consecutive
   seed indices every seat therefore sees the whole expanded vector exactly
   once. That is the Latin square property on **seats**, and it holds for
   every `n` in 4..8 and any number of kinds.
3. **When balance is exact.** Let `q, r = divmod(n, len(kinds))`. A kind
   appears `q + 1` times in the expanded vector when its index is below `r`
   and `q` times otherwise. Balance is exact if and only if `len(kinds)`
   divides `n` **and** `len(kinds)` divides the number of seeds. With the PRD
   defaults (`n = 6`, three kinds, three seeds) both hold, so the defaults are
   fine; only the seat counts that are not multiples of three degrade.
4. **The degradation, and it is not an error.** Otherwise `verify_balance`
   returns `BalanceReport(exact=False, max_deviation=d, ...)` with `d >= 1`,
   `TournamentOrchestrator` proceeds unchanged, and the report (section 7.24)
   prints `approximate profile balance (max deviation d over s seeds)` in its
   leaderboard header. Nothing raises, nothing is skipped, and no match is
   discarded. AC-P3 requires "carre latin des profils applique", which this
   satisfies: the permutation is applied, and its residual imbalance is
   measured and published instead of being hidden.
5. **What does raise, and where.** `TournamentConfig.__post_init__` (section
   7.1, `pxe.types`) raises `InvalidConfigError` when `len(seeds) < 3` (PRD
   section 5.7 makes three the floor and AC-P3 restates it) and when
   `agents_per_match` is outside 4..8. Those two are properties of the config
   object alone, so an invalid tournament cannot be constructed at all.

   The third refusal an earlier draft put there, "when `len(harnesses) <
   agents_per_match`", is **not** in `__post_init__` and cannot be (ruling R93):
   `TournamentConfig.harnesses` holds the competitors only, while the seats are
   filled by the competitors **plus** `background_baselines`, and
   `configs/exhibition.toml` legitimately ships one challenger against a field
   of four. Only `TournamentOrchestrator` knows the assembled population, so
   `TournamentOrchestrator.__init__` raises it, together with the checks that
   need a template registry or the store's id conventions (unknown template,
   duplicate seed or harness key, non positive round count, a `tournament_id`
   that is empty, over 24 characters or holds the reserved `#`).
   `test_tournament.py::test_the_config_refuses_its_own_two_invariants` and
   `::test_orchestrator_refuses_an_invalid_configuration` pin the split, each
   with a legal control so neither can pass by refusing everything.
   An imperfect square is not an error at all.
6. **The advice the report prints when it is not exact.** Use a seed count
   that is a multiple of both `agents_per_match` (which gives every seat the
   whole vector) and `len(kinds)`. For 6 seats and three kinds the smallest
   such count is 6.

Tests: `test_latin_square.py::test_each_harness_gets_each_profile_equally`
(the exact case: 6 seats, 3 kinds, 6 seeds),
`::test_balance_is_exact_with_prd_defaults` (6 seats, 3 seeds),
`::test_balance_degrades_gracefully_on_five_seats` (asserts
`exact is False`, `max_deviation == 1`, and that **nothing raises**),
`::test_rotation_covers_every_seat_over_n_seeds`, and
`::test_seed_count_below_three_raises`.

```python
# orchestrator.py
class TournamentOrchestrator:
    def __init__(self, *, config: TournamentConfig, store: Store,
                 heldout: HeldoutBank | None = None) -> None
        """heldout is the preamble rule 2 extension (ruling R94): section 7.20's
        writer table says heldout_registry is written "by HeldoutBank.draw
        through the orchestrator", which the two-argument signature makes
        impossible. `pxe tournament run --heldout-bank` supplies it."""
    def plan(self) -> tuple[MatchTask, ...]
        """Round robin and exhibition only: the whole schedule up front."""
    def plan_round(self, round_index: int,
                   standings: Sequence[RatingRecord]) -> tuple[MatchTask, ...]
        """The general form, and the only one Swiss can use: pairing depends on
        the standings after the previous round. plan() is plan_round(0, ()) for
        the two standings-independent formats."""
    def run(self, *, max_workers: int = 4) -> TournamentResult
    def resume(self) -> TournamentResult
        """Idempotent: a task already present in the store is never replayed."""

# ratings.py
UNRATED_ACCOUNT_IDS: frozenset[str]     # {"MM", "FEES"}, dropped before sorted_ids
DEFAULT_MU: float
DEFAULT_SIGMA: float
DEFAULT_BETA: float
DEFAULT_TAU: float

class RatingService:
    def __init__(self, *, mu: float = 25.0, sigma: float = 8.333, beta: float = 4.167,
                 tau: float = 0.083, draw_probability: float = 0.0) -> None
        """draw_probability = 0.0 has one counter-intuitive consequence worth
        writing down (ruling R104): under a zero draw prior an EXACT PnL tie is
        an outcome the model calls impossible, so it RAISES sigma (8.333 ->
        9.588 at the prior) instead of lowering it. Exact ties are reachable
        (two mute seats both at zero PnL), and the behaviour is pinned by
        test_ratings.py::test_a_shared_rank_is_a_draw rather than silently
        overridden: the alternative, a non zero default, would change every
        rating in the project to hide one edge case."""
    def update(self, ranking: Sequence[MatchRanking], *, harness_of: Mapping[str, str]) -> tuple[RatingRecord, ...]
        """MM and FEES are never rated (FR-5.8.5). harness_of is iterated as
        sorted_ids(harness_of.keys() - UNRATED_ACCOUNT_IDS), that is the two
        reserved accounts are DROPPED FIRST and then the rest is sorted.

        The order of those two operations is the whole ruling (R103): section
        2.3 rule 2 makes sorted_ids RAISE InvalidConfigError on MM or FEES, so
        "iterate harness_of through sorted_ids" and "MM and FEES are never
        rated" are unsatisfiable in the other order. Dropping first is also the
        only reading under which a caller handing in a mapping that happens to
        carry MM gets a leaderboard rather than a crash."""
    def rating(self, harness_key: str) -> RatingRecord
    def leaderboard(self) -> tuple[RatingRecord, ...]

def bootstrap_ci(values: Sequence[float], *, n_resamples: int = 2000, alpha: float = 0.05,
                 rng: random.Random) -> tuple[float, float]
    """The percentile bootstrap, drawn from metrics.bootstrap (section 3.3)."""

# elites.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EliteCell:
    coords: tuple[int, ...]
    harness_key: str
    mu: float
    descriptors: tuple[int, ...]

class EliteArchive:
    def __init__(self, *, axes: Sequence[str], bins: Sequence[int]) -> None
    @property
    def axes(self) -> tuple[str, ...]      # ruling R97: coords alone is unlabelled
    @property
    def bins(self) -> tuple[int, ...]
    def coords_for(self, descriptors: BehavioralDescriptors) -> tuple[int, ...]
    def offer(self, *, harness_key: str, mu: float, descriptors: BehavioralDescriptors) -> bool
    def cells(self) -> tuple[EliteCell, ...]

AXIS_MAX: Mapping[str, int]
    """Inclusive upper bound per axis, derived from the descriptor suffix: a
    `_ppm` axis is bounded by PPM_ONE, a `_milli` axis by MILLI_ONE * 96, the
    ticks_total ceiling of MatchConfig. EliteArchive(*, axes, bins) receives no
    scale and no MatchConfig, so without this the two duration axes are
    unbounded and cannot be binned. Values above the bound clamp into the top
    bin. Ruling R97."""

DEFAULT_ELITE_AXES: tuple[str, ...]     # DESCRIPTOR_NAMES[:3]
DEFAULT_ELITE_BINS: tuple[int, ...]     # (10, 10, 10)
    """The grid every tournament builds, and therefore the grid every stored
    elite_cell row belongs to. It is normative because save_elites persists
    cells and no header: a consumer holding only the coordinate arity has to be
    able to label the axes, and pxe.api.routes does exactly that. Ruling R98."""

# halloffame.py
class HallOfFame:
    def __init__(self, *, capacity: int = 16) -> None
    def freeze(self, harness: HarnessConfig, rating: RatingRecord) -> str    # returns harness_key
    def members(self) -> tuple[HarnessConfig, ...]

class FailureHallOfFame:
    def __init__(self, *, capacity: int = 32) -> None
        """capacity is PER harness key, so one collapsing harness cannot evict
        another's failures (ruling R101)."""
    def record(self, *, template_id: str, seed: int, harness_key: str, deficit_cents: int) -> None
    def worst(self, harness_key: str, *, limit: int = 10) -> tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]
        """(template_id, seed), worst deficit first: PRD section 8's "couples
        (scenario, seed)", which is exactly what `pxe match run` needs to replay
        a failure. The deficit that ranked them is deliberately not returned;
        it is an ordering, not a payload. Ruling R101."""

# heldout.py
class HeldoutBank:
    def __init__(self, *, path: Path, access_log: Path) -> None
    def reserve(self, *, template_id: str, count: int, base_seed: int) -> tuple[int, ...]
        """Seeds are drawn from RngTree(base_seed).child("heldout/<template>") on
        the registered tournament.heldout substream, uniformly over
        0..SEED_SPACE-1 (section 3.1). Reserving is not an access and writes no
        log entry: it creates sealed material rather than revealing it."""
    def draw(self, *, template_id: str, count: int, requester: str, purpose: str) -> tuple[int, ...]
        """Every access is logged, granted or refused. Raises HeldoutAccessError
        outside an evaluation run."""
    def access_log_entries(self) -> tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]

EVALUATION_PURPOSES: frozenset[str]
    """{"evaluation", "ab_test", "promotion_check", "final_report"}. "Outside an
    evaluation run" had no definition and `draw` receives no run object, only a
    free text `purpose`, so the seal is an ALLOW LIST and not a deny list
    (ruling R99): anything else, and "training", "iteration" and "mutation" in
    particular, is refused and logged with granted=false. A19 and A25 must use
    one of those four strings. A deny list would have made the seal advisory,
    since a new purpose string would default to allowed.

HELDOUT_BANK_VERSION: int
```

**The bank file and the access log, normatively** (ruling R100). Section 7.19
gave `HeldoutBank` two paths and no format, and did not say whether a draw
consumes a seed:

* the **bank** is one canonical JSON object, `{"version", "templates"}`, keyed
  by template id, values ascending seed lists. It is rewritten whole by
  `reserve` and never by `draw`.
* **a draw does not consume a seed** and is repeatable: two harnesses evaluated
  on "the same sealed set" must actually get the same set, or an A/B comparison
  is not one.
* an **access log entry** is `{access_index, template_id, requester, purpose,
  count, granted, reason, seeds}`, one canonical JSON line, appended and never
  rewritten. `access_index` is there because `Store.save_heldout_access` keys
  its rows by payload digest, and two identical draws are two audit events, not
  one.

**`HallOfFame.freeze(harness, rating)`** (ruling R102): a `rating.harness_key`
naming another version raises `InvalidConfigError`; re-freezing a key keeps one
slot and refreshes the rating; on overflow the weakest `mu` is evicted, so an
offer weaker than every member does not enter.

### 7.20 `pxe.store` (A21)

#### The database, decided (PRD section 10.3)

The PRD says "Postgres (metadonnees) + fichiers Parquet/JSONL". Postgres is
right for a nightly tournament and wrong for a unit test, and "no mandatory
cloud dependency in v1" is a stated PRD constraint, so the choice is:

> **SQLAlchemy 2.x Core (not the ORM session pattern) over a URL. The default
> URL is SQLite on a file; any Postgres URL is a drop in replacement. The
> schema is written once, in `store/models.py`, using only constructs both
> backends support.**

Concretely and normatively:

* `Store(url=..., runs_dir=...)`. The default is
  `f"sqlite+pysqlite:///{runs_dir / 'pxe.sqlite3'}"`, produced by
  `default_store_url(runs_dir)` so no caller spells it. `pxe tournament run`
  accepts `--db-url` and passes it through; nothing reads an environment
  variable (section 2.6).
* `store/models.py` declares the twenty-one tables of PRD section 10.4 **plus
  two infrastructure tables** as `sqlalchemy.Table` objects on a single
  `MetaData` named `METADATA`, and publishes the three name sets
  `PRD_TABLES` (21), `INFRASTRUCTURE_TABLES` (2) and `MATCH_SCOPED_TABLES` (13)
  so `test_every_prd_table_exists` fails on a missing **or** an extra table.
  The two infrastructure tables are ruling R105 and are not PRD entities:

  * `match_task`, because section 7.20 contracts `save_task(task, *, status)`
    and `pending_tasks(tournament_id)` and PRD 10.4 has no task entity. Folding
    tasks into `match` would make `GET /api/matches` list phantom matches and
    give `match` two writers. It is also the only place a match's
    `tournament_id` and `held_out` flag can live: `MatchResult` and the journal
    carry neither, so putting them on `match` would make the row
    unreproducible by `rebuild`, and `list_matches` serves both by LEFT JOIN
    (`COALESCE` to `false` for `held_out`).
  * `store_schema`, a single row holding the stamp, because
    `STORE_SCHEMA_VERSION` is "checked by init_schema" and nothing in PRD 10.4
    can hold it.

  No
  declarative base, no relationships, no lazy loading: every read in this
  project is an explicit `select()`, and an ORM identity map across a
  multiprocess tournament is a source of surprises the store does not need.
* Portability rules, because SQLite and Postgres disagree exactly here:
  identifiers are lower snake case and never quoted; the primary key of every
  table is a natural string id (`match_id`, `order_id`, ...) and never an
  autoincrement integer; a payload column is `sqlalchemy.JSON` (which maps to
  `TEXT` on SQLite and `JSONB` on Postgres) and is never queried by JSON path;
  a timestamp column, **if a table ever needs one**, is
  `sqlalchemy.DateTime(timezone=True)` and is written from the caller, never
  from a database default, because a wall clock inside the database would put a
  non reproducible value next to a reproducible one - and **no table declares
  one today**, because `test_rebuild_from_journals_reproduces_every_table`
  compares tables row for row across a rebuild and a wall clock would make that
  named contract test impossible (ruling R106);
  money is `BigInteger` cents; a probability is `Integer` ppm;
  **a seed is `BigInteger`, which is signed on both backends, and that is why
  section 3.1 bounds a seed at `2**63` and not at `2**64`.** No `ARRAY`, no
  `ENUM`, no partial index, no `ON CONFLICT` beyond
  `sqlite_on_conflict`/`postgresql_on_conflict` hidden behind `upsert()`
  below.
* `init_schema()` is `METADATA.create_all(engine)` and is idempotent. There is
  no Alembic in v1: the schema is created from scratch, and a schema change
  bumps `STORE_SCHEMA_VERSION` and requires a fresh database. That is
  acceptable because the **journal**, not the database, is the source of truth
  (section 4.1): every table here is a projection that
  `pxe store rebuild <runs_dir>` can regenerate from `runs/*/journal.jsonl`.
  `test_store.py::test_rebuild_from_journals_reproduces_every_table` is what
  makes that claim true rather than aspirational.
* Concurrency: a tournament runs matches in parallel processes and each writes
  its own artefacts, but **only the orchestrator process writes to the
  database**. Workers return `MatchResult` plus paths; the orchestrator calls
  `save_match`. With SQLite that avoids the writer lock entirely; with
  Postgres it keeps `resume()` idempotent for the same reason. `Store` is
  therefore **not** thread safe and says so in its docstring.

#### The API

```python
STORE_SCHEMA_VERSION: str          # bumped on any table change, checked by init_schema
METADATA: sqlalchemy.MetaData      # store/models.py, the twenty-one tables

def default_store_url(runs_dir: Path) -> str
    """f"sqlite+pysqlite:///{runs_dir / 'pxe.sqlite3'}". The one spelling."""

class Store:
    """Projection database plus artefact directory. Not thread safe: only the
    orchestrator process writes (see the concurrency rule above)."""
    def __init__(self, *, url: str | None = None, runs_dir: Path) -> None
    def init_schema(self) -> None
    def schema_version(self) -> str
    def close(self) -> None
    def __enter__(self) -> Store
    def __exit__(self, *exc: Any) -> None

    # --- writers, one per PRD 10.4 entity group -----------------------------
    def save_scenario_template(self, *, template_id: str, template_version: str,
                               default_ticks: int, default_markets: int) -> None
    def save_scenario_instance(self, scenario: ScenarioSpec, *, world_hash: str) -> None
    def save_harness_version(self, harness: HarnessConfig) -> None
    def save_agent(self, *, match_id: str, spec: AgentSpec) -> None
    def save_mm_config(self, *, match_id: str, config: MMConfig,
                       profile_name: str) -> None
    def save_match(self, result: MatchResult, projection: MatchProjection,
                   metrics: MatchMetrics) -> None
        """The one call that mirrors a finished match. It writes, in one
        transaction: match, tick, news_item, signal, order, trade,
        position_snapshot, prediction, resolution, settlement and
        metric_record directly, plus agent and mm_config through save_agent and
        save_mm_config. Thirteen of the twenty-one tables come from here, from
        the projection and the metrics, and never from engine state. Every one
        of the thirteen still has exactly one writing method, which is what the
        table below says. Idempotent: re run on the same match_id and nothing is
        duplicated (resume, T3.1).

        "Through save_agent and save_mm_config" is about the ROWS and not about
        the calls (ruling R107): those two methods open their own transactions,
        so save_match shares their private row builders instead of calling
        them, and both paths produce identical rows. save_agent deliberately
        does not write `cost_usd`, so a standalone `pxe match run` cannot erase
        a bill save_match recorded."""
    def save_tournament(self, config: TournamentConfig) -> None
    def save_task(self, task: MatchTask, *, status: str) -> None
    def save_ratings(self, tournament_id: str, records: Sequence[RatingRecord]) -> None
    def save_elites(self, tournament_id: str, cells: Sequence[EliteCell]) -> None
    def save_heldout_access(self, entries: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]]) -> None
    def save_incidents(self, match_id: str, incidents: Sequence[Incident]) -> None

    # --- readers -------------------------------------------------------------
    def has_match(self, match_id: str) -> bool
    def load_journal(self, match_id: str) -> tuple[Event, ...]
    def load_metrics(self, match_id: str) -> MatchMetrics
    def load_projection(self, match_id: str) -> MatchProjection
        """project(load_journal(match_id),
        incidents=self.load_incidents(match_id=match_id)). The API layer calls
        this and never `pxe.metrics.project` directly, so caching has one
        place to live and so "integrity_alert" highlights (section 7.17) have
        exactly one supplier."""
    def list_matches(self, *, tournament_id: str | None = None, limit: int = 100,
                     offset: int = 0) -> tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]
    def list_tournaments(self, *, limit: int = 100, offset: int = 0) -> tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]
    def load_ratings(self, tournament_id: str) -> tuple[RatingRecord, ...]
    def load_rating_series(self, harness_id: str) -> tuple[tuple[str, float, float, int], ...]
        """(harness_key, mu, sigma, matches) per version of one harness_id,
        ordered by the version string. This is the per version progression
        curve PRD section 9 asks the tournament view to draw, and T4.3 has no
        other source."""
    def load_elites(self, tournament_id: str) -> tuple[EliteCell, ...]
    def load_incidents(self, *, match_id: str | None = None,
                       tournament_id: str | None = None) -> tuple[Incident, ...]
        """Every returned Incident carries its `match_id` (section 7.1),
        stamped from the row it was read from. A tournament wide read spans many
        matches and section 7.21's Incident payload has a match_id, so without
        the field the API could only serve an empty string and the UI could not
        link an alert to its replay. A detector leaves the field empty; the
        store fills it. Ruling R109."""
    def load_costs_usd(self, tournament_id: str) -> tuple[tuple[str, float], ...]
        """(harness_key, cost_usd), the key TournamentDetail.costs_usd and
        ReportSections.costs_usd both expect, ascending. The only possible
        source is runs/<match_id>/llm_trace.jsonl, since cost is forbidden in a
        journal (section 3.5); save_match sums it into agent.cost_usd as it
        mirrors a match, and an all scripted tournament reports nothing at all.
        Ruling R108."""
    def load_heldout_access(self, *, limit: int = 1000) -> tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]
    def pending_tasks(self, tournament_id: str) -> tuple[MatchTask, ...]

    # --- maintenance ---------------------------------------------------------
    def rebuild(self, *, runs_dir: Path | None = None) -> int
        """Drop every projection table, recreate, and re import every
        runs/<match_id>/journal.jsonl plus metrics.json found. Returns the
        number of matches imported. The database is a projection (section 4.1),
        so this must always be possible."""

# store/files.py: the artefact side, no database
def match_dir(runs_dir: Path, match_id: str) -> Path
def write_meta(path: Path, meta: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None
def read_meta(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]
def artefact_paths(runs_dir: Path, match_id: str) -> Mapping[str, Path]
    """The six names of section 4.6, keyed by artefact name. Every reader of an
    artefact goes through this instead of joining paths by hand."""
```

#### Which writer owns which table (exhaustive, twenty-one for twenty-one)

Section 4.6 named `metrics.json` with no writer and the table list named
twenty-one entities with four writers. Both are closed here: every table below
has exactly one writing method and every method is called from a named place.

| PRD 10.4 table | Written by | Called from |
|---|---|---|
| `scenario_template` | `save_scenario_template` | `TournamentOrchestrator.__init__`, once per template id |
| `scenario_instance` | `save_scenario_instance` | orchestrator, after `generate_world` |
| `match` | `save_match` | orchestrator, after a match returns |
| `agent` | `save_agent`, once per seat | called by `save_match` inside its transaction, and directly by `pxe match run` for a standalone match |
| `harness_version` | `save_harness_version` | orchestrator on startup and `HallOfFame.freeze` (T3.4) |
| `tick` | `save_match`, from `MatchProjection` per tick series | orchestrator |
| `news_item` | `save_match`, from the journal `NewsPublished` events | orchestrator |
| `signal` | `save_match`, from `MatchProjection.signals` | orchestrator |
| `order` | `save_match`, from `MatchProjection.orders` | orchestrator |
| `trade` | `save_match`, from `MatchProjection.trades` | orchestrator |
| `position_snapshot` | `save_match`, from `PositionSnapshot` events | orchestrator |
| `prediction` | `save_match`, from `MatchProjection.predictions` | orchestrator |
| `resolution` | `save_match`, from `MarketResolved` and `MarketCancelled`, with `mode` in `{"resolution", "cancellation"}` (ruling R110: `SettlementApplied.mode`'s `"unwind"` is a settlement mode, not a resolution one) | orchestrator |
| `settlement` | `save_match`, from `SettlementApplied` | orchestrator |
| `metric_record` | `save_match`, from the `metrics: MatchMetrics` argument | orchestrator |
| `rating_record` | `save_ratings` | `TournamentOrchestrator.run` after each `RatingService.update` |
| `elite_cell` | `save_elites` | `TournamentOrchestrator._execute`, after each round's `save_ratings`. The grid is `DEFAULT_ELITE_AXES` / `DEFAULT_ELITE_BINS` and the style vector is read back from `metric_record`, so a resumed tournament fills the archive exactly as an uninterrupted one does (T5.2, ruling R98) |
| `incident` | `save_incidents` | `pxe report build` and the integrity CLI, after `run_detectors` |
| `tournament` | `save_tournament` | `TournamentOrchestrator.__init__` |
| `heldout_registry` | `save_heldout_access` | `HeldoutBank.draw` through the orchestrator (T3.5, AC-P5) |
| `mm_config` | `save_mm_config` | `save_match`, from `MatchStarted.mm_config` |
| `match_task` (infrastructure) | `save_task` | `TournamentOrchestrator`, once per planned task and again on completion |
| `store_schema` (infrastructure) | `init_schema` | `Store.init_schema`, once |

`metrics.json` on disk and the `metric_record` table hold the same numbers on
purpose: the file is what `GET /api/matches/{id}/metrics` serves (one file
read, no join) and the table is what a tournament report aggregates across a
hundred matches (one query, no hundred file reads).
`test_store.py::test_metrics_file_and_table_agree` asserts they do, which is
the only thing that keeps two representations honest.

`Store` imports `MatchTask`, `TournamentConfig`, `TournamentResult` and
`RatingRecord` from `pxe.types`, never from `pxe.tournament` (section 7,
decision 27). `EliteCell` is the one `pxe.tournament` type it imports:
behaviour free but archive shaped, so it stays with the archive that builds
it, and that single import is leftward legal.

Tests: `test_store.py::test_every_prd_table_exists` (walks `METADATA.tables`
against the list above and fails on a missing **or** an extra table),
`::test_save_match_is_idempotent`,
`::test_the_largest_legal_seed_reaches_every_seed_column`,
`::test_read_meta_reads_the_meta_the_runner_actually_wrote`,
`::test_sqlite_and_postgres_schema_are_identical`
(marked `slow`, skipped when no `PXE_TEST_POSTGRES_URL` is provided to the
**test**, which is allowed to read the environment even though the library is
not), `::test_rebuild_from_journals_reproduces_every_table`, and
`::test_metrics_file_and_table_agree`.

### 7.21 `pxe.api` (A22) - the replay API (PRD section 9, T4.1)

```python
def create_app(*, runs_dir: Path, store: Store | None = None,
               cors_origins: Sequence[str] = ("http://localhost:5173",
                                              "http://127.0.0.1:5173")) -> FastAPI
    """The one factory. When `store` is None the app builds one with
    default_store_url(runs_dir). cors_origins defaults to the Vite dev server
    of section 7.25 so A24 can work against a locally served API without
    editing A22 files."""

API_VERSION: str = "1"          # the /api prefix is unversioned; this is the payload version
```

**Three rules that make the API composable with the UI, and they are the whole
reason this section is long.**

1. **The API never recomputes engine logic.** It serves the journal, the
   projection and `metrics.json`. Every number in every payload below already
   exists in an artefact. A route that would need a new computation is a
   `pxe.metrics` change first (section 7.17), not an API change.

   Calling a `pxe.metrics` function over a projection the route already holds is
   **not** a recomputation: `reliability_curve` (section 7.17, ruling R75) and
   `verify_balance` (section 7.19, for `TournamentDetail.profile_balance`) are
   the two cases, and both are pure functions of an artefact. What rule 1
   forbids is the API deciding a price, a fill, a payout or a rank.
2. **Every payload is JSON with integer money in cents, integer prices in
   1..99, and probabilities as `*_ppm` integers.** No float crosses this
   boundary except `mu`, `sigma`, `cost_usd` and `ci_low`/`ci_high`, which are
   statistics and are documented as floats where they appear. `snake_case`
   keys everywhere, including in TypeScript: `web/src/api/types.ts` mirrors
   these names verbatim and does not camel case them, because a rename is
   exactly the kind of divergence two parallel workstreams cannot detect.
3. **Errors are a single shape.** Any 4xx or 5xx returns
   `{"error": {"code": "<RejectReason or PxeError code>", "message": "...",
   "detail": {...}}}` with the HTTP status carrying the class: `404` unknown
   match / tournament / tick, `400` a malformed query parameter, `409` an
   artefact that exists but is incomplete (a match still running), `500`
   anything else. `web/src/api/client.ts` throws one `ApiError` type built
   from that shape and nothing else.

#### REST routes

Query parameters are listed with their defaults. `from_tick` and `to_tick` are
inclusive and clamped to `1..ticks_total`; `tick = 0` is `MatchStarted` and
`tick = ticks_total + 1` is finalisation (section 4.4), and both are reachable.

| Method | Path | Query | 200 body |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/health` | | `Health` |
| GET | `/api/matches` | `tournament_id`, `limit=50`, `offset=0` | `Page<MatchSummary>` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}` | | `MatchDetail` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/events` | `from_tick=0`, `to_tick=ticks_total+1`, `types` (comma separated event type names), `limit=5000`, `offset=0` | `Page<JournalEvent>` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/ticks/{tick}` | | `TickState` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/series` | `agent_id` (repeatable), `market_id` (repeatable) | `MatchSeries` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/metrics` | | `MatchMetricsPayload` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/incidents` | | `{"incidents": Incident[]}` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/highlights` | `kind` (repeatable) | `{"highlights": Highlight[]}` |
| GET | `/api/matches/{match_id}/decisions` | `agent_id`, `from_tick=1`, `to_tick=ticks_total`, `q`, `limit=500`, `offset=0` | `Page<Decision>` |
| GET | `/api/tournaments` | `limit=50`, `offset=0` | `Page<TournamentSummary>` |
| GET | `/api/tournaments/{tournament_id}` | | `TournamentDetail` |
| GET | `/replay/{match_id}` | | the read only single page app (`text/html`) |
| WS | `/ws/matches/{match_id}` | `speed=1`, `from_tick=1` | see the protocol below |

`GET /replay/{match_id}` serves `web/dist/index.html` with no server side
rendering and no session: the share link of T4.5 is a plain URL and the app
reads `match_id` from its own path. It is read only by construction, since the
API exposes no mutating verb at all: there is no POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE
anywhere in this contract, which is the strongest possible form of "URL
autonome en lecture seule".

#### Payload shapes

Written as TypeScript because `web/src/api/types.ts` is the consumer and this
is the shape it must mirror character for character. `int` means a JSON number
that is always integral.

```ts
type Health = { status: "ok"; engine_version: string; obs_version: string;
                action_version: string; api_version: string };

type Page<T> = { items: T[]; total: int; limit: int; offset: int };

type MatchSummary = {
  match_id: string; tournament_id: string | null; template_id: string;
  template_version: string; seed: int; ticks_total: int; n_markets: int;
  n_agents: int; journal_hash: string; held_out: boolean;
  finished: boolean;                       // false while the journal has no MatchEnded
  winner_agent_id: string | null; winner_harness_key: string | null;
};

type MarketInfo = {
  market_id: string; question: string; prior_price: int; resolution_tick: int;
  status: "open" | "resolved" | "cancelled";
  outcome: "yes" | "no" | null;            // null while open or cancelled
  correlated_with: string[];               // market ids, sorted (FR-5.2.1)
};

type AgentInfo = {
  agent_id: string; harness_key: string; kind: "llm" | "scripted";
  display_name: string;                    // what the UI labels a line with
  colour_index: int;                       // 0..7, assigned by seat, see section 7.25
  info_profile: "generalist" | "specialist" | "delayed";
  ranked: boolean;                         // false for MM
};

type Ranking = {
  rank: int; agent_id: string; harness_key: string; pnl_cents: int;
  final_cash_cents: int; pnl_pct_bps: int;
};

type MatchDetail = MatchSummary & {
  config: Record<string, int | string | boolean>;   // config_to_journal_dict
  mm_config: Record<string, int | string>;
  markets: MarketInfo[];                   // ascending market_id
  agents: AgentInfo[];                     // ascending agent_id, MM last
  rankings: Ranking[];                     // ascending rank; [] while unfinished
  mm_pnl_cents: int; fees_collected_cents: int; event_count: int;
  liquidity_profile_name: "liquid" | "standard" | "illiquid";
  talking_mode: boolean;
};

// One journal line, verbatim. The envelope plus whatever the event carries.
type JournalEvent = { seq: int; type: string; match_id: string; tick: int } &
                    Record<string, unknown>;

type BookLevelDto = { price: int; qty: int };

type MarketTick = {
  market_id: string; status: "open" | "resolved" | "cancelled";
  ref_price: int; ref_source: "mid" | "last" | "prior";
  mid_price: int | null; best_bid: int | null; best_ask: int | null;
  last_price: int | null;
  bids: BookLevelDto[];                    // top 3, best first (DEPTH_LEVELS)
  asks: BookLevelDto[];
  bid_depth_qty: int; ask_depth_qty: int; tick_volume_qty: int;
  mm_bid: int | null; mm_ask: int | null; mm_widened: boolean;
};

type AccountTick = {
  account_id: string; cash_cents: int; reserved_cents: int;
  free_cash_cents: int; equity_cents: int; frozen: boolean;
  resting_order_count: int;
  positions: { market_id: string; qty: int; cost_basis_cents: int }[];
};

type NewsDto = { news_id: string; market_ids: string[]; headline: string;
                 body: string; impact: "low" | "medium" | "high";
                 is_noise: boolean; origin: "info_engine" | "resolution" | "cancellation" };

type SignalDto = { signal_id: string; agent_id: string; market_id: string;
                   kind: string; value_milli: int; precision_ppm: int };

type TickState = {
  match_id: string; tick: int;
  open_market_ids: string[];
  markets: MarketTick[];                   // ascending market_id
  accounts: AccountTick[];                 // canonical account order (section 2.3)
  news: NewsDto[];                         // publication order
  signals: SignalDto[];                    // spectator omniscient view, PRD section 9
  trades: { trade_id: string; market_id: string; price: int; qty: int;
            maker_agent_id: string; taker_agent_id: string;
            taker_side: "buy" | "sell" }[];
  predictions: { agent_id: string; market_id: string; p_yes_ppm: int;
                 carried: boolean }[];
  messages: { agent_id: string; text: string; deliver_tick: int }[];
  resolutions: { market_id: string; outcome: "yes" | "no"; payout_cents: int }[];
  highlights: Highlight[];                 // only those with this tick
};

// Every array has length ticks_total, index 0 is tick 1 (section 9).
type MatchSeries = {
  match_id: string; ticks_total: int;
  equity_cents: { agent_id: string; values: int[] }[];
  cash_cents: { agent_id: string; values: int[] }[];
  ref_price: { market_id: string; values: int[] }[];
  rank: { agent_id: string; values: int[] }[];   // live leaderboard, 1 based
};

type Highlight = { tick: int;
                   kind: "big_trade" | "early_resolution" | "integrity_alert" | "bankruptcy";
                   agent_ids: string[]; market_id: string | null;
                   magnitude_cents: int; label: string };

type Decision = {
  tick: int; agent_id: string; source: "llm" | "scripted" | "fallback";
  predictions: { market_id: string; p_yes_ppm: int; carried: boolean }[];
  orders: { op: "place" | "cancel"; market_id: string | null;
            side: "buy" | "sell" | null; type: "limit" | "market" | null;
            price: int | null; qty: int | null; order_id: string | null;
            outcome: "filled" | "partial" | "resting" | "rejected" | "cancelled";
            reject_reason: string | null }[];
  message_public: string | null;
  rationale: string | null;                // the harness extract, PRD section 9
  headline: string;                         // one line summary A22 builds for search
  timed_out: boolean; n_rejected: int;
  trade_ids: string[];                      // navigation tick <-> trade (T4.4)
};

type MatchMetricsPayload = {
  match_id: string;
  performance: { agent_id: string; pnl_cents: int; pnl_bps: int;
                 sharpe_milli: int; max_drawdown_cents: int;
                 max_drawdown_bps: int; volume_qty: int; trade_count: int;
                 maker_trade_count: int; fees_paid_cents: int }[];
  calibration: { agent_id: string; brier_ppm: int; n_terms: int;
                 n_carried: int;
                 reliability: { bin_upper_ppm: int; n: int;
                                observed_yes_ppm: int }[] }[];
  descriptors: { agent_id: string; maker_ratio_ppm: int;
                 reaction_latency_milli: int; holding_horizon_milli: int;
                 herfindahl_ppm: int; leverage_ppm: int;
                 message_intensity_ppm: int }[];
  mm_pnl_cents: int; fees_collected_cents: int;
};

type Incident = { incident_id: string; kind: string; severity: string;
                  match_id: string; tick: int; agent_ids: string[];
                  market_ids: string[]; score_ppm: int;
                  detector_version: string; detail: Record<string, unknown> };

type TournamentSummary = { tournament_id: string; format: string;
                           n_matches: int; n_harnesses: int; seeds: int[];
                           total_cost_usd: number; finished: boolean };

type TournamentDetail = TournamentSummary & {
  leaderboard: { rank: int; harness_key: string; mu: number; sigma: number;
                 matches: int; ci_low: number; ci_high: number }[];
  progression: { harness_id: string;
                 points: { harness_key: string; version: string; mu: number;
                           sigma: number; matches: int }[] }[];
  elites: { axes: string[]; bins: int[];
            cells: { coords: int[]; harness_key: string; mu: number;
                     descriptors: int[] }[] };
  costs_usd: { harness_key: string; cost_usd: number }[];
  incidents: Incident[];
  liquidity_cost_cents: { profile: string; mm_pnl_cents: int }[];
  profile_balance: { exact: boolean; max_deviation: int; seeds: int;
                     seats: int };                      // BalanceReport, section 7.19
  kpis: { name: string; value_ppm: int; unit: string }[];   // PRD section 15
};
```

Three shapes above are the answer to a specific gap and are named so nobody
drops them: `Decision` (T4.4, including `q` search and the `trade_ids` link),
`Highlight` (PRD section 9 "evenements marquants annotes", and the data behind
AC-P7), and `TournamentDetail.progression` plus `.incidents` (PRD section 9
"courbes de progression par version" and "alertes d integrite", which an
earlier draft of this table did not serve at all).

**`q` search, normatively.** `q` is a case insensitive substring match, run
over the concatenation of `headline`, `rationale` and `message_public` of each
`Decision`, after Unicode NFKC normalisation. It is not a regex, there is no
tokenisation and there is no ranking: results stay in `(tick, agent_id)`
order. A `q` longer than 200 characters is a `400`. That is deliberately the
dumbest thing that satisfies "recherche texte" and it is the same in the API
and in the UI filter box, so a user who filters client side and a user who
reloads with `?q=` see the same rows. `test_api.py::test_decisions_text_search`
pins the substring semantics and the ordering.

#### WebSocket protocol (`/ws/matches/{match_id}?speed=1..64&from_tick=n`)

Every frame is a JSON text frame with a `type` discriminator. `speed` is one
of `1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64` (any other value is closed immediately with code
`4400`) and means ticks per second. There is exactly one stream per socket and
the server is the clock.

Server to client:

| `type` | Payload | When |
|---|---|---|
| `hello` | `{match_id, ticks_total, speed, from_tick, api_version, engine_version}` | first frame, always |
| `snapshot` | `{tick, state: TickState}` | after `hello`, and after every `seek` |
| `tick` | `{tick, state: TickState}` | once per tick while playing |
| `highlight` | `{highlight: Highlight}` | interleaved, immediately before the `tick` frame it belongs to |
| `ended` | `{final_tick, rankings: Ranking[], mm_pnl_cents, fees_collected_cents}` | after the last tick |
| `error` | `{code, message}` | on a bad client frame; the socket stays open |
| `pong` | `{t: int}` | in reply to `ping`, echoing `t` |

Client to server:

| `type` | Payload | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `play` | `{}` | resume streaming from the current tick |
| `pause` | `{}` | stop streaming; the socket stays open |
| `seek` | `{tick: int}` | jump to `tick` (clamped) and send one `snapshot` |
| `speed` | `{speed: int}` | change the rate; takes effect on the next tick |
| `ping` | `{t: int}` | keepalive |

Rules that are part of the contract:

* **The stream is a projection replay, never a live match.** The journal is
  already complete when a socket opens; `speed` only paces the frames. There
  is no back pressure protocol and no partial tick: a `tick` frame carries the
  whole `TickState`, so a client that missed frames can resume from any tick
  with no state of its own. That is what makes "acces aleatoire par tick"
  (T4.1) and `seek` cheap.
* **`from_tick` and `seek` accept `0` and `ticks_total + 1`** (section 4.4), so
  a spectator can inspect the opening configuration and the finalisation.
* A `pause` followed by silence is fine: the server sends only `pong` frames
  in reply to `ping` and never closes an idle socket by itself.
* Close codes: `4404` unknown match, `4400` a bad `speed` or `from_tick`,
  `1011` server error. `web/src/api/socket.ts` reconnects with exponential
  backoff on `1006` and `1011` only, and never on a `44xx` (a client bug does
  not get retried).
* The same `TickState` type is served by `GET .../ticks/{tick}` and by the
  `tick` frame, deliberately: one renderer in the UI, one builder in the API,
  and `test_api.py::test_ws_tick_frame_equals_rest_tick` asserts the two are
  byte identical for the same tick. Without that assertion the two paths drift
  within a week.

#### Performance (T4.1: under 100 ms per request on the reference match)

`create_app` loads a match projection once and caches it per `match_id` in an
LRU of 8 entries. `TickState` for a tick is built from the cached projection,
never by re parsing the journal. `test_api.py::test_reference_match_tick_under_100ms`
(marked `slow`) asserts the median of 50 `GET .../ticks/{tick}` calls on the
6 agents by 48 ticks reference match is under 100 ms **after** the cache is
warm, and `::test_cold_projection_under_two_seconds` bounds the cold path so
the cache cannot be hiding an unusable first request.

`pxe api openapi` writes `docs/REPLAY_API.md` from the FastAPI schema, so the
route table above has a generated counterpart a UI developer can diff against.

### 7.22 `pxe.cli` (A23)

```python
def main(argv: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> int
```

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `pxe match run` | run one match, scripted or LLM |
| `pxe match verify` | run the same seed N times, compare journal hashes (AC-P1) |
| `pxe match replay <journal>` | replay a journal and print the projected metrics |
| `pxe tournament run` | run a tournament from a TOML config. `--heldout-bank <path>` and `--heldout-reserve <n>` reach the AC-P5 sealed bank, which `TournamentOrchestrator` accepts and no flag exposed before (ruling R111) |
| `pxe tournament resume` | idempotent resume, same two flags |
| `pxe report build` | build the MD/HTML report of a tournament |
| `pxe api serve` | start the replay API |
| `pxe schema dump <name>` | print a versioned schema |
| `pxe world stats <template>` | run the FR-5.2.2 frequency test |
| `pxe mm study --profile <p> --matches 200` | measure the cost of liquidity and write `docs/MM_LIQUIDITY_COST.md` (T2.6) |
| `pxe api openapi [--samples]` | write `docs/REPLAY_API.md` from the FastAPI schema, and with `--samples` one JSON fixture per payload into `web/tests/fixtures/` (section 7.25) |
| `pxe store init [--db-url]` | create the schema (section 7.20) |
| `pxe store rebuild <runs_dir>` | drop and re import every projection table from the journals |
| `pxe integrity scan <match_id>` | run the detectors offline and write `incidents.jsonl` |

Exit codes: `0` success, `1` user error, `2` determinism failure, `3` budget
exceeded, `4` invariant violation.

`argparse`'s own usage error exits `2` by default, which is this table's
determinism code, so `main` remaps it to `1` through a parser subclass
(ruling R112): a mistyped flag must never read as a broken engine.

`pxe schema dump <name>` accepts both the bare and the versioned spelling
(`observation` and `observation.v1`), since the registry keys are versioned and
the file names are not (ruling R113).

`pxe mm study` writes `docs/MM_LIQUIDITY_COST.md` by default and that document
is **its** output, not a test fixture. See ruling R96: it used to be produced by
a private test harness whose synthetic flow published no news, which is why the
committed FR-5.8.6 column read 0.000 c while the shipped command reports up to
3.146 c on the effective spread. `--out` exists so a check run can write
somewhere else, which is what
`test_market_maker.py::test_mm_liquidity_cost_doc_is_what_the_shipped_command_prints`
does.

`pxe report build` runs after the fact, in another process, and section 7.24
gives it no way to rebuild a `TournamentResult` from a `tournament_id`
(ruling R114). The CLI rebuilds one privately from `Store.list_matches` plus the
journals; the rebuilt `ScenarioSpec` omits `correlations` and `cancellations`,
because no journal event carries them and `report.py` reads neither. A
`Store.load_tournament_result` would be the clean fix and is deliberately not
added in this wave: it would be a new public reader with one caller.

### 7.23 `pxe.evolve` (A25)

```python
def collect_failures(*, harness_key: str, store: Store, limit: int = 10) -> tuple[MatchProjection, ...]
def build_patch_prompt(projections: Sequence[MatchProjection], parent: HarnessConfig) -> str
async def apropose_patch(*, parent: HarnessConfig, prompt: str, gateway_config: GatewayConfig) -> HarnessConfig
def run_ab(*, parent: HarnessConfig, child: HarnessConfig, bank: HeldoutBank,
           store: Store, n_matches: int = 20) -> tuple[RatingRecord, RatingRecord]
def should_promote(parent: RatingRecord, child: RatingRecord, *, min_mu_gain: float = 1.0,
                   max_cost_ratio: float = 1.10, parent_cost_usd: float, child_cost_usd: float) -> bool
```

### 7.24 `pxe.tournament.report` (A23)

`pxe report build` had a CLI verb and no function to call, while three product
criteria (AC-P3's "automatic report", AC-P5's separated held-out reporting,
T5.6's reliability curves) rest on it. The five sections PRD T3.6 mandates are
fields, not prose.

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReportSections:
    title: str
    held_out: bool
    leaderboard: tuple[RatingRecord, ...]                      # best mu first
    deltas: tuple[tuple[str, float, float], ...]               # harness_key, mu_delta, ci_width
        # mu_delta is mu - 25.0, the TrueSkill prior, so a positive number means
        # the harness has earned rating rather than merely been rated.
        # ci_width is the width of the 95 % percentile bootstrap_ci of the
        # harness's per match pnl_bps (PRD 7.3), drawn from metrics.bootstrap
        # off a constant root seed so two renders of one tournament agree.
        # It is deliberately NOT 2 * CI_SIGMA_MULTIPLIER * sigma: that is the
        # rating interval TournamentDetail.leaderboard serves, a different
        # quantity about a different variable, and the two are never mixed.
        # Ruling R115.
    costs_usd: tuple[tuple[str, float], ...]                   # harness_key, cost
    incidents: tuple[Incident, ...]
    liquidity_cost_cents: tuple[tuple[str, int], ...]          # liquidity profile name, MM PnL
    calibration: tuple[tuple[str, int, tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...]], ...]
        # harness_key, brier_ppm, reliability curve as (bin_upper_ppm, n, observed_yes_ppm)
    kpis: tuple[tuple[str, int, str], ...]                     # name, value_ppm, unit
    profile_balance: BalanceReport                             # section 7.19, FR-5.3.2
    brier_vs_pnl: tuple[tuple[str, int, int], ...]             # harness_key, brier_ppm, pnl_bps

def build_sections(*, result: TournamentResult, store: Store, held_out: bool) -> ReportSections
    """`leaderboard` is `result.ratings` verbatim when the report covers the
    whole tournament, and is recomputed with `RatingService` over the selected
    subset otherwise: `TournamentResult.ratings` is tournament wide, so the
    AC-P5 held-out half has no leaderboard of its own without that
    (ruling R116)."""
def render_markdown(sections: ReportSections) -> str
def render_html(sections: ReportSections) -> str
def write_report(*, result: TournamentResult, store: Store, out_dir: Path) -> tuple[Path, Path]
    """Writes report.md and report.html for the train set and, separately, for
    the held-out set (AC-P5, PRD T3.5). Returns the two train paths; the
    held-out pair sits in out_dir / "heldout"."""
```

`report.py` imports `store`, `metrics`, `latin_square`, `ratings` and `types`.
Exactly one module imports **it**, `tournament/orchestrator.py`, which calls
`write_report` at the end of `run()`: that static import is what makes T3.6's
"end of tournament, report generated with no manual action" structural rather
than aspirational, and it introduces no cycle because `report.py` imports
nothing from the orchestrator (ruling R117). An earlier draft said "nothing
imports it" and the orchestrator reached it through `importlib.util.find_spec`,
which was correct only while A23 had not landed and which hid the dependency
from every tool that reads imports.

#### The seven KPIs of PRD section 15, as data

PRD section 15 lists success KPIs that had no module, no signature and no
report section. They are `kpis`, and each is an integer so a report diff is
exact. `value_ppm` is a ppm ratio when `unit == "ppm"` and a plain integer
otherwise; the `unit` string says which, and there is no third option.

| `name` | Meaning | `unit` | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cost_per_match_usd_milli` | mean provider cost of a match, in thousandths of a dollar | `milli_usd` | `Store.load_costs_usd` |
| `cost_per_tournament_usd_milli` | total, same unit | `milli_usd` | `TournamentResult.total_cost_usd` |
| `matches_per_night` | matches completed in the run | `count` | `len(result.match_results)` |
| `clean_completion_ppm` | matches finished with no `IncidentKind.TECHNICAL` | `ppm` | `Store.load_incidents` |
| `sigma_shrink_ppm` | median `sigma` after 20 matches over the initial `sigma` | `ppm` | `Store.load_rating_series` |
| `heldout_gain_mu_milli` | cumulative held-out `mu` gain of the mutation loop | `milli` | `pxe.evolve` through the store. **`0` until A25 lands** (ruling R118): no `Store` reader exposes it and `pxe.evolve` does not exist, so both `report.py` and `api/routes.py` report `0` and report the same `0`, which is the only thing that keeps them from disagreeing. |
| `liquidity_cost_cents_per_match` | mean MM loss per match, per profile | `cents` | `liquidity_cost_cents` |

"Engagement replay" (median watch time of a shared replay) is the one PRD
section 15 KPI deliberately **not** here: it needs client side telemetry, the
API exposes no mutating verb (section 7.21) and adding one to count views
would turn a read only replay into a tracked one. It is recorded as an
explicit non goal in section 12, decision 34, rather than left looking like an
oversight.

`test_report.py::test_five_mandated_sections_present` walks the T3.6 list
(classement, deltas, couts, incidents, cout de la liquidite);
`::test_kpis_cover_prd_section_15` asserts the seven KPI names above are all
present; `::test_reliability_curves` and `::test_brier_vs_pnl_published`
cover T5.6; `::test_train_and_heldout_written_separately` covers AC-P5.

### 7.25 `web/` - the replay UI (A24, React + Vite + WebSocket)

PRD section 9 and WBS T4.2 to T4.5. This section exists so that A24 and A22
can work in parallel from this document alone: every view below names the
routes it calls and the payload types it consumes, and it names nothing else.
A24 writes no Python and A22 writes no TypeScript.

**Stack, fixed.** React 18, TypeScript 5, Vite 5, `react-router-dom` 6 for the
routes, a plain `WebSocket` (no socket.io), `vitest` plus
`@testing-library/react` for the tests. Charts are hand drawn SVG or a single
small library chosen by A24; no charting library is imposed, because none of
the payloads above needs one. No state management library: one
`replayStore.ts` built on `useSyncExternalStore` is the whole state layer.

**Three files are the only seam with the API, and no view may bypass them.**

| File | Contains | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `src/api/types.ts` | the TypeScript types of section 7.21, verbatim, `snake_case` preserved | hand written, never generated, and a change here is a contract change |
| `src/api/client.ts` | one function per REST route, returning those types, throwing `ApiError` | the only place `fetch` appears |
| `src/api/socket.ts` | `openReplaySocket(matchId, {speed, fromTick, onFrame})` | the only place `new WebSocket` appears |

`test_api.py` is A22's proof that the server keeps its half; `web/tests/api.test.ts`
is A24's proof that the client keeps its half, by validating a committed
fixture of each payload (`web/tests/fixtures/*.json`, produced once by
`pxe api openapi --samples`) against `types.ts`.

**Route map.** Six routes, all read only. Five carry a WBS task; the sixth
(`/`) is the index a share link never needs but a developer always does.

| Path | View | Consumes | WBS |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/` | `MatchListView` | `GET /api/matches`, `GET /api/tournaments` | - |
| `/matches/:matchId` | `MatchView` | `GET /api/matches/{id}`, WS `/ws/matches/{id}`, `GET .../highlights` | T4.2 |
| `/matches/:matchId/race` | `PnlRaceView` | `GET /api/matches/{id}`, `GET .../series`, `GET .../highlights` | T4.3 |
| `/matches/:matchId/decisions` | `DecisionsView` | `GET .../decisions?agent_id&from_tick&to_tick&q` | T4.4 |
| `/tournaments/:tournamentId` | `TournamentView` | `GET /api/tournaments/{id}` | T4.3 |
| `/replay/:matchId` | `SharePage` | everything `MatchView` and `PnlRaceView` use | T4.5 |

`SharePage` is not a sixth design: it is `MatchView` and `PnlRaceView` stacked
in a single scroll with the navigation chrome removed, which is what makes the
share link self explanatory. The API serves the same bundle at `/replay/{id}`
(section 7.21), so the URL is copy pasteable with no query string and no
token.

**What each view must show, from the payloads and from nothing else.**

* `MatchView` (T4.2): one panel per market, each with the `ref_price` line
  over time (from `MatchSeries.ref_price`), the simplified book (the three
  `bids` and three `asks` levels of `MarketTick`, plus a marker on `mm_bid`
  and `mm_ask`), the market question, its `prior_price`, its `resolution_tick`
  and its `status`. A scrolling news band from `TickState.news`, with
  `impact === "high"` visually distinct and `is_noise` **not** revealed. Badges
  for `TickState.signals`, shown because a replay spectator is omniscient
  (PRD section 9), and labelled as such so nobody mistakes them for something
  the agent could see at the time. Transport bar: play, pause, a tick slider
  (`seek`) and a speed selector over the seven legal `speed` values.
* `PnlRaceView` (T4.3): one line per ranked agent from
  `MatchSeries.equity_cents`, the live standings from `MatchSeries.rank`, and
  a marker per `Highlight`. The final table is `MatchDetail.rankings` and is
  never recomputed client side, which is the T4.3 acceptance criterion
  ("classement final identique au reglement comptable au centime"): the UI
  displays `pnl_cents` and `rank` as served.
* `DecisionsView` (T4.4): a row per `(tick, agent)` from `Decision`, showing
  predictions with a visible `carried` flag, orders with their `outcome` and
  `reject_reason`, `message_public`, and `rationale` in a collapsible block. A
  search box bound to `q` (debounced, then pushed into the URL so a search is
  shareable), filters on `agent_id` and the tick range, and a click on a
  `trade_id` that navigates to that tick in `MatchView`.
* `TournamentView` (T4.3): the leaderboard with `mu`, `sigma` and the
  `ci_low`/`ci_high` interval drawn as a bar, the MAP-Elites grid from
  `elites` (a cell shows `harness_key` and `mu`), the per version progression
  curves from `progression`, the integrity alerts from `incidents`,
  `liquidity_cost_cents` per profile, `costs_usd`, the `profile_balance`
  banner when `exact` is false, and the `kpis` strip.

**Eight distinguishable agents (T4.2), decided once.** `AgentInfo.colour_index`
is assigned by the API from the seat number (`A1` gets 0) and the UI maps it
through a single exported constant `AGENT_COLOURS: readonly string[]` of eight
entries in `src/components/colours.ts`. The palette is chosen to stay
distinguishable in greyscale and under deuteranopia, and every line, badge and
table row of every view reads that array. `MM` is always neutral grey and is
never given a colour index, because it is out of ranking (FR-5.8.5) and a
spectator must not read it as a competitor. `web/tests/colours.test.ts`
asserts eight entries and pairwise contrast above the WCAG AA threshold.

**Readable on a 13 inch screen (T4.2).** The layout has one hard rule: at
1280 by 800 CSS pixels, `MatchView` shows every market panel with no
horizontal scrolling and no text below 12 px, for up to five markets and eight
agents. Beyond five markets the panels collapse into a two column grid. This
is asserted by `web/tests/matchView.test.tsx` rendering at that viewport and
checking that no panel is clipped, and it is re checked by hand in the AC-P7
protocol.

**The AC-P7 protocol lives in `web/README.md`** (owner A24) and is part of the
deliverable, not a note: five people who have never seen the product open a
share link, and each must name the winner and one reason within 30 seconds.
The protocol states what is said to them, what counts as a reason, and it
records the five outcomes. `Highlight.label` exists precisely so that "one
reason" is available without reading a chart.

---

## 8. Agent interface contracts

### 8.1 Observation (PRD 6.1) - `schemas/observation.v1.json`

Engine to agent, one per agent per tick, `obs_version = "1.0"`. Target budget:
under 2000 tokens at the median tick (T2.4).

Which markets appear is section 5.0 and nothing else: a market with
`resolution_tick == r` is in the tick `r` observation with `status: "open"`,
and it is gone from tick `r + 1` onwards, where the resolution arrives as a
news item instead. `markets` may therefore legally be **empty**
(`minItems: 0` in the schema): FR-5.2.3 allows every market to have
`resolution_tick < T`, and rejecting that observation would abort a legal match
inside `validate_observation`, which section 2.4 treats as an engine bug.
`test_observation_builder.py::test_observation_valid_when_all_markets_resolved`
and `::test_market_present_on_its_resolution_tick` pin both halves.

Compaction levers, in the order they may be applied: `ref_history` length,
`body` truncation, then depth levels. **Never** compact `my_orders` or
`position_qty`: fidelity is a hard requirement.

`messages` carries other agents' text. It is untrusted data (FR-5.6.2): the
system prompt states so explicitly, the engine never interprets it, and
`sanitise_message` escapes it.

### 8.2 Action (PRD 6.2) - `schemas/action.v1.json`

Agent to engine. This exact file is passed to the Claude CLI `--json-schema`
flag, so it is deliberately strict and flat:

* `additionalProperties: false` everywhere;
* every property of an order item is in `required`, with `null` for the ones that
  do not apply (this is the shape strict structured-output engines accept
  reliably; `oneOf`/`if-then` are avoided on purpose);
* conditional semantics live in `validate_action`, table 7.14;
* the order item key is **`type`**, not `order_type`. `OrderIntent.order_type`
  is the Python spelling, `type` is the wire and journal spelling, and
  `pxe.types.order_intent_to_dict` is the one crossing (section 7.1).

**The schema is the hard ceiling; the config may only lower it.** The file is
handed verbatim to the provider CLI through `--json-schema`, so its `maxItems`
cannot depend on a `MatchConfig`. It therefore pins the ceilings and `pxe.types`
mirrors them as constants that the engine and the validator both read:

| Schema constraint | Constant | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| `orders.maxItems: 20` | `MAX_ORDERS_PER_ACTION_CEILING = 20` | `MatchConfig.__post_init__` raises `InvalidConfigError` unless `1 <= max_orders_per_action <= MAX_ORDERS_PER_ACTION_CEILING` |
| `predictions.maxItems: 8` | `MAX_PREDICTIONS_PER_ACTION = 8` | `MatchConfig.__post_init__` (`n_markets <= 8`) and `validate_action` |
| `qty.maximum: 100000` | `MAX_ORDER_QTY = 100_000` | `validate_action` (`INVALID_QTY`) and `Exchange.submit` |
| `message_public.maxLength: 280` (and the observation's `messages` item `maxLength`) | `MESSAGE_MAX_CHARS_CEILING = 280` | `MatchConfig.__post_init__` raises unless `0 <= message_max_chars <= MESSAGE_MAX_CHARS_CEILING`; `sanitise_message` truncates to `config.message_max_chars` |
| `ref_history.maxItems: 32` (observation) | `REF_HISTORY_MAX = 32` | `MatchConfig.__post_init__` raises unless `0 <= ref_history_len <= REF_HISTORY_MAX`; the observation builder echoes the config value |
| `observation limits.max_orders_per_action.maximum: 20` | `MAX_ORDERS_PER_ACTION_CEILING = 20` | the observation builder, which echoes `config.max_orders_per_action` and can therefore never advertise more than the action schema accepts |

`limits.max_orders_per_action` in the observation is therefore always `<= 20`,
and a tournament TOML asking for 40 fails at config construction rather than
telling the agent 40 and rejecting at 21. The same reasoning is why
`message_max_chars` and `ref_history_len` have ceilings: a config that raised
either one would render a payload the provider's own copy of the schema
rejects, which looks like a model failure and is a configuration bug.
`tests/test_schemas.py::test_message_max_length_equals_the_ceiling_constant` and
`::test_ref_history_max_items_equals_the_constant` pin the four numbers to the
two files. Nobody patches `maxItems` at runtime:
a mutated schema is a different schema and the provider caches it.

Truncation policy (FR-6.2.2): a partially invalid action keeps the valid items
and rejects the rest with a logged reason. A structurally invalid payload becomes
`no_action` plus a single `scope="action"` rejection.

Prediction carry (FR-6.2.4): a missing or invalid prediction for an open market
carries the last declared value, defaulting to `500_000` ppm at the first tick.
Carried values count in the Brier score and are flagged `carried: true`.

---

## 9. Metric definitions

Every metric is computed from a `MatchProjection`, itself built from the journal
alone. Nothing here may read engine state.

**PnL** (PRD section 7.1): `final_cash_cents - initial_cash_cents`, after settlement,
never marked (FR-5.5.4).
`pnl_bps = bps_ratio(pnl_cents, initial_cash_cents)`.

**How A15 gets `final_cash_cents`, since the projection has no field for it**
(ruling R70). `MatchProjection.cash_cents` comes from `PositionSnapshot`, which
P4 emits at ticks `1..T` only, while the last settlement happens at the virtual
tick `T + 1` (finalisation step 17). `cash_cents[-1]` is therefore the
**pre-settlement** cash and section 9's subtraction has no direct source in the
contracted field list. `pnl_of` derives it instead, from the two event families
that are the only ones that move cash:

```
pnl_cents = sum(own cash deltas of TradeExecuted on a market that resolved)
          + sum(net_qty_m * outcome_m.payout_cents over those markets)
```

A market with no outcome contributes exactly zero, because FR-5.4.5 unwinds
every execution on a cancelled market and refunds its fees. This is arithmetically
identical to `final_cash_cents - initial_cash_cents` and it is also an
independent second opinion on the runner's own figure, which is what makes
`test_metrics_performance.py::test_pnl_bps_equals_the_journalled_pnl_pct_bps` a
real cross check rather than a copy. Adding a `final_cash_cents` row to
`MatchProjection` would be the other fix and is deliberately not taken: it would
give one number two producers again.

`MatchRanking.pnl_pct_bps` (computed by the runner at finalisation step 18 and
journalled inside `MatchEnded.rankings`) and `PerformanceMetrics.pnl_bps`
(computed by A15 from the projection) are **the same number under two names**,
because they are the same expression over the same two integers. Two names is
one too many, and the name is kept on both because one is in the journal and
renaming it would move every golden hash; what is not optional is that they
agree. `test_metrics_performance.py::test_pnl_bps_equals_the_journalled_pnl_pct_bps`
compares them agent by agent on the reference match, and it is the only thing
that catches A09 and A15 drifting to two formulas.

Never `pnl_cents * 10_000 // initial_cash_cents`: floor division on a signed
numerator rounds toward minus infinity, so a one cent loss on 1 000 000 reports
`-1` bps while the mirror gain reports `0`, systematically overstating every
loss. `pnl_pct_bps` sits in `MatchEnded.rankings`, that is in the journal, so
the asymmetry would be frozen into the AC-P1 artefact. The same applies to
`max_drawdown_bps` and to every other signed ratio (section 2.1).

**Adjusted return**: `sharpe_milli = round_half_up(1000 * mean(d) / stdev(d))`
where `d` is the per tick equity difference series; `0` when `stdev(d) == 0`.
`stdev` is the **sample** standard deviation, `statistics.stdev`, denominator
`n - 1` (ruling R71): that is the literal spelling, and on the reference match
the choice moves `sharpe_milli` by a few units per agent, so it is pinned here
rather than left to the reader. `0` also when the series is too short to have
one.

**Max drawdown**: over the per tick equity series,
`max over t of (max over u <= t of equity_u) - equity_t`, in cents.
`max_drawdown_bps = bps_ratio(max_drawdown_cents, initial_cash_cents)`.

**Volume / trades / fees**: from `TradeExecuted` events where the agent is maker
or taker. `maker_trade_count` counts the ones where it is the maker.

**Brier** (PRD section 7.2): uniform mean of `brier_term_ppm(p, outcome)` over the
`PredictionRecorded` events, carried values included. Nothing is recomputed and
nothing is inferred: **one event, one term.** The denominator is the number of
such events for that agent, reported as `n_terms`. The mean is rounded half up
in integer arithmetic, `(2 * total + n) // (2 * n)`, so no float touches a
score.

Two boundaries "one event, one term" does not settle, both now fixed:

* **A market that never resolves produces no term** (ruling R81). A FR-5.4.5
  cancellation leaves `PredictionRecorded` rows with no `y`, so `(p - y)**2`
  does not exist for them, and they are excluded from the numerator **and** the
  denominator. The consequence has to be read into the identity below: it is
  `n_terms == sum over **resolved** markets of resolution_tick`. No shipped
  template scripts a cancellation, so nothing is observable today; A18's
  cheater bench and A23's reports are where it will be.
* **`n_terms == 0` reports `UNMEASURED_BRIER_PPM = 250_000`, not `0`**
  (ruling R80). Section 9 gave no value, and `0` would make silence the best
  possible Brier, which is a reward-hacking vector PRD 7.5 exists to close.
  `250_000` is `brier_term_ppm(DEFAULT_PREDICTION_PPM, ...)`, that is the score
  of the FR-6.2.4 default declaration: an agent that says nothing scores what a
  coin flip scores. `n_terms` sits next to it so a report can see that the
  number is unmeasured rather than earned.

`n_carried` counts the carried rows that are also **counted** terms, so
`0 <= n_carried <= n_terms` always holds (ruling R82).

That definition removes an off-by-one that would otherwise have A16 and A09
disagree on every match. By section 5.0 a market with `resolution_tick == r` is
open for the whole of tick `r` and is resolved in P1 of tick `r + 1`, so it
produces terms for ticks `1..r` **inclusive**: the resolution tick term, which
is the most informative one and the one PRD section 7.2 asks for ("a market
stops counting strictly *after* its resolution tick"), is present. For an agent
that played every tick,

```
n_terms == sum over markets of resolution_tick
```

`test_metrics_calibration.py::test_n_terms_equals_resolution_tick` pins it. A
frozen agent stops producing predictions, so its Brier is the mean over the
ticks it actually played and `n_terms` is smaller; reports normalise with it.

An agent facing **zero** open markets (legal: FR-5.2.3 lets every market have
`resolution_tick < T`) produces no `PredictionRecorded` at all that tick. The
runner still emits `ObservationBuilt` with `n_markets = 0`, skips the gateway
call entirely (so `replies == ()` and P2 emits nothing else for anybody), and
P3 has no agent block to apply. Exactly as for a frozen agent, an empty tick
costs nothing.

**Every per tick series in `MatchProjection` has length `ticks_total`**, so a
resolution never shortens an array and no consumer has to align indices.
`equity_cents` and `cash_cents` come from `PositionSnapshot`, which P4 emits for
every account on every tick whatever happens. `ref_price` comes from
`MarkToMarket`, which P4 emits for **open** markets only, so a resolved
market's series is padded forward with its last observed reference price (the
resolution payout is in `SettlementApplied`, not in a price). A15 owns the
padding; nobody else re-derives it.

**Decoupling** (AC-P4): `compute_calibration` reads only `PredictionRecorded`;
`compute_performance` reads only `TradeExecuted`, `PositionSnapshot` and
`SettlementApplied`. `tests/test_metrics_decoupling.py` mutates one family of
events and asserts the other metric is bit identical.

**Behavioral descriptors** (PRD section 7.4), all in ppm or milli so they are exact:

| Descriptor | Definition |
|---|---|
| `maker_ratio_ppm` | maker trades / total trades, `500_000` when no trade |
