| `reaction_latency_milli` | mean ticks between a `SignalDelivered` on market m and the agent's next trade on m, x1000, capped at the horizon |
| `holding_horizon_milli` | mean number of ticks a non zero position is held, x1000 |
| `herfindahl_ppm` | sum over markets of `(abs(position_notional_m) / total)^2` at the median tick |
| `leverage_ppm` | mean over ticks of `reserved_cents / cash_cents` |
| `message_intensity_ppm` | ticks with a message / ticks played |

Six descriptors, and five of the six needed a boundary the table above does not
give. All of them are now rules (rulings R77 and R78):

* **"Ticks played"** is the seat's live window: ticks `1..T`, truncated at the
  tick it was frozen if it was. It governs `leverage_ppm`,
  `message_intensity_ppm` and both duration windows. A frozen seat's silence
  after the freeze is not behaviour and must not dilute a descriptor. The freeze
  tick is read off the projection's `bankruptcy` highlights.
* **`reaction_latency_milli`**: a trade on the signal's own tick is latency
  `0`, because P1 delivers and P3 trades inside one tick; an **unanswered**
  signal contributes the horizon (the cap), not zero and not nothing; a seat
  that received no signal at all reports `ticks_total * 1000`, that is maximal
  latency, so "never reacted" and "reacted slowest" read the same way.
* **`herfindahl_ppm`**: the median tick of `1..T` is the **lower** median,
  `(T + 1) // 2`; `position_notional_m` is `abs(position_qty_m) * ref_price_m`;
  a flat seat reports `0`.
* **`leverage_ppm` and `holding_horizon_milli` are rebuilt from `OrderRecord`
  and `TradeRecord`**, because `MatchProjection` carries neither a
  `reserved_cents` series nor a per `(agent, market)` position series although
  `PositionSnapshot` journals both. The rebuild uses the section 6.1 formulas,
  which invariant I3 makes exactly equal to the ledger, and
  `test_metrics_behavioral.py` asserts that equality against **every**
  `PositionSnapshot` of all three golden journals rather than trusting it.
  Adding the two series to `MatchProjection` is the cleaner fix and is the one
  change to make if a second consumer ever needs them.
* **Only `maker_ratio_ppm` has a stated "no data" value.** The other five:
  latency is the cap (above), the remaining four are `0`.
* **Section 2.1's ppm row governs the four `_ppm` descriptors and the milli row
  the two `_milli` ones.** A `_milli` duration's natural range is
  `0..ticks_total * 1000`, up to `96_000`, so the `0..1_000_000` ppm bound does
  not apply to it.

**Cost of liquidity** (FR-5.8.5): the market maker PnL, computed like any agent
PnL but excluded from ranking and ratings, and published in every report. It is
the same `pnl_of` as a seat's, called with `MM`.

**The spread of a preset is measured on the quotes FR-5.8.4 did not widen**
(ruling R96, FR-5.8.6). `LiquidityCostRow` publishes two means and they are not
interchangeable:

* `mean_effective_spread_milli`, over **every** two sided quote. This is what an
  agent actually paid, so it is the number the cost of liquidity is read
  against, and it sits above the preset by construction whenever anything was
  widened.
* `mean_base_spread_milli`, over the quotes with `MMQuoted.widened is False`.
  **This is the FR-5.8.6 measurement.** "The mean spread of a preset is within
  one cent of the preset" is a claim about the recipe, and FR-5.8.4 deliberately
  doubles the spread for `w` ticks after a high impact news item; checking
  FR-5.8.6 over the widened ticks too makes the two requirements contradict each
  other in the same document. Measured over the shipped command on real baseline
  matches, the effective spread misses the one cent tolerance on two of the
  three presets (`standard` by 1.351 c, `illiquid` by 3.146 c) while the base
  spread is inside it on all three (0.000 c, 0.002 c, 0.016 c; the residual is
  the 1..99 price clamp).
* `widened_quotes` is published beside them, because a study where it is zero
  has not exercised FR-5.8.4 and then the two means are the same number twice.

---

## 10. Tests, markers, golden fixtures

Markers, all registered in `pyproject.toml`, strict mode on:
`slow`, `statistical`, `property`, `e2e`, `llm`, `determinism`.

Rules:

* A test that needs an LLM is marked `llm` and is **not** run in CI by default.
* A test that asserts a hash is marked `determinism` and may never be skipped.
* Statistical tests use a fixed seed and an explicit tolerance stated in a
  comment; they are marked `statistical`.
* `tests/` is a package: `tests/__init__.py` exists so that `mypy`'s
  `module = "tests.*"` override addresses it by dotted name and so that two
  workstreams writing a `helpers.py` do not collide in `sys.modules`. It holds
  a docstring and nothing else.
* `tests/conftest.py` (A01) provides the shared fixtures and **reserves ten
  names**. Nobody else defines a fixture called `repo_root`, `schemas_dir`,
  `standard_config`, `rng_tree`, `tmp_journal`, `tiny_world`, `flat_accounts`,
  `scripted_gateway`, `action_schema` or `observation_schema`.

  | Fixture | Yields | Landed |
  |---|---|---|
  | `repo_root` | the repository root `Path` | yes |
  | `schemas_dir` | `repo_root / "schemas"` | yes |
  | `standard_config` | `MatchConfig(seed=20260827)`, the PRD section 5.7 defaults | yes |
  | `rng_tree` | `RngTree(standard_config.seed)`, built by the caller (section 3.1) | yes |
  | `action_schema`, `observation_schema` | the parsed schema dicts | yes |
  | `tmp_journal` | an open `Journal` on `tmp_path/journal.jsonl` | A02 |
  | `tiny_world` | a 2 market, **24** tick `election` world, so it pairs with a legal `MatchConfig(ticks_total=24, n_markets=2)`: `MatchConfig.__post_init__` refuses anything under 24 and section 2.6 makes a disagreement fatal | A03 |
  | `flat_accounts` | a 6 seat, 5 market `AccountBook`, everything flat | A06 |
  | `scripted_gateway` | a `ScriptedGateway` over six `mute` baselines | A12, A13 |

  The four unlanded fixtures are **already written**, with the exact call they
  will make, behind `pytest.importorskip`. A test that uses one therefore
  **skips** with a named reason until its module exists, instead of erroring
  with "fixture not found". Reserving the name today is what stops
  twenty-four workstreams from inventing twenty-four spellings of "a flat
  account book"; when A06 lands, `flat_accounts` starts working everywhere at
  once and no test file changes.
* Two repository wide rules are asserted by `tests/test_style_rules.py` (A01)
  because no single workstream can hold them: contract rule 4 (the U+2014 dash
  is forbidden in produced content, with the French PRD and `docs/reviews/`
  exempt as inputs) and section 2.1's ban on the builtin `round()` inside
  `src/`. The CI `quality` job runs that file by name.
* `tests/golden/` holds three frozen journals plus their hashes. Regenerating one
  is a deliberate act that must be justified in the commit message. Exactly
  **five** kinds of change legitimately move a golden hash, and each requires a
  version bump in the same commit: a `MatchConfig` field or default
  (`ENGINE_VERSION`), an event payload or phase order (`ENGINE_VERSION`), a
  draw algorithm in `pxe.rng` (`RNG_ALGORITHM_VERSION` **and**
  `ENGINE_VERSION`), a world or info generation rule (`template_version`), or
  **a scripted baseline's policy constants (`ENGINE_VERSION`)**.

  The fifth is the one nobody expects, which is why it is listed. Section 7.15
  gives no per-baseline behavioural spec beyond six adjectives, so every edge
  threshold, order size, noise amplitude and position cap in
  `agents/baselines/*.py` is an A12 invention; it is also, and just as much as a
  `MatchConfig` default, an input to the journal of every scripted match, since
  the golden fixtures are scripted matches. Retuning a baseline to make it
  "better" moves all three hashes. See ruling R58 in section 12.2.

  A change to `pxe.runner.observation_builder` is deliberately **not** on that
  list: since `obs_hash` left the journal, the observation renderer cannot move
  a hash at all. Neither is `runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl`, which the
  runner writes when it is given an `obs_path` (section 7.12) and which is
  outside the hash by the same rule.

Anti "vacuous pass" rule: every test that asserts on a journal must first assert
that the journal is non empty and contains at least one event of the type under
test. A test that would pass on an empty run is a bug. The same rule applies to
a test that scans files: `test_style_rules.py::test_the_scan_actually_sees_files`
asserts the walk found `docs/CONTRACTS.md`, `src/pxe/types.py` and
`pyproject.toml` before any style claim is made.

#### The gate every workstream runs before pushing (T1.1)

```
make test          # the whole suite, quiet
make lint          # ruff check src tests
make format-check  # ruff format --check src tests
make typecheck     # mypy, strict, over src/pxe
make gate          # lint, format-check, typecheck and cov, in CI order
```

`make gate` runs `cov` and not `test`: `cov` is the same suite under coverage,
so running both would run every test twice.

`make` is a thin wrapper: every recipe is a single `$(PY) -m <tool>` call and
`PY` defaults to `.venv/Scripts/python.exe` on Windows and `.venv/bin/python`
elsewhere, overridable as `make test PY=python`. Nothing in the Makefile uses
a shell built-in, so the same commands work in `cmd.exe`, Git Bash and `sh`.
`.github/workflows/ci.yml` (A01) runs, in five jobs: `quality` (the four
targets above plus the style scan), `test` on **both** Linux and Windows,
`determinism-cross-platform` (which publishes the reference journal hash from
each platform as an artefact, the only place AC-P1's "two machines" clause can
actually be checked), `coverage` (publishes `coverage.xml` and `htmlcov/`,
which is T1.1's "couverture publiee"), and `web` (skipped until
`web/package.json` exists, then `npm ci`, typecheck, vitest, build).

Windows is in the matrix deliberately and not as politeness: it is the primary
development platform here and it is the only one where section 4.2's newline
trap bites.

Two anti-vacuous tests are named here because they cover boundaries no single
workstream can see:

* `test_determinism.py::test_reused_agent_objects_replay_identically` (`determinism`)
  runs two consecutive matches over the **same** scripted agent instances and
  asserts both journals equal the journals of two fresh runs. Without it,
  `ScriptedAgent.reset` (section 3.1) can be unimplemented and every single
  match test still passes, while every tournament is silently non
  deterministic.
* `test_metrics_decoupling.py::test_projection_row_types_are_populated`
  asserts every one of the five `MatchProjection` row tuples is non empty on
  the reference match. A projection that returns five empty tuples satisfies
  every type annotation A16, A17 and A18 code against.

---

## 11. Traceability

**The rule for this section: no blank cell.** Every objective, every FR, every
AC-Px and every WBS task from T1.1 to T5.6 names a module *and* a named test.
"Manual protocol" is not a test name; where a criterion genuinely needs human
judgement (AC-P7), the automated part is named separately from the human part
and both are listed. A row with a `-` in the test column is a contract bug.

### 11.1 Objectives

| Id | Objective | Module | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Total determinism | `pxe.rng`, `pxe.runner.match_runner`, `pxe.events` | `test_rng.py::test_draw_vectors_are_frozen`, `::test_shuffle_vector_is_frozen`, `test_types.py::test_match_config_is_journal_encodable`, `test_determinism.py::test_same_seed_same_hash`, `::test_reused_agent_objects_replay_identically` |
| O2 | 100 % objective evaluation | `pxe.metrics.projection`, `.performance`, `.calibration`, `.behavioral` | `test_metrics_performance.py`, `test_metrics_calibration.py`, `test_metrics_decoupling.py::test_scores_are_independent` |
| O3 | Industrial tournaments (100 matches a night) | `pxe.tournament.orchestrator`, `pxe.tournament.scheduling`, `pxe.store` | `test_tournament.py::test_nightly_plan_and_budget` (`slow`, `e2e`), `test_store.py::test_save_match_is_idempotent` |
| O4 | Demonstrated improvement loop | `pxe.evolve.mutate` | `test_evolve.py::test_promotion_rule` |
| O5 | Public replay | `pxe.api.routes`, `pxe.api.ws`, `web/` | `test_api.py::test_reference_match_tick_under_100ms` (`slow`), `web/tests/sharePage.test.tsx`, plus the AC-P7 human protocol in `web/README.md` |

**T3.2's "expected baseline order" is two claims, because the contract only
states one order.** Section 7.15 ranks none of the six baselines and the only
ordering any requirement gives is FR-5.3.1's "a Bayesian scripted agent beats a
random one", which is a relation and not a permutation. So
`test_baseline_order_recovered` recovers a **designed** four rung strength
ladder over 200 real matches, which is a claim about the rating service, and
`test_the_rating_service_recovers_the_one_ordering_the_contract_states`
recovers FR-5.3.1 on the **shipped** baselines over 60 seat-rotated matches,
which is a claim about the arena. Neither substitutes for the other, and the
second exists because the first alone never touches
`pxe.agents.baselines`.
| T3.3 multi-seed aggregation | A19, A20 | `tournament/orchestrator.py`, `tournament/ratings.py` (`bootstrap_ci`) | `test_tournament.py::test_min_three_seeds`, `test_ratings.py::test_bootstrap_ci_is_seeded_and_stable` |
| T3.4 Hall of Fame | A20 | `tournament/halloffame.py`, `types.harness_config_hash` | `test_elites.py::test_frozen_harness_replays_identically`, `test_types_boundaries.py::test_harness_config_hash_is_filled_and_stable`, `::test_harness_key_separates_two_prompts_under_one_version` |
| T3.5 held-out bank | A20 | `tournament/heldout.py`, `store/db.py` (`save_heldout_access`) | `test_heldout.py::test_access_is_logged_and_sealed`, `::test_sealed_seeds_are_never_drawn_for_training` |
| T3.6 automatic reports | A23 | `tournament/report.py`, `cli.py` | `test_report.py::test_five_mandated_sections_present`, `::test_kpis_cover_prd_section_15`, `::test_train_and_heldout_written_separately`, `::test_reliability_curves`, `test_cli.py::test_report_build_writes_both_formats` |

---

## 12. Decisions a reviewer should challenge

These are the judgement calls made here that are not literally dictated by the
PRD. Each is cheap to change **now** and expensive to change once 25 agents have
built on it.

### 12.0 Overrides of the PRD (rule 1), stated as overrides

Rule 1 of this document says the PRD wins on any functional disagreement, and
that this document must be amended in the same commit. Three of the decisions
below are not clarifications: they are **functional overrides**, and burying
them in a numbered list let an implementer read the PRD (which rule 1 tells
them to prefer) and build a different system. They are therefore lifted out
here, each with the PRD sentence it overrides and the reason, and the
implementation follows **this table**, not the PRD text, for these three
points and only these three.

The PRD file itself is an input artefact of this repository and is not edited
here (it is the signed off French specification and it is quoted, never
rewritten). This register is the amendment rule 1 asks for, and it is the one
place a reader has to check.

| # | PRD text overridden | What is built instead | Reason | Reversible? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| O-1 | PRD section 5.7: "Cash initial \| 10 000 \| fixe par tournoi", with no unit | `initial_cash_cents = 1_000_000`, that is 10 000 **contract units** | Read as cents, an agent could buy at most 100 contracts at 100 c, while the standard market maker quotes 25 per side with `I_max = 300`. The arena degenerates: nobody can move a price and every match is a draw at the prior. The PRD gives no unit, so this is the only reading under which its own MM presets (section 5.8.6) are usable. | Yes, one field, but it moves every golden hash |
| O-2 | FR-5.5.1: "vente (y compris a decouvert) a p c -> (100 - p) x qte" | An **order** reserves `(100 - p) x qty`; an open **short position** reserves `100 x qty` | The two are the same thing measured at two moments. On fill the seller receives `p x qty` in cash, so free cash moves by exactly `-(100 - p) x qty` under both readings; the escrow on the position must be the full payout because that is what the position can cost. Taking FR-5.5.1 literally on the position leg under collateralises every short by `p x qty` and lets an agent go cash negative on a YES resolution, which FR-5.5.5 forbids. This override makes FR-5.5.1 and FR-5.5.5 consistent rather than choosing between them. | No: the alternative is unsound |
| O-3 | PRD section 7.2: the Brier is a "moyenne uniforme ... sur {ticks ou le marche est ouvert} x {marches}" | A frozen (bankrupt) agent stops producing terms; its Brier is the mean over the ticks it actually played, and `n_terms` is published beside it | Taken literally, a bankrupt agent keeps being asked for free predictions it cannot act on and can farm a good Brier while unable to trade, which is precisely the reward hacking PRD section 7.5 exists to prevent. The mitigation is not a hidden one: `CalibrationMetrics.n_terms` is in every report and in `GET /api/matches/{id}/metrics`, so two agents with different `n_terms` are visibly not comparable. | Yes: call frozen agents for predictions only |

Nothing else in the list below contradicts the PRD. Everything else fills a
gap the PRD leaves open (a signature, an ordering, a data shape) or records a
choice the PRD explicitly delegates.

### 12.1 The full list

1. **`initial_cash_cents = 1_000_000`.** The PRD says "Cash initial 10 000"
   without a unit. Reading it as 10 000 cents would cap an agent at 100 contracts
   while the standard market maker quotes 25 per side with `I_max = 300`, which
   would make the arena degenerate. It is read as 10 000 contract units.
   **This is a PRD override: see section 12.0, row O-1.**
2. **No floats in the journal at all.** Probabilities become `*_ppm` integers and
   latent values `*_milli` integers. This makes AC-P1 structurally safe rather
   than dependent on float repr stability, at the cost of a conversion at the
   agent boundary and slightly coarser Brier arithmetic (exact to 1e-6).
3. **Position collateral for a short is `100 * qty`, not `(100 - p) * qty`.**
   The `(100 - p)` form is the *incremental free cash* effect and applies to
   resting orders; once filled, the seller already holds the `p * qty` in cash,
   so the escrow is the full payout. The two together reproduce FR-5.5.1 exactly.
   **This is a PRD override: see section 12.0, row O-2.**
4. **No netting between a resting sell and an existing long.** Selling 5 while
   long 5 still reserves `(100 - p) * 5`. This is over-conservative but matches
   FR-5.5.2's "sum of worst cases" and is far easier to prove correct.
5. **All-or-nothing rejection per order intent**, not partial acceptance, on a
   collateral shortfall. The alternative (shrink the quantity to what is
   affordable) is friendlier to agents but adds a second, silent way for an order
   to change size.
6. **"Atomically" in P3 means "not interleaved with another agent"**, not
   "all-or-nothing". A single bad intent inside a block does not void the block.
7. **A market order reserves collateral at its band converted price** before
   matching, even though it may fill cheaper. Simple and safe, slightly
   restrictive at the margin.
8. **The action schema is flat with nullable fields** rather than `oneOf`
   discriminated on `op`. Strict structured-output engines handle
   `additionalProperties: false` + all-keys-required + nullable types far more
   reliably than `oneOf`/`if-then`. Conditional semantics moved into Python.
9. **Frozen (bankrupt) agents are not called at all** and stop producing
   predictions, so their Brier is a mean over fewer terms. The alternative
   (keep asking them for free predictions) would let a bankrupt agent farm a good
   Brier while unable to trade. **This is a PRD override: see section 12.0,
   row O-3.**
10. **Integrity incidents are not in the match journal.** Detectors run offline
    over a finished journal so a detector version bump never changes a journal
    hash. The engine only journals `IncidentKind.TECHNICAL`.
11. **Observations are neither journalled nor hashed into the journal.** They
    are pure projections; the full payloads, their digest and their byte size
    all go to `observations.jsonl`. The earlier version of this decision kept
    `obs_hash` and `obs_bytes` inside `ObservationBuilt`, which was
    self-defeating: the journal *is* the hash, so any compaction lever A10
    touched (one more depth level, a shorter `body`, a renamed key) moved every
    golden hash, and the digest was taken over `stable_json`, the one encoder
    that accepts floats. That put a presentation module inside AC-P1 and routed
    floats into the hash chain, for no engine benefit: with scripted agents the
    observation content has zero effect on any engine decision. The event still
    carries `agent_id`, `obs_version`, `n_markets`, `n_news` and `n_signals`,
    which are engine facts a replay needs to prove no agent was skipped.
12. **Resolution outcomes are published as a HIGH impact news item**, which means
    the market maker widens after every resolution (FR-5.8.4). That is arguably
    desirable (a resolution is a real information event) but it is a choice.
13. **`ObservationBuilt` and per tick observations are skipped for frozen
    agents**, so an LLM match costs strictly less after a bankruptcy.
14. **One provider call per agent per tick covering all markets**, never one call
    per market. This is a cost decision (about 0.027 USD and 6 s per call, about
    35k cached input tokens of CLI overhead) and it shapes the observation and
    action schemas.
15. **The synchronous bridge lives in the gateway** (`collect_actions` runs
    `asyncio.run` over `acollect_actions`), so the runner stays pure and
    synchronous per FR-5.1.2 and the PRD's architecture principle 3.
16. **`mm_initial_cash_cents` defaults to 10x an agent's cash** so the market
    maker is never the binding liquidity constraint. It lives on `MatchConfig`
    and nowhere else, `__post_init__` enforces
    `mm_initial_cash_cents >= initial_cash_cents`, it enters invariant I2 and
    it is journalled, so `mm_pnl_cents` (FR-5.8.5, the published cost of
    liquidity) is recomputable from the journal alone.

17. **`MatchConfig` is float free by rule, and the timeout lives in
    `GatewayConfig`.** An earlier draft carried `agent_timeout_s: float = 60.0`
    on `MatchConfig`, which crashed `canonical_json` on the first event of
    every match and, had it not crashed, would have made the journal hash
    depend on a wall clock setting. Section 2.6 states the rule and two tests
    enforce it. `MatchConfig.seed` defaults to `0` so `MatchConfig()` is
    constructible in those tests.

18. **`pxe.rng` implements its own permutation and distributions on
    `getrandbits`.** The alternative was to keep using `random.Random.shuffle`
    and `.gauss` and to caveat AC-P1 down to "one platform". Owning about 150
    lines of Fisher-Yates, Acklam inverse CDF, Marsaglia-Tsang gamma and fixed
    order `log`/`exp` buys back the "two machines" clause of AC-P1 and makes
    the algorithm versionable by us (`RNG_ALGORITHM_VERSION`). The cost is that
    those routines are ours to test; `test_rng.py` pins their output vectors.

19. **The bankruptcy predicate is the PRD's, not an equity floor.** FR-5.5.5
    says "cash et collateral libres epuises", so the reachable branch is
    `free_cash_cents <= floor and resting_order_count == 0`. Keeping only
    `equity_cents <= 0` (the earlier draft) made the whole freeze path
    unreachable, because I4 and I9 force `equity >= free_cash >= 0`. The equity
    floor survives as a second, defensive branch. Note the consequence: an
    agent that deploys its entire capital and leaves nothing on the book *is*
    frozen, permanently. That is harsh, and it is what the PRD says; the
    resting order clause is the only softening (an agent that can still cancel
    something is not stuck).

20. **STP emits `OrderCancelled` then `STPCancelled`, and only the first
    carries money.** The alternative (money on `STPCancelled`, no
    `OrderCancelled`) would leave an order leaving the book without the event
    every projection uses to reconstruct the book. Carrying it on both is the
    double count this decision exists to prevent.

21. **Collateral freed by an STP cancellation is not available to the order
    that caused it.** The pre-trade solvency check runs first. The opposite
    choice is defensible (it is friendlier to an agent flipping a position) but
    it must be *a* choice, because the two readings produce different journals
    from the same seed.

22. **A market cancellation refunds the taker fees paid on that market.**
    FR-5.4.5 says the cancellation "restitue le cash", and fees are cash. This
    forces `AccountBook` to track `fees_paid_cents` per `(account, market)`,
    which `PerformanceMetrics.fees_paid_cents` wanted anyway. With the default
    `taker_fee_bps = 0` the difference is invisible, which is exactly why it is
    decided now rather than discovered later.

23. **AC-P1 is scoped to scripted matches** (section 3.7). An LLM match cannot
    be re-run to the same journal, because `AgentTimedOut` is a wall clock
    decision that is journalled. What is guaranteed for an LLM match is replay
    of the recorded journal. The README used to promise more than that.

24. **The harness key carries the config hash: `<harness_id>@<version>+<config_hash[:8]>`.**
    Section 2.2 previously said `<harness_id>@<version>` "assigned by gateway",
    which was wrong twice over. It was wrong about the producer (no function in
    `pxe.gateway`'s public API ever built it, while four workstreams consumed
    it, so three key builders would have been written), and it was wrong about
    the content: T3.4 requires frozen versions to be identified by
    "hash config+prompt", and two different system prompts shipped under the
    same `version` tag would otherwise share one TrueSkill rating, one
    MAP-Elites cell and one Hall of Fame slot. Both `harness_key` and
    `harness_config_hash` now live in `pxe.types` and `HarnessConfig` fills
    `config_hash` in `__post_init__`, so a journal never carries an empty hash.

25. **A market is tradable on its own resolution tick and resolves in P1 of the
    next one (section 5.0).** The earlier reading (resolve at the start of tick
    `r`) had three heads: the market was untradable on the tick everyone cares
    most about, its Brier term (the most informative one, and the one PRD
    section 7.2 asks for) was never recorded, and with the PRD default
    `resolution_tick == T` for every market, tick `T` produced an observation
    with an empty `markets` array that the schema then rejected, which section
    2.4 turns into an aborted match. The cost of the new reading is a virtual
    tick `T + 1` on which finalisation settles; that is cheap, explicit, and it
    makes `n_terms == sum of resolution_ticks` a one line check.

26. **The gateway returns `AgentReply`, and validation lives in the runner.**
    `collect_actions` used to return `AgentAction`, which implied validation
    inside an impure module with neither `open_market_ids` nor
    `resting_order_ids`, discarded the `Rejection` list FR-6.2.2 needs, and left
    `AgentTimedOut.reason` with nowhere to come from. `AgentReply` also keeps
    the non journalled facts (raw text, cost, latency, tokens, attempts) in one
    typed place instead of scattering them, and `AgentReply.raw` being always
    the `schemas/action.v1.json` shape means A11 handles one input shape rather
    than "a dict from A14 or an object from A12".

27. **The four tournament data carriers live in `pxe.types`.** `MatchTask`,
    `TournamentConfig`, `TournamentResult` and `RatingRecord` are pure data that
    `pxe.store` persists and `pxe.tournament` consumes, so defining them in
    `pxe.tournament` made the two packages import each other, which the section
    7 arrow forbids and which `evolve` turned into a three-way tangle. Behaviour
    stays in `pxe.tournament`. The alternative (a fifth package for shared
    tournament types) buys nothing: `pxe.types` is already the place everyone
    imports leftward from.

28. **The market maker receives an `InventoryView`, not an `AccountBook`.**
    FR-5.8.3's cap and skew are expressed on inventory, which lives in the
    ledger; `requote` had no accounts handle at all, so A07 would have reached
    into a private attribute or kept a second inventory count that drifts from
    A06's and breaks I10. Passing the whole `AccountBook` would have worked but
    hands a pure quoting component a mutation handle and makes `pxe.mm` import
    `pxe.exchange.accounts`. The frozen snapshot lives in `pxe.types`, so
    neither module imports the other.

29. **`Oracle.resolve` owns P1 step 4 sub-steps a, b, c and e; the runner owns
    only d.** Written as five runner bullets *and* as an `Oracle.resolve`
    returning `cancelled_order_ids` and `settlements`, the step had two literal
    implementations and produced two `cancel_all` passes and two
    `MarketResolved` events. The runner keeps sub-step d (the resolution news)
    because the item comes from the info engine, which the oracle does not hold,
    and because it must join the tick's other news in the single
    `mm.note_news` handover of step 5b.

30. **Every event has exactly one emitter, listed in section 4.5.** Three
    places in this document previously gave one event two owners
    (`SettlementApplied` to both `AccountBook` and `settlement.settle`,
    `MarketResolved` to both the oracle and the runner, the resolution
    `NewsPublished` to nobody in particular). `AccountBook` therefore holds no
    `Journal` at all, which is the structural version of the rule rather than a
    promise.

31. **The three PRD overrides are registered in section 12.0, not hidden in
    this list.** Decisions 1, 3 and 9 are functional deviations, not
    clarifications, and rule 1 says the PRD wins. Since the PRD file is an
    input artefact this repository does not edit, the amendment rule 1
    requires takes the form of the override table in section 12.0: it names
    the exact PRD sentence, the replacement, the reason and whether it is
    reversible. An implementer who reads only the PRD and only this list now
    has one table to check instead of three scattered bullets.

32. **Persistence is SQLAlchemy 2.x Core over a URL, SQLite by default,
    Postgres by substitution.** The PRD names Postgres. Requiring a running
    Postgres to execute `test_store.py` would make the T1.1 gate depend on a
    service, and PRD section 10.3 also says "pas de dependance cloud
    obligatoire en v1". Core and not the ORM session pattern, because every
    read here is an explicit `select()` and an ORM identity map across a
    multiprocess tournament buys nothing. No Alembic in v1: the database is a
    projection of the journals and `pxe store rebuild` regenerates it, which
    `test_store.py::test_rebuild_from_journals_reproduces_every_table` proves.
    The cost is that a schema change needs a fresh database; the benefit is
    that a schema change cannot corrupt a source of truth, because the
    database is not one.

33. **The UI directory is `web/`, and the replay API is read only by
    construction.** The directory is named after the PRD module (`web-ui`) and
    an earlier draft called it `ui/`; every reference is corrected, including
    `.gitignore`, the ruff `extend-exclude` and the CI job. Read only by
    construction means the API contract of section 7.21 contains **no** POST,
    PUT, PATCH or DELETE at all. That is a stronger guarantee than "the share
    URL is read only" (AC-P7, T4.5) and it is asserted by
    `test_api.py::test_api_exposes_no_mutating_verb`. It also settles a
    question that would otherwise be answered differently by A22 and A24: no,
    the UI cannot bookmark, annotate or vote on a replay in v1.

34. **PRD section 15's "engagement replay" KPI is deliberately not
    implemented.** Median watch time of a shared replay needs client side
    telemetry and a write endpoint to receive it, and decision 33 makes the
    API read only on purpose. Adding a view counter would turn an anonymous
    read only share link into a tracked one, which is a product change and not
    a metric. The other seven KPIs of PRD section 15 are implemented as
    `ReportSections.kpis` (section 7.24). This is recorded as a decision
    rather than left as a gap so that nobody rediscovers it as a bug.

35. **FR-5.3.2's "equal number of times" degrades to a measured deviation, and
    an imperfect square is not an error.** With three profile kinds, 4 to 8
    seats and the PRD default of 3 seeds, exact balance is arithmetically
    impossible for seat counts that are not multiples of three, so
    `verify_balance` returns a `BalanceReport` and never a bare `False`. The
    tournament runs, and the report prints the residual deviation. The
    alternative (raise, or silently pretend) either fails AC-P3 on the PRD's
    own defaults or hides a real imbalance from the reader of a leaderboard.
    Section 7.19 states the expansion, the rotation and the exact divisibility
    condition. What still raises is a seed count below three, which the PRD
    does state as a floor.

36. **The number of markets is a parameter in exactly three places and the
    runner refuses a disagreement.** PRD section 5.7 makes it a tunable range
    (2 to 8, default 5) and it was a template constant nowhere in a signature,
    which made FR-5.2.1's range untestable. It now lives on `MatchConfig`
    (journalled, authoritative), on `generate_world` / `WorldTemplate.build`,
    and implicitly as `len(ScenarioSpec.markets)`; `MatchRunner.__init__`
    raises when they disagree (section 2.6). A template may legitimately
    refuse a count outside its own range, and it raises rather than clamping,
    because a silent clamp produces a world whose market count contradicts the
    journalled config.

37. **A private signal about a market that is not tradable this tick is
    dropped by the runner, after the draw.** `InfoProfile.delay_ticks` makes a
    late signal about a matured market routine. Delivering it hands the agent
    a `market_id` absent from its own `markets` array and leaves
    `reaction_latency_milli` undefined; filtering it inside `InfoEngine`
    instead would make a pure component read live market status and would make
    the RNG consumption of `info.signals.tick.<tick>` depend on resolution
    history, breaking FR-5.1.4. Dropping after the draw, in the runner, keeps
    the info stream a pure function of `(world, profiles, seed, tick)` and
    costs one filter (section 5, P1 step 3).

38. **Exhibition matches do not move ratings, and Swiss byes rotate.** PRD
    section 8 lists exhibition as a format without saying whether it counts.
    Letting a curated novelty match move a TrueSkill leaderboard would make
    the ranking depend on who was chosen as the field, which is the one thing
    a rating is supposed to be robust to. Byes are the other half of the same
    problem: with a population that is not a multiple of `agents_per_match`
    somebody sits out every round, and a fixed bye order would systematically
    starve the tail of the standings of matches (and therefore of sigma
    shrinkage). Both are asserted, in
    `test_tournament.py::test_exhibition_does_not_move_ratings` and
    `::test_swiss_byes_rotate`.

---

### 12.2 Wave-1 integration rulings (A02..A13)

The twelve work packages of wave 1 were built in parallel against sections 4 to
7, and each one reported where those sections were silent, ambiguous or self
contradictory. The **contradictions** were fixed in place, in the section that
owned them: sections 5.0, 5 P1 step 3, 5 P3 step 9, 6.2 and 6.4 each carry a
paragraph saying what changed and why. What follows resolves everything that was
merely **unspecified**, so that a choice an implementer had to make is now a rule
and the next reader does not make a different one.

Each ruling names the section it belongs to. A ruling that moves a journal byte
says so, because those are the ones that cannot be revisited without
regenerating `tests/golden/`.

#### Section 7.4, `pxe.journal` (A02)

**R1. `StoreError` is the journal's misuse error.** `Journal.__init__` raises it
for `buffer_size < 1`, and every method raises it after `close()`. Neither case
is an agent behaviour nor a hash failure, so `JournalHashMismatchError` would be
the wrong code. `close()` is idempotent.

**R2. An event carrying a foreign `match_id` raises `JournalHashMismatchError`,
exactly like a `seq` gap.** `append` checks both halves of the envelope. Writing
an event of match B into the journal of match A corrupts two hashes at once,
which is the same class of failure as a `seq` gap.

**R3. `verify_journal` performs exactly these six checks, in order:** the
sequence is non empty; every event carries one and the same `match_id`;
`seq == index + 1`; `tick` is non decreasing; `MatchStarted` appears only at
index 0 with `seq == 1` and `tick == 0`; `MatchEnded`, if present, is last. It
does **not** require `MatchStarted` or `MatchEnded` to be present, so a journal
flushed mid match still verifies. It **raises** on an empty sequence: "the
journal of a match that never started" is a caller bug, not a valid input. Tick
ordering is checked here and not in `append` precisely because a mid-match
journal is legitimately incomplete.

**R4. `filter_events(events)` with no type returns `()`.** The other reading
("no filter, therefore everything") fails silently for a caller doing
`filter_events(events, *maybe_empty_list)`, which is the only way the zero
argument case arises in practice.

**R5. `iter_ticks` yields one entry per distinct tick, ascending, merging a tick
that appears in two non contiguous runs.** Order inside a tick is preserved. For
any journal satisfying section 4.4 the merging and run-splitting readings
coincide, so this only pins the behaviour on a malformed journal.

**R6. No public name in 7.4 appends to an existing journal file.** `Journal`
always opens `"w"`; a journal is written once, from `seq` 1. Section 4.2's
mention of mode `"a"` states the `open()` arguments a writer must honour **if**
it appends, not an entry point that exists today. A resume story (A21's
`ResumeError`) that needs to reopen a journal is a new entry point in 7.4 and a
contract change, not a use of the current one.

**R7. `Journal` does not validate `match_id` against `RE_MATCH_ID`.** Several
packages hand it toy ids in unit tests. This is the only such hole and it is
closed downstream: `MatchRunner.__init__` refuses a journal whose `match_id`
disagrees with its own, so nothing reaches a real journal without that check.

#### Section 7.5, `pxe.world` (A03)

**R8. `World` is declared in `world/templates/base.py` and re-exported by
`world/generator.py`.** 7.5 lists the class under `generator.py`, but a template
must construct a `World` and `generator.py` must import the templates for its
registry, which is a cycle; ruff `PLC0415` closes the lazy-import escape.
`generator.py` names `World` in its `__all__`, so
`from pxe.world.generator import World` is unchanged and legal under
`no_implicit_reexport`. Only the file map was off by one level.

**R9. `WorldTemplate.default_markets` is declared metadata and `generate_world`
never consults it.** The literal signature is `n_markets: int = 5` with no
`None`, so no code path exists on which a default could apply. It stays on the
protocol because a report and the CLI display it.

**R10. The news calendar is drawn from `world.template`.** Section 3.3 registers
no `world.news_plan` substream and `World.news_plan` is A03 output, so the plan
comes out of the template's own substream. Adding a dedicated name later moves
every golden hash.

**R11. `outcome_frequency` draws seeds `base_seed + i` for `i` in
`0..draws - 1`.** The function returns frequencies only, so a caller checking
FR-5.2.2's convergence must reproduce that sequence to compute the reference
latent probabilities. The sequence is therefore contractual, not an
implementation detail, and `pxe world stats` (section 7.22) depends on it.

**R12. Each template declares its own legal market range and `generate_world`
raises `InvalidConfigError` outside it:** `election` 2..8, `harvest` 2..6,
`league` 3..8. FR-5.2.1's "2 to 8" is the range of the **feature**, not a promise
that every template spans it: a league table cannot be expressed with two teams.
A consumer sweeping market counts reads `min_markets` and `max_markets` off the
template rather than assuming 2..8.

**R13. No shipped template produces a `ScenarioSpec.cancellations` entry.** A
scripted cancellation is a scenario-script intervention (FR-5.4.5) and none of
the three worlds scripts one, so the FR-5.4.5 unwind path is reachable only from
a hand built `ScenarioSpec`. `test_oracle.py`, `test_settlement.py` and
`test_match_runner.py` each build one rather than expecting a generated world to
contain one.

**R14. `LatentProcess.value_milli` clamps a tick above the stored horizon to the
terminal value and raises `InvalidConfigError` on a negative tick.** The clamp is
what lets the oracle resolve at the virtual tick `T + 1` without a special case.
`generate_world` accepts `ticks_total >= 2`; the 24 tick floor belongs to
`MatchConfig` alone, so a unit test can build a small world without a legal match
config. The latent-to-probability map is documented in `world/latent.py` and is
integer only (no `libm` `exp` or `sin`, section 3.2); A04's signals and A08's
`latent_value_milli` read that same map.

#### Section 7.6, `pxe.info` (A04)

**R15. `SignalKind.THRESHOLD` carries the level in its magnitude and the
direction of the bound in its sign.** `Signal.value_milli` for a `THRESHOLD` is a
probability level in thousandths; a non negative value asserts "the probability is
**at least** this", a negative value asserts "**at most** this". This mirrors
`DIRECTION`, whose sign is also its meaning. The implied probability a consumer
reads off it is the midpoint of the asserted half. Both halves of the channel are
pinned against this sentence: `pxe.info.noise.threshold_milli` produces it and
`pxe.agents.base.signal_evidence` consumes it. Without a written convention the
producer and the consumer can disagree silently while both packages' own tests
pass, which is the failure this ruling exists to prevent.

**R16. `Signal.value_milli` is not on the latent scale.** Section 2.1 defines
`_milli` generically as "latent/signal value, thousandths, signed", but a
`POINT_ESTIMATE` value is a **probability** in thousandths, which is what the
observation schema's `[0, 1]` range mirrors. A signal value never carries a raw
latent quantity.

**R17. `precision_ppm` is `s / (s + sigma)` with `s = LATENT_SCALE_MILLI`, and
`pxe.info.noise.sigma_milli_for_precision` is its exact inverse.** Both names are
public. A Bayesian consumer needs the inverse to turn a self declared precision
back into a variance and weight the signal (FR-5.3.1); with only the forward
direction published, the information channel is unusable by the agent it exists
for.

**R18. `InfoEngine` self caps at 12 news items per tick (`NEWS_MAX_PER_TICK`).**
`schemas/observation.v1.json` caps `news` at 20, and the runner appends one item
per market resolved (P1 step 4d) and cancelled (step 5) into the same array, so
the engine may not spend the whole budget: 12 leaves 8 for the resolutions of a
maximal 8 market world. The split is contractual so A09 and A10 can rely on it
instead of each capping defensively.

**R19. `news_for_tick`, `signals_for_tick` and `high_impact_market_ids` raise
`InvalidConfigError` for `tick < 1` and return empty for `tick > ticks_total`.**
Tick 0 is `MatchStarted` and has no diffusion; a tick past the horizon is
finalisation, which publishes no news.

**R20. `build_profile` raises `InvalidConfigError` when `focus_market_id` is
passed for a kind that is not a specialist.** A focus market on a generalist is
silently meaningless, and silently meaningless is how a Latin square assigns a
profile nobody notices is wrong.

#### Sections 7.7 and 7.8, `pxe.exchange` (A05)

**R21. `Exchange` is defensive at the P3 boundary and never raises on a
malformed intent.** Section 7.14 says every `RejectReason` member has exactly one
producer, and it already gives `UNKNOWN_ORDER` two (the P2 table and the P3
stale-cancel rule). The rule that wins is section 2.4's: agent misbehaviour is
never an exception at the engine boundary. So a structurally malformed `place`
intent that `validate_action` should have dropped is refused with
`MISSING_FIELD`, `INVALID_QTY` or `INVALID_PRICE`, and a `market_id` absent from
the scenario with `UNKNOWN_MARKET`, rather than aborting the match. None of these
fires when A11 is correct, so no journal moves; the alternative is a match that
dies on a validator bug instead of journalling it.

**R22. `OrderRejected.price` for a rejected `market` order is the band converted
price when the rejection happens after the conversion, and `intent.price`
otherwise.** A market intent carries no price, but the collateral check and the
order cap both run against the FR-5.4.3 band converted price, so that is the
number that explains the rejection. Concretely: `ORDER_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and
`INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` carry the band price; everything decided before the
conversion carries `intent.price`, which is `null` for a market order. **This is
a journal byte.**

**R23. `OrderPlaced.active_orders_after` counts the accepted order plus the
orders resting at acceptance, before any STP cancellation the same submission
triggers.** The field exists to show the FR-5.4.2 cap being respected, so it is
always `<= config.max_active_orders_per_market`. The alternative (count after the
STP block) makes the field smaller than the cap it is there to evidence. **This
is a journal byte.**

**R24. `MarketState.outcome` as returned by `Exchange` is always `None`.**
`close_market(status=...)` never receives an outcome and section 7.8 forbids the
exchange from learning one, so the field cannot be filled there. A consumer reads
`MatchState.outcomes`, which the oracle fills. The field stays on the carrier
because the same type is used elsewhere.

**R25. `close_market` on a market that still has resting orders raises
`InvariantViolationError`.** P1 step 4a mandates `cancel_all` first, and that
ordering is the only one keeping I4 true throughout the resolution. A silent
close would leave collateral reserved against orders that no longer exist.

#### Section 7.9, `pxe.exchange.accounts` and `.fees` (A06)

**R26. `release_order(*, account_id, order, released_qty)` receives the order
**before** the release is applied.** `order.remaining_qty` is therefore the
pre-release quantity. `AccountBook` drops the order from `resting_order_count`
when the release exhausts what it held, which is what makes the FR-5.5.5 freeze
predicate ("no resting orders") evaluable from the ledger. `Exchange` reserves
once at acceptance and then releases per fill plus the residual; an over-release,
or a release against an unknown order id, raises `InvariantViolationError`.

**R27. `check_trade_invariant` also asserts the fee is the per fill fee.** I5 as
written ("and the fee lands in `FEES`") is not decidable from the three deltas
alone, so the check additionally asserts
`fee_cents == taker_fee_cents(config.taker_fee_bps, trade.price, trade.qty) ==
trade.taker_fee_cents` and that the vault holds at least that fee. This is
section 6.1's "one owner" rule with teeth: an exchange charging an aggregate
across price levels fires an I5 breach instead of silently creating a cent.

**R28. `AccountState.positions` omits a market on which the account is flat with
a null cost basis, even when it paid a taker fee there.** A closed round trip with
`taker_fee_bps > 0` leaves `fees_paid_cents > 0` with `qty == 0` and
`cost_basis_cents == 0`, so that market does not appear in `AccountState.positions`
nor in `PositionSnapshot.positions[]`, while `AccountBook.fees_paid_cents` still
reports it. Consequence, stated so no downstream metric gets it wrong: fees paid
are summed from `TradeExecuted.taker_fee_cents`, never from `PositionSnapshot`.

**R29. `AccountBook` exposes no accessor for the market ids it holds.** A consumer
needing them reads them from the scenario, which is their owner. `account_ids()`
having no market-side twin is deliberate, not an omission.

#### Section 7.10, `pxe.mm` (A07)

**R30. FR-5.8.4's widening window is `w + 1` ticks and the contract formula is
authoritative.** `widen_until_tick = tick + w` with the window test
`tick <= widen_until_tick` widens the news tick plus the following `w`. The PRD's
"le spread est double pendant w ticks" and `MMQuoted.widen_until_tick`'s "last
tick of the widening window" (inclusive) are off by one against each other; the
formula in 7.10 wins, since it is the one a journal records.

**R31. A `HIGH` impact `NewsItem` with an empty `market_ids` widens nothing.**
7.10 says the widening is "for its markets", and an item about nothing in
particular names none. Global widening on a market-less item would have every
noise headline double the spread on every market at once.

**R32. `MMConfig.enabled = False` means `requote` returns `()` and emits no
`MMQuoted` and no `cancel_all`.** Any pre-existing market maker order stays
resting: disabling the quoter is not a cancel-everything instruction. Nothing
sets this mid match today, which is why the distinction had to be written down
before something does.

**R33. The `mm.tiebreak` substream is the market maker's only randomness and it
breaks the suppressed-side tie.** Section 7.10 specifies no randomness at all,
which left `ReferenceMarketMaker.__init__` contractually requiring an `RngTree` a
faithful implementation never draws from. The one draw is the "clamping would
make `bid >= ask`" branch, which side to drop; with `clamp_price` bounded to 1..99
and `half_spread_cents() >= 1` that branch is unreachable for any anchor, so the
draw never happens in a shipped match and no golden hash depends on it. The
substream stays registered and named so the parameter has a purpose.

**R34. The T2.6 liquidity study is a journal-only reducer, and these five names
are part of 7.10:** `LiquidityCostRow`, `LiquidityStudy`, `liquidity_cost_row`,
`build_liquidity_study` and `render_liquidity_study`. Section 13 forbids A07 from
importing `pxe.exchange.accounts` and `Exchange.__init__` requires an
`AccountBook`, so `pxe.mm` cannot run a match and `pxe mm study` had no function
to call. Reducing finished journals instead satisfies decision 16's
"recomputable from the journal alone" and section 2.7's filesystem ban:
`render_liquidity_study` returns a string and the caller writes the file.

#### Section 7.11, `pxe.oracle` (A08)

**R35. `ResolutionReport.payout_cents` is `0` for a cancellation.** Only
`outcome` was documented as `None` there. A cancellation pays nothing; it
restores cost basis, which the `settlements` field carries.

**R36. A second `resolve` or `cancel_market` on the same market raises
`InvalidConfigError`, and so does a `resolve` on the market's own resolution
tick.** Neither is idempotent: a second call would emit a second `MarketResolved`
and settle a second time. The wording matches `Exchange.close_market`'s "market
is already closed". There is no oracle-specific error class and none is needed:
this is a caller bug, not a corrupted ledger, so `InvariantViolationError` (which
aborts the match) would be too strong.

**R37. An `Oracle` built over a replayed world returns `()` from
`due_cancellations`.** Section 7.12 rebuilds the replayed scenario with
`cancellations=()`, which is correct for replay (the `MarketCancelled` events in
the journal are the record) but means the oracle cannot re-drive a replayed
match's cancellations. `replay_journal` reads the events; it never re-runs the
oracle.

#### Section 7.12, `pxe.runner` (A09)

**R38. `run_match`'s `out_dir` **is** this match's artefact directory.** It is the
`runs/<match_id>/` of section 4.6, and `run_match` writes `journal.jsonl`,
`observations.jsonl` and `meta.json` directly into it. It is **not** the runs
root: the `runs_dir` to `runs_dir/<match_id>` step belongs to
`pxe.store.files.match_dir` (section 7.20), the one builder of that layout. A
caller holding a runs root passes `match_dir(runs_dir, match_id)` with the same
`match_id`. Without this sentence two callers silently overwrite one journal.

**R39. `MatchRunner.__init__` takes an eighth, keyword-only argument with a
default: `obs_path: Path | None = None`.** This is the caller of
`write_observation` that section 7.13 promised and section 4.6 requires; without
it `runs/<match_id>/observations.jsonl` had an owner in table 1 and no producer
anywhere, so the artefact was never written. It defaults to `None` (write
nothing) because the file is explicitly outside AC-P1: it is not hashed and not
required to replay, so a unit test that only wants a journal omits it and no
golden hash moves. `run_match` passes `out_dir / "observations.jsonl"`. This is
an extension under rule 2 of this document, not a widened signature.

**R40. P4 step 14's first branch only fires with a non zero floor.** With integer
prices, free cash never reaches exactly 0 through ordinary buying, because the
pre-trade check refuses the last indivisible order. So at the default
`bankruptcy_free_cash_floor_cents = 0` the free-cash branch is unreachable and
the step's "the one that actually fires" is true only above it. The predicate is
unchanged; `test_match_runner.py::test_bankruptcy_freezes_agent` sets a floor of
3 000 against 20 000 initial cash to reach it.

#### Section 7.13, `pxe.runner.observation_builder` (A10)

**R41. `validate_observation` has no call site inside a match.** It is a test and
tooling entry point. Section 8.1's remark that "rejecting that observation would
abort a legal match inside `validate_observation`" describes the hazard, not a
call: `build_observation` deliberately does not call it, because a per-agent
jsonschema pass inside P1 is a cost the engine does not need to pay on every tick
of every match.

**R42. A message is delivered when `deliver_tick <= tick` and its author is not
the recipient.** FR-5.6.1 delivers to the **other** agents, so the recipient's own
message is dropped from its own observation. The bound is inclusive, matching
what the runner hands over.

**R43. `build_observation` filters signals defensively, twice over.** It drops a
signal addressed to another agent and a signal whose `market_id` is not tradable
this tick. The runner already applies the second filter at P1 step 3, so this is
a belt-and-braces rule that guarantees the payload can never carry a dangling
`market_id` whatever the caller hands over.

**R44. `news` keeps the first 20 items in publication order.** That is the
schema's `maxItems`. Combined with R18 (the info engine self caps at 12) the cap
is unreachable in a shipped match, which is the point.

**R45. `obs_bytes` is `len(stable_json(observation_to_json(obs)).encode("utf-8"))`
and `estimate_tokens` counts characters, not bytes.** `obs_bytes` is the size of
the stored `payload`, so `observations.jsonl` can be budgeted from its own
contents.

**R46. Fidelity wins over the token budget.** T2.4's 2000 tokens is a **median
tick target**; the section 7.13 fidelity rule (100 % of the agent's resting
orders and non flat positions) is a **hard rule**. With
`max_active_orders_per_market = 20` over 5 markets a saturated book renders at
roughly 2 650 estimated tokens and that is correct behaviour, pinned by
`test_observation_builder.py::test_fidelity_wins_over_the_budget_when_the_book_is_saturated`.
An agent that cannot see its own orders cannot manage them, which is a worse
failure than a long prompt.

**R47. `MarketObservation.status` carries no information and that is fine.** Only
open markets appear in an observation and the schema pins `{"const": "open"}`. The
field is kept for shape stability with the rest of the market carriers.

#### Section 7.14, `pxe.runner.action_validator` (A11)

**R48. The first row of 7.14's table is scoped to the envelope, not to the whole
payload.** As written, "fails `schemas/action.v1.json` outright ->
`SCHEMA_INVALID` -> nothing survives" makes nine later rows of the same table
unreachable, because the schema already pins `price` 1..99, `qty` 1..100000, the
`side`/`type`/`op` enums, `orders.maxItems` 20, `predictions.maxItems` 8 and
`message_public.maxLength` 280. The resolution, which restores all nine rows:

* a schema error **inside** an `orders` or `predictions` **item**, plus
  `maxItems` on those two arrays and `maxLength` on `message_public` and
  `rationale`, are handled **semantically**: the offending item is dropped or
  truncated with its own specific reason, and the rest of the action survives;
* every other envelope failure is **fatal** and yields `SCHEMA_INVALID`: the
  payload is not an object, a top-level key is missing, a top-level key is
  unknown, or `predictions` is not an array.

**R49. A fatal rejection carries `source = FALLBACK`.** The row says the result is
`AgentAction.no_action`, whose default source is `FALLBACK`, and it **is** the
FR-5.1.1 fallback: no action of the agent's survived. Metrics therefore see a
fallback, which is what they should count.

**R50. A missing `action_version` is `SCHEMA_INVALID`; a present but unknown one
is `ACTION_INVALID`.** `const: "1.0"` makes both a schema failure in principle,
but the two are different situations: an absent key is a malformed envelope, a
wrong value is a version the validator understands the shape of and refuses.

**R51. A `predictions` entry that is not an object, or one missing `market_id`,
is `UNKNOWN_MARKET`; one missing or malformed `p_yes` is
`INVALID_PROBABILITY`.** The table is declared exhaustive over producible
reasons, so these two cases needed a home rather than a new member.

**R52. `rationale` is sanitised and truncated to the schema's own `maxLength`,
and no rejection is emitted.** It is journalled verbatim in
`AgentActionReceived`, so it must be sanitised; it carries no semantics, so
truncating it is not a refusal of anything. The limit is read from
`action.v1.json`, never duplicated as a literal.

**R53. `Rejection.detail` is capped at 200 characters, enforced in
`__post_init__`.** The cap is stated in `pxe/events.py` for the event; it belongs
to the carrier that feeds it too, otherwise the truncation happens at emission
time and the reason text a test asserts on differs from the one journalled.

**R54. Truncation emits one rejection per dropped entry.** For
`TOO_MANY_ORDERS_IN_ACTION` and `TOO_MANY_PREDICTIONS` the event is "emitted per
rejected item" and carries an `item_index`, so a single summary rejection would
have no index to carry.

**R55. `sanitise_message` escapes to HTML entities: `` ` `` to `&#96;`, `<` to
`&lt;`, `>` to `&gt;`.** Truncation is applied **last**, so `max_chars` holds
after the expansion, and a trailing half-entity is stripped rather than left
dangling.

#### Section 7.15, `pxe.agents` (A12)

**R56. `make_baseline` raises `InvalidConfigError` on an unknown name.**
`UnknownProfileError` was the other candidate and is reserved for information
profiles; a baseline name is a configuration value.

**R57. `BaselineAgent` is internal, and A18's cheaters reach the baselines
through `make_baseline`.** Section 7.15's "nothing outside A12 imports a baseline
module by name" stands. A cheater that needs to wrap a baseline composes the
`ScriptedAgent` that `make_baseline` returns rather than subclassing a private
class.

**R58. A baseline's policy constants are journal-affecting, and changing one is
the fifth kind of change that legitimately moves a golden hash.** See section 10,
where the list now says so. Section 7.15 gives no per-baseline behavioural spec
beyond six adjectives, so every edge threshold, size, noise amplitude and
position cap is an A12 invention that is nonetheless inside the journal of every
scripted match, exactly like a `MatchConfig` default.

#### Section 7.16, `pxe.gateway` (A13)

**R59. `AgentGateway` has a fourth method,
`reset_agents(*, config: MatchConfig, rng: RngTree) -> None`.** Section 3.1
obliges the runner to reset every scripted seat between `MatchStarted` and tick
1, and 7.16 exposed neither the agents nor a reset hook, which left the runner
discovering agents by reflecting on private attributes. Reflection cannot see an
agent a gateway stores under a name it does not guess (`ClaudeCliGateway` keeps
`harnesses`), and a silently skipped reset kills O1 without failing a single
test. So the hook is now on the protocol: `BaseGateway` implements it as a no-op
(a provider backed gateway has no scripted state), `ScriptedGateway` resets each
seat with
`rng.child(f"agent/{agent_id}").substream(f"agent.{agent_id}")`, and
`CompositeGateway` delegates in table order. The gateway builds the per seat
generator because only it knows which seats it holds.

**R60. `BudgetTracker.record` and `check_tokens` take two optional keyword
extensions.** `record(reply, *, tick: int | None = None)` and
`check_tokens(..., agent_id: str | None = None, tick: int | None = None)`, plus a
`note_tick(tick)` that `BaseGateway.acollect_actions` calls once per tick.
`tick_tokens(agent_id, tick)` is otherwise unimplementable: `record` is handed
only a reply and `AgentReply` has no `tick` field, so the per tick map has no
source of a tick. A14 keeps calling `record(reply)` literally and still gets
correct attribution.

**R61. `max_tokens_per_match` is per agent when `check_tokens` is given an
`agent_id`, and whole-match otherwise.** `types.GatewayConfig` calls it "per
match, per agent" while 7.16 calls it the guard that "bounds a runaway match",
and the contracted signature carries no `agent_id`, so the per-agent reading was
unimplementable as written. Both readings are now reachable and the caller picks.

**R62. The concrete adapter records cost, and the bridge only checks it.** A14
holds the budget and the real provider cost, so A14 calls `record`.
`BaseGateway` calls `check` before dispatch and never records: counting in both
places double charges every call.

**R63. `BaseGateway.__init__(*, gateway_config=None, budget=None)`.** 7.16
contracts no `__init__` for it, yet it needs a `GatewayConfig`
(`collect_actions` receives only a `MatchConfig`, and `ScriptedGateway(*, agents)`
takes no gateway config). Frozen class-level defaults keep a subclass that never
calls `super().__init__` working.

**R64. `AgentReply`'s six telemetry fields have defaults; the five contractual
ones do not.** `cost_usd=0.0`, `latency_ms=0`, `input_tokens=0`,
`output_tokens=0`, `cached_input_tokens=0`, `attempts=1`, so a scripted or
fallback reply is constructible. No positional order changed.

**R65. A non positive USD cap forbids spending, and `remaining_usd` clamps at
0.0.** `GatewayConfig` documents `max_tokens_per_match = 0` as unlimited but
gives no "unlimited" spelling for USD, so `0.0` cannot mean both. A tournament
that wants no USD cap sets a large number.

**R66. Cached input tokens are excluded from the token budget.** 7.16's own cost
model puts about 35 000 cached tokens of unavoidable CLI overhead on every call
against a `max_input_tokens_per_call` of 8 192, so counting them would reject
every call ever made.

**R67. `CompositeGateway.acall_agent` reuses the config of the last
`acollect_actions` call**, and `MatchConfig()` if there has been none.
`acall_agent` has no `config` argument (7.16) but a sub-gateway's
`acollect_actions` requires one, so the group entry point remembers what it was
handed for the single call entry point to reuse.

### 12.3 Wave-3 integration rulings (A14..A23)

The nine work packages of wave 3 were built in parallel against sections 7.16 to
7.24, and each reported where those sections were silent, ambiguous or
self contradictory. As in 12.2, the **contradictions** were fixed in the section
that owned them (3.1, 4.5, 4.6, 7.16 to 7.22, 7.24 and 9 each carry a paragraph
saying what changed and why), and what follows resolves what was merely
**unspecified**. A ruling written into a section above is cited here by number
and not repeated.

Five of these are code defects and not documentation gaps, and they are marked
**[defect]**: they were shipped behaviour that could not work.

#### Section 7.16, `pxe.gateway.claude_cli` and `.prompt` (A14)

**R68. `llm_trace.jsonl` has a writer.** Section 4.6 gives A14 the file and
section 7.16 contracted no function for it, so the artefact had an owner and no
API. `LlmTraceEntry` plus `write_llm_trace(path, entry)` are in 7.16 now. Cost,
latency and token counts are exactly what section 3.5 forbids in a journal, so
this file is where they live and it is outside the hash by construction.

**R69. A14 reads `observation_builder` and `schema_registry`.** Written into
7.16 and into A14's "May read" column in section 13. Both have exactly one
owner, and re-implementing either inside the gateway would create a second
observation renderer or a second schema loader.

**R71b. `ClaudeCliGateway.acall_agent` reuses the config of the last
`acollect_actions` call**, and `MatchConfig()` if there has been none. This is
ruling R67 word for word, which was written for `CompositeGateway` only;
`build_system_prompt(harness, *, config)` needs a config and `acall_agent` has
none, so the identical rule applies to the CLI gateway.

**R72b. One `timeout_s`, split across attempts.** 7.16 says `timeout_s` bounds
each `acall_agent` **and** that a failed call is retried `retries` times. Both
cannot be literal: with a per attempt `timeout_s` the outer bound cuts attempt
two and reports `AGENT_TIMEOUT, attempts=1`, which is a lie about what happened.
The rule is `timeout_s / (retries + 1)` per attempt, so the tick budget
FR-6.2.3 asks for is respected and every attempt gets a fair share of it.

**R72c. `max_tokens_per_match` binds the *next* call.** 7.16 says it is
"checked after" the call and gives that check no effect. Discarding an already
paid, valid answer helps nobody, so the breach is recorded and logged and the
cap binds through the pre-call check of the following call.

**R72d. FR-5.1.1's "incident logged" is not a journal event in the gateway.**
The gateway holds no `Journal` and section 4.5 gives `incident_raised` to the
module that holds one. A failed call produces a warning log plus an
`llm_trace.jsonl` line; the journal-visible event is the runner's
`AgentTimedOut`, which is what FR-5.1.1's named test asserts.

**R72e. "At least two providers" (T2.3) means two `(executable, model)`
backends**, plus a `CompositeGateway` that can mix CLI and scripted seats in one
match. It does not mean a second vendor SDK: "every LLM call goes through the
Claude Code CLI" stands.

**R72f. `estimate_tokens` stays "never used by the engine"** and the FR-6.2.3
pre-call input estimate is a private prompt-text estimate inside
`claude_cli.py`, reusing the one `TOKEN_CHARS_PER_TOKEN` ratio rather than
introducing a second divisor.

#### Section 7.17, `pxe.metrics` (A15, A16, A17)

**R70. PnL is derived from executions and outcomes, not read off a series.**
See section 9.

**R71. `stdev` is the sample standard deviation, denominator `n - 1`.** See
section 9.

**R72. `MatchProjection.resolution_ticks` is the *scheduled* tick of *every*
market**, taken from `MatchStarted.markets`, and not one entry per
`MarketResolved`. A tuple restricted to resolved markets would silently drop a
cancelled market, and A16's `test_n_terms_equals_resolution_tick` sums over it.

**R73. `Highlight.magnitude_cents` per kind.** See section 7.17.

**R74. The `big_trade` threshold uses the lower median.** See section 7.17.

**R75. `metrics.json` does not carry the reliability curve; the metrics route
calls `reliability_curve` itself.** See sections 7.17 and 7.21.

**R76. `metrics.json` carries a `"format": "pxe.metrics/1"` marker** so
`read_metrics` refuses a foreign file instead of half-parsing it.

**R77, R78.** The six behavioral descriptors, their "no data" values, their
window and the two that are rebuilt from orders and trades: see section 9.

**R79 to R82.** Reliability bins, the empty Brier, the unresolved market and the
scope of `n_carried`: see section 9.

**A15's build-time dependency on A16 and A17 is a sequencing fact, not a bug.**
`aggregate.py` imports `calibration` and `behavioral`, ruff's `PLC0415` forbids
the lazy-import escape in `src/`, and `mypy --strict` is part of the gate: A15
therefore could not be green before A16 and A17 landed. Nothing in A15 had to
change when they did. If a future wave splits the same way again, dispatch
`aggregate.py` to the later package rather than expecting a red gate to be
ignored for a day.

#### Section 7.18, `pxe.integrity` (A18)

**R83. `incidents.jsonl` is replaced, not appended.** See section 4.5.

**R84. There is no wash-specific threshold.** See section 7.18.

**R85 to R87.** The dispersion scale, the severity grading and the units of
`score_ppm`: see section 7.18.

**R88. `make_cheater` raises `InvalidConfigError` on a missing or self
accomplice**, consistent with R56.

**R89. The five detector classes and `DETECTOR_VERSION` are public names.** A
`Protocol` needs named implementations and `run_detectors(detectors=...)` is
otherwise uncallable.

**R89b. AC-P6's unit of observation is one seat pair in one match.** Precision
and recall had no stated denominator. A true positive is a colluding pair the
detectors flagged; a false positive is any pair flagged in an honest match. A
per-match or per-agent unit gives different numbers, so the unit is fixed here
and the figures in section 11.3 are measured on it.

#### Section 7.19, `pxe.tournament` (A19, A20)

**R90 to R94 and R97 to R104.** Swiss round derivation, `exhibition_pairings`,
the two collapsed relaxations, the orchestrator's `heldout` argument,
`BalanceReport.max_deviation`, the archive accessors and default grid, the bank
format and purposes, the two Halls of Fame and the zero draw prior: all in
section 7.19.

**R93. `TournamentConfig.__post_init__` exists, with two of its three
checks. [defect]** It did not exist at all, so a `TournamentConfig` built
anywhere other than through the orchestrator was entirely unvalidated. The third
check the contract put there is impossible: `harnesses` holds the competitors
only while the seats are filled by the competitors plus `background_baselines`,
and `configs/exhibition.toml` ships one challenger against a field of four. See
section 7.19 rule 5.

**R95. Seeds are 63 bit. [defect]** See section 3.1. This was a live crash and
not a documentation gap: `HeldoutBank.reserve` drew over the full unsigned 64
bit space and roughly half of every sealed set could not be inserted into a
`BigInteger` column, so an AC-P5 held-out run died on its first `save_task`.

**R98. `elite_cell` is written by a real tournament. [defect]** Section 7.20's
writer table always said "orchestrator at end of tournament", and the
orchestrator never built an `EliteArchive` at all: the only elite cells that had
ever existed were the ones `test_api.py` inserted by hand, so T5.2's grid
reached the UI in a fixture and nowhere else. `TournamentOrchestrator._execute`
now builds the `DEFAULT_ELITE_AXES` grid, offers every seat of every folded
match with the style vector read back from `metric_record`, and saves the
archive beside the ratings.
`test_tournament.py::test_a_real_tournament_fills_the_map_elites_archive` is the
gate.

#### Section 7.20, `pxe.store` (A21)

**R105 to R108 and R110.** The two infrastructure tables, the absence of a
timestamp column, the row builders `save_match` shares, the key of
`load_costs_usd` and the `resolution.mode` vocabulary: all in section 7.20.

**R109. `Incident` carries a `match_id`. [defect]** With more than one match in
a tournament, `GET /api/tournaments/{id}` served `match_id: ""` on every
integrity alert, because `Store.load_incidents(tournament_id=...)` returned
carriers that could not hold the value section 7.21's payload requires.
`pxe.types.Incident` gained a defaulted `match_id` field (last, so no producer
changes), the store stamps it from the row, and
`test_store.py::test_incidents_are_replaced_and_filtered` now spreads two
matches across one tournament and asserts each alert names its own.

**`MatchTask` still carries no `tournament_id`,** and the store reads it off the
task id with the convention `<tournament_id>#<suffix>`, which A19 produces as
`<tournament_id>#r<rr>-g<ggg>-<template_id>-<seed>` and
`TournamentOrchestrator.__init__` enforces by refusing a `#` in a tournament id
and refusing an id over 24 characters. That convention is now part of section
2.2. Adding the field to `MatchTask` is the alternative and was not taken in
this wave: it is an anchor change with three consumers and no behaviour
difference.

#### Section 7.22 and 7.24, `pxe.cli` and the report (A23)

**R111 to R118.** The held-out flags, the argparse exit code, the schema name
spellings, the privately rebuilt `TournamentResult`, the definition of `deltas`,
the held-out leaderboard, the orchestrator's static import of `report`, and
`heldout_gain_mu_milli`: all in sections 7.22 and 7.24.

**R96. `docs/MM_LIQUIDITY_COST.md` is produced by `pxe mm study`. [defect]**
Section 1 and the document's own first line said so and it was false: the
committed bytes came from a private test harness whose synthetic `informed_flow`
publishes no news, so FR-5.8.4 never fired and the FR-5.8.6 column read
`0.000 c`. Run through the shipped command over real baseline matches the same
column reads `1.351 c` on `standard` and `3.146 c` on `illiquid`, that is a
**failing** acceptance criterion that had been published as a passing one. The
resolution is in section 9 (two spreads, and FR-5.8.6 is measured on the base
one) and the document is now byte-pinned to the command by
`test_market_maker.py::test_mm_liquidity_cost_doc_is_what_the_shipped_command_prints`.

#### Still open, deliberately

Two things wave 3 reported are **not** fixed here, and saying so is the point of
this subsection:

1. **`make test` and `make cov` do not deselect the `llm` marker.** Only the CI
   job passes `-m "not llm ..."`, so `make gate` would spend about 1.30 USD if
   the paid tests did not also self-skip unless `PXE_LLM_E2E=1` **and** the CLI
   is on `PATH`. The belt is in place and the braces are not: the proper fix is
   `-m "not llm"` in the `test` and `cov` recipes, and `Makefile` is A01's file.
2. **`heldout_gain_mu_milli` is `0`** until A25 lands, in the report and in the
   API alike, which is at least one number in two places rather than two. See
   ruling R118.


---

## 13. Implementation work packages

This is the dispatch table for the implementation wave. One work package, one
agent, one module. **Two work packages never own the same file**, and every
file of the tree in section 1 appears exactly once in the "Files owned" column
below, which is what makes the wave parallelisable: no two agents ever need to
edit the same line, so nothing has to be merged by hand.

Three rules apply to every package and are not repeated per row:

1. **Read-only for everyone, always:** `docs/CONTRACTS.md` (this file),
   `PRD_Prediction_Exchange v2.md`, `docs/reviews/*.md`, `schemas/*.json`,
   `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `README.md`,
   `.gitignore`, `src/pxe/{__init__,types,events,rng,errors}.py`,
   `tests/{__init__,conftest}.py`. Those are A01's and A01 is done. A package
   that believes it needs a change in one of them **stops and says so**: it is
   a contract change, not a code change (rule 1 of this document).
2. **The dependency arrow of section 7 is the read permission.** A package may
   import from any module to its left on that arrow and from nothing to its
   right. The "May read" column names only the non-obvious extras, that is the
   modules it actually calls; the anchor modules of rule 1 are always readable
   and are not repeated.
3. **A package may add a test file only if that file is in its own row.** The
   fixture names of section 10 are reserved and are not redefined. Every
   acceptance test file listed below must exist and pass, `make gate` must be
   green, and section 10's anti-vacuous rule applies with teeth: a test that
   would pass on an empty journal does not count as done.

Packages whose module is a `Protocol` other packages implement (A12's
`ScriptedAgent`, A13's `AgentGateway`) land the protocol first, because five
other packages type against it.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| A01 | anchor: shared types, events, RNG, errors, schemas, packaging, this document | `pyproject.toml`, `Makefile`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `README.md`, `.gitignore`, `docs/CONTRACTS.md`, `schemas/observation.v1.json`, `schemas/action.v1.json`, `src/pxe/__init__.py`, `src/pxe/py.typed`, `src/pxe/types.py`, `src/pxe/events.py`, `src/pxe/rng.py`, `src/pxe/errors.py`, `tests/__init__.py`, `tests/conftest.py` | stdlib only | `tests/test_types.py`, `tests/test_types_boundaries.py`, `tests/test_events.py`, `tests/test_rng.py`, `tests/test_errors.py`, `tests/test_schemas.py`, `tests/test_style_rules.py` **(landed and green)** |

not extra files: A24 owns every file under `web/`, and no other package owns
anything there.

Three entries of the section 1 tree are deliberately absent from this table:
`runs/` is generated and git ignored, and the two input artefacts
(`PRD_Prediction_Exchange v2.md` and `docs/reviews/*.md`) are owned by nobody
because nobody may edit them. Everything else in the tree appears in exactly
one row above.

### 13.1 What "done" means for a work package

A package is done when all five of these hold, and not before:

1. Every public name of its section 7 subsection exists with the **literal**
   signature written there, argument names included.
2. Its acceptance test file or files exist and pass, and each one asserts on a
   non empty journal or projection first (section 10's anti-vacuous rule).
3. `make gate` is green: `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `mypy --strict`
   over `src/pxe`, and the whole suite under coverage. `mypy` runs with
   `no_implicit_reexport`, so a public name missing from `__all__` is a type
   error at the import site rather than a runtime surprise.
4. It owns no file outside its row and has edited no file outside its row.
   `git diff --name-only` against the wave's base commit is the check.
5. Every traceability row of section 11 that names one of its modules names a
   test that now exists. A row whose test is still missing is not "in
   progress", it is a red gate.
